# CXC port: known defects

Behavior of CXC v0.2.40 that looks unintended and that the recorded corpus ([contract/schema/cxc](../../contract/schema/cxc/README.md); fixtures in `contract/fixtures/cxc`) keeps exactly as the oracle printed it. The port reproduces behavior first: parity with the oracle wins over repair, and a replay never takes a defect for the right answer.

- A port issue that finds a defect adds one line here and does not fix it. A defect the port reproduces on purpose keeps its line with `port: kept`. No line is deleted; a later change that repairs one edits its line to `port: fixed` and names that change.
- Exceptions, where the port fixes a defect instead of keeping it: `reset --help`, which deletes session state instead of printing help (decision 9 of the port's Wave 0 decisions, recorded in Linear); and, under the parity rule revision of 2026-10-03, a defect that a review finding of kind security names, and a defect that loses or truncates settings, state or record files. A change that fixes one tags its recorded cases intentionally-changed in the replay and marks its line `port: fixed`; a line of that class stays `port: kept` until such a change lands.
- Line shape: `- <what the oracle does> (<pointer>); port: <pending|kept|fixed>.` A fixture pointer is an id under `contract/fixtures/cxc/`; a source pointer is a path in the upstream repository at tag v0.2.40, with a line where one line carries the behavior.
- The defects of the earlier Python-to-Go port of the bridge and relay are in [docs/port/known-defects.md](../port/known-defects.md).

## Seeded from the corpus recording

- `cxc reset --help` is not help: the scope parser defaults to state, so it deletes session state exactly like `reset --state` and exits 0 (fixture `cli__reset__help_is_not_help_and_resets_state`); port: fixed, the one exception.
- `cxc doctor --help` is not help: the hooks option parser rejects it with `cxc-ops error: unknown hooks option: --help` and exit 1 (fixture `cli__doctor__help_is_an_unknown_option`); port: kept (the CRW-618 section carries it as `crw-ops error: unknown hooks option: --help`, the R29-renamed text).
- `cxc provider` ignores every argument, even `--help`, and runs the same probe (fixture `cli__provider__detect_line_and_ignored_args`); port: pending.
- `cxc metric parse-line` does not read stdin as its help says: v0.2.40 joins the argv after the verb (the `--session` token included), so the stdin form and the ordinary argv form exit 1 with `null`, and only a form whose session value itself completes the METRIC line, such as `parse-line METRIC --session =1`, succeeds (fixture `cli__metric__record_show_kind_parse_line`; source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/metric-cli.ts:145`); port: kept.
- `cxc plan init` ignores `--date` whatever its value: the parser reads only `--phases` and `--cwd`, skips every other `--` token and takes the first bare argument as the slug, so the unit date is a YYMMDD prefix of that argument or else the day's date, although the verb's help says `--date` is for callers that carry their own prefix (fixture `cli__plan__init_normalises_slug_and_date`, whose `--date 2026-01-01` coincides with the recorded clock's day; source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/plan-cli.ts:92`, `:95` and `:177`); port: pending.
- `cxc loop init` with a goalplans directory that is a symlink fails with a thrown error that only the CLI's generic handler prints (`codexclaw cli failed: goalplan state path must not be a symlink: ...`, exit 1) instead of a command-level refusal (fixture `cli__loop__symlinked_goalplans_root_refused`; source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/goalplan.ts:311`, `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/cli.ts:531`); port: pending.
- The managed-worktree deletion guard exits 1 with empty stdout, and no deny envelope, when its stdin is oversized: its verb `worktree-guard-pretool` does not start with `pre-tool-use`, so the generic oversize deny does not apply (fixture `hook__pre-tool-use-guarding-managed-worktree-deletion__oversized_stdin_exits_1`); port: pending.
- The same guard's fallback matches the slot id as a raw substring, so `rm -rf` on an unrelated path that merely contains the slot id is denied as an unresolvable target while the same command on a neighbouring path passes (fixture `hook__pre-tool-use-guarding-managed-worktree-deletion__slot_substring_denies_unrelated_target`); port: pending.
- Write and Edit calls reach the edit lint (its matcher is `^(apply_patch|Write|Edit)$`) but are never linted, because it reads only `tool_input.command` (fixture `hook__pre-tool-use-linting-apply-patch__allowed_edits`; matcher in `contract/schema/cxc/hook-declarations.json`); port: pending.
- A subagent's patch skips the edit lint: the edit leg sits below the root-only subagent exit (fixture `hook__pre-tool-use-linting-apply-patch__subagent_payload_skips_lint`); port: pending.
- `cxc memory` and `cxc memory grant` reach recall's chat and memory usage text with exit 0 instead of the allow-write handler; only `cxc memory allow-write` reaches the pabcd-state command (fixture `cli__memory-allow-write__non_allow_write_routes_to_recall`); port: pending.
- The MCP server's default project scope is its working directory, and the plugin's MCP declaration sets that to the plugin root (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/subagent-config/src/mcp.ts:129`, `plugins/codexclaw/.mcp.json`; the recorded MCP fixtures use the global scope); port: pending.
- Recall's recovery line is cut inside the plugin path when the expanded path is long (fixture `hook__session-start-injecting-recall-context__compact_source_gets_recovery_directive`, listed as path-length dependent in `contract/schema/cxc/coverage.json`); port: pending.
- The spawn hook runs the same `git ls-files --error-unmatch` three times for a direct spawn (fixture `hook__pre-tool-use-attaching-skills__baseline_empty_workspace` records three identical git calls, and fixture `hook__pre-tool-use-attaching-skills__managed_fallback_candidate_null_effort` one for a managed dispatch; source `plugins/codexclaw/components/subagent-config/src/store.ts:322`); port: pending.
- The spawn hook never sweeps unused grants: a grant file carries a 15-minute expiry, but only a grant that is claimed is removed (fixture `hook__pre-tool-use-attaching-skills__root_recursion_request_mints_coordinator_grant`; source `plugins/codexclaw/components/subagent-config/src/spawn-attach-hook.ts:381` and `:405`); port: pending.
- `cxc disable` keeps the install manifest after reverting the flags and the managed key it recorded (fixture `cli__disable__reverts_live_flags_and_key`, whose resulting tree still holds the manifest); port: pending.

## Found by the interview tracker port

- `reconstructInterview` turns a persisted partial interview into a ready-shaped one: a dimension score missing its arrays or confidence, a confidence outside [0, 1] and absent contradictions or assumptions arrays are repaired to empty arrays and 0, so a tracker with every level `max` and one recorded scan passes `isInterviewReady` after reconstruct although the same object given directly does not (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/interview.ts:140`, `:158-160`, `:193` and `:213` against `:271` and `:274-281`); port: kept.
- The 50-entry cap drops the oldest entries, so an old unrecorded assumption or malformed-assumption sentinel is evicted by 50 later recorded assumptions and then stops blocking readiness, in `reconstructInterview` and `normalizeInterview` alike (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/interview.ts:212` and `:368`); port: kept.
- `MAX_AUTO_ROUNDS` is defined and `autoResolveCount` is carried through reconstruct and normalize, but no runtime decision enforces the cap or uses the count to limit auto-resolution (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/interview.ts:33`, `:64-65`, `:220` and `:369`); port: kept.
- `normalizeInterview` caps contradictions and assumptions without re-validating their entries, unlike `reconstructInterview`, so an entry with an invalid severity written in memory is persisted as is and corrected only on the next read (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/interview.ts:367-368` against `:185-213`); port: kept.
- A persisted assumption object with `recorded: true` whose `id` or `text` is not a string is reconstructed with an empty `id` or `text` and still counts as recorded, so readiness accepts an assumption whose content was lost (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/interview.ts:202-204` and `:277`); port: kept.
- When only a low- or medium-severity contradiction keeps an otherwise complete interview from being ready, the gate reports `interview is not ready (dimensions/assumptions incomplete)`, which names the wrong cause: that fallback is emitted only when no other warning exists, and only high-severity contradictions are counted (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/interview.ts:315`, `:319` and `:339`); port: kept.

## Found by the common primitives port

- `writeInterviewPolicy` truncates and rewrites the settings file (`codexclaw.json`) in place with `writeFileSync` instead of publishing by rename, so a crash or a concurrent hook read can see a truncated file, which `readInterviewPolicy` answers with the default `new-unit` and `readPabcdEnabled` with enabled, unless a recognized `CODEXCLAW_PABCD` value overrides the file (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/interview-policy.ts:105` and `:47-74`, against the rename helper at `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/atomic-write.ts:31`); port: fixed by CRW-427 (a settings data-loss defect, fixed by decision: `WritePolicy` publishes through a temp file in the same directory, fsync and rename with `Publish` in `internal/pabcd/crwdir/atomic.go`, so a reader sees the old file or the new one whole).
- A settings file that exists and is writable but cannot be read (for example mode 0200) is treated as malformed: `writeInterviewPolicy` reports `replacedMalformed` and rewrites the file with only `interview`, so every other setting in it is lost (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/interview-policy.ts:92-109`); port: fixed by CRW-427 (a settings data-loss defect, fixed by decision: `WritePolicy` in `internal/pabcd/projectcfg/policy.go` tells a file it could not read from one it read and found not to be a JSON object; the first is refused with a reason and left unchanged, only the second is replaced and reported as `ReplacedMalformed`).
- `ensureCodexclawDir` leaves an empty `.gitignore` when the exclusive create of the ignore file succeeds but the write fails before any bytes are written (a full disk): the `rmdirSync` of the now non-empty directory fails silently, the error is thrown, and the next call finds the directory and returns it without writing the rules, so runtime files can show up in Git (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/codexclaw-dir.ts:13-15` and `:19-23`); port: kept.
- The comment on `splitLines` says it tolerates a CR-only final line, but its pattern `/\r?\n/` needs a LF, so `splitLines("a\r")` returns `["a\r"]` with the CR kept (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/text-lines.ts:15-17`, which the `cxc-ops`, `recall`, `config-guard` and `messenger-bridge` components carry as byte-identical copies); port: kept.

## Found by the source identity port

- `captureSourceIdentity` decodes each NUL-terminated field of `git status --porcelain=v1 -z` as UTF-8 text, so a file name with invalid UTF-8 bytes turns into U+FFFD characters and the file is then read under that changed name: its content is not hashed (unless another file carries the replacement name) and two distinct names can collide (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/source-identity.ts:102` and `:160`; port scenarios `invalid_utf8_path` and `invalid_utf8_collide` in `internal/pabcd/source/testdata/scenarios.json`); port: kept.
- Git prints the original path as one more field after a worktree-side rename (status ` R`, which `git add -N` produces), but the oracle consumes that field only when the index column is `R` or `C`, so the original path is parsed as a record of its own: one of four UTF-16 units or more becomes a bogus entry (its first two units read as the status, the rest after one unit as the path) and a shorter one is dropped by the length check (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/source-identity.ts:109-113`; port scenarios `intent_to_add_rename` and `intent_to_add_rename_astral_split_in_xy` in `internal/pabcd/source/testdata/scenarios.json`); port: kept.
- Captured from a subdirectory of the repository, the capture reads every entry under that directory while git reports status paths relative to the repository root, so an entry has no content or takes the content of a different file with the same relative path under that directory: an edit can leave the identity unchanged and an unrelated change can move it (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/source-identity.ts:160`, `:196` and `:208`; port scenario `modified_file_captured_from_subdirectory` in `internal/pabcd/source/testdata/scenarios.json`); port: kept.

## Found by the corpus replayer

- Fixture `cli-help__top-level__no_arguments` stores its only step as `{}`: the recorder's JSON encoding drops an empty `cli` list, so the step no longer says it is a cli step (a defect of the recorder, not of the oracle). The replayer reads a step of no kind as a cli step without arguments (fixture `cli-help__top-level__no_arguments`); port: kept.

## Found by the session state port

- A D-close recovery marker whose successor is absent or malformed is restored with `legacy: true` so recovery refuses it, but the flag did not survive a write: the restored marker is persisted with `nextWorkPhaseId: null`, and the next read took that key as an explicit "no successor" and dropped the flag, so any later state write turned a refused marker into one that recovery may act on (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/state.ts:440-481`; recorded as the `again` answer of the five `marker_*` cases in `internal/pabcd/state/testdata/oracle-restore.json`); port: fixed by CRW-648 (a loss of state, fixed by decision under the parity rule revision of 2026-10-03: `reconstructDcloseRecovery` now reads the stored `legacy: true` and restores `Legacy` with no successor whatever `nextWorkPhaseId` holds, so a persisted marker keeps its distinction across every write; the five `again` answers and the stored `legacy: true` over a successor (`marker_legacy_input`) are tagged intentionally-changed in `internal/pabcd/state/oracle_test.go`, tests `TestDcloseLegacySurvivesAWriteAndRead` and `TestDcloseLegacyStoredShapes`).
- `reconstructUnverified`, which `readStateStrict` runs, cuts a tombstone's `receiptClaimed` with `String.slice(0, 256)`, which counts UTF-16 code units, so a claim with an astral character at that position is cut inside it and `JSON.stringify` writes the unpaired surrogate as a `\ud83d` escape (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/state.ts:82`); a Go string cannot hold a lone surrogate, so the port writes U+FFFD there (documented in the package comment of `internal/pabcd/state`); port: pending.
- `findForeignSessionCopies` compares `path.resolve`d state-file paths, which keep two symlink spellings of one tree apart, and then tests existence through the link, so a candidate that is a symlink to the current tree reports the session's own file as a foreign copy (recorded in a check against the oracle's `dist/state.js`; source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/state.ts:343-373`; pinned by `TestFindForeignSessionCopiesTakesASymlinkAliasForAnotherTree` in `internal/pabcd/state`); port: kept.

## Found by the host-state reads port

- `getGoalActiveStatus` answers `inactive` whenever `existsSync` is false, and `existsSync` is false on any access error, so a goals database inside a directory the process cannot traverse hides an active goal and the interview is not suppressed: it fails open where an unreadable database fails closed (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/goal-active.ts:64`); port: kept.
- `getGoalActiveStatus` resolves the database path outside its `try`, so a missing home directory (`os.homedir()` throws) escapes as an exception instead of the fail-closed `unreadable` (same file, line 63; read, not run); port: kept (`GoalsDBPath` returns the error and the caller decides).
- `readTranscriptTail` reads the whole transcript to keep its last 64 KiB (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/transcript.ts:43-46`); port: kept.
- `hasStageMarkerForPhase` finds the stage marker anywhere in the tail, so a user message that quotes `[codexclaw: PLAN]` counts as an injected directive and the passive directive is skipped (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/transcript.ts:57-64`, caller `hook.ts:809`); port: kept.
- `cxcOnPath` accepts any regular file named `cxc` on PATH, executable or not (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/cxc-ops/src/cxc-resolve.ts:55-62`, checked with a mode 0644 file); port: not carried, decision 2 removes the PATH rung.
- `cxcInvocation` puts the dispatcher path in double quotes without escaping, so an installation path holding a double quote, dollar sign, backtick or backslash breaks the injected command (same file, lines 83 and 86); port: kept for the runtime pointer path.
- With an empty `HOME`, `os.homedir()` is empty and the fallback directories resolve against the working directory (`.codex`, `.codexclaw`) (source `goal-active.ts:35`, `recall/src/index-db.ts:21`, `skill-search/src/cache.ts:15`, `subagent-config/src/store.ts:130`; checked with Node 24); port: kept.
- `CODEXCLAW_HOME` with surrounding whitespace names two directories: recall and skill-search use it as given, subagent-config trims it (`index-db.ts:21`, `cache.ts:15`, `store.ts:130`); port: kept (`CRWHome` returns the value as given, a consumer that trims does so itself).
- `resolveGoalsDbPath` joins the database directory with `path.join`, which removes a `..` lexically before the OS can follow a symlink, so a `CODEX_SQLITE_HOME` that ends in `<symlink>/..` names the lexical parent's `goals_1.sqlite` and not the database that path reaches; a goal in the latter is missed and the interview is not suppressed (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/goal-active.ts:36`; checked with Node 24, `path.join("/a/alias/..", "goals_1.sqlite")` is `/a/goals_1.sqlite`); port: kept (`GoalsDBPath` cleans the same way; the open itself keeps a `..` as written, as `node:sqlite` does).

## Found by the session state writes port

- `ensureState` falls back to an exclusive `writeFileSync` of the final path when the hard link fails with EPERM, ENOTSUP or EXDEV, and that call creates the file before it writes it, so a failed write (a full disk) leaves an empty or truncated state file at the final path; `ensureState` never repairs an existing file and every strict reader reports it unreadable (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/state.ts:402`; read from the source, a failing write is not exercised); port: fixed by CRW-445 (a state-loss defect, fixed by decision under the parity rule revision of 2026-10-03: after the failed link the default is written and fsynced to a second temp file beside the final path and published by a rename that refuses to replace, so a failed write leaves nothing at the final path; where the platform has no such rename the file is still created in place, but a failed write, fsync or close removes the file that call created, and that route can still expose a partial file while it is written, can still be left behind by a kill, and can undo another writer's rename that lands between its check and the removal; anything already at the final path, of any kind, is left alone and answers false as before; no recorded case exercised a failing write, so none is tagged intentionally-changed and `ensure_link_EPERM`, `ensure_link_ENOTSUP`, `ensure_link_EXDEV` and `ensure_fallback_restamps` replay unchanged; the new cases are in `internal/pabcd/state/write_test.go`).
- `withSessionLock` creates the lock file with an exclusive `writeFileSync` before the pid is written, so a failed write (a full disk) throws with an empty lock file left behind, and with no stale-lock breaker every later acquisition exhausts until someone removes the file by hand (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/state.ts:676`; read from the source, not exercised); port: kept.
- `withSessionLock` releases by path with `rmSync(lockPath, { force: true })` and never checks that the file is still its own, so a lock that another process took after the first was removed by hand is deleted by the first holder's release and two critical sections can overlap (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/state.ts:689`; case `lock_release_removes_a_foreign_lock` in `internal/pabcd/state/testdata/oracle-writes.json`); port: kept.
- `ensureState` refuses an id that `sanitizeKey` would rewrite, but `writeState` and `withSessionLock` accept one and use the rewritten key, so `a/b` and `a-b` share one state file and one lock and the later write replaces the earlier (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/state.ts:380` against `:624` and `:672`; cases `write_alias_ids` and `lock_key_is_sanitised` in `internal/pabcd/state/testdata/oracle-writes.json`); port: kept.
- A process killed between the temp write and the rename of `writeState` leaves `<id>.json.<pid>.<ms>.tmp` behind for good: nothing sweeps orphaned temp files (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/state.ts:626-639`; case `killed_between_temp_write_and_rename` in `internal/pabcd/state/testdata/oracle-writes.json`); port: kept.

## Found by the attest and plan gate port

- `hasFailVerdictTail` reads the last verdict-shaped line of the final five as FAIL only when it matches `^verdict\s*[:=]\s*fail\b` after the line is trimmed, so `VERDICT: FAILED`, `VERDICT: FAILURE`, a bold-wrapped `**VERDICT: FAIL**`, `Verdict - FAIL` and a verdict that follows a lone carriage return or U+2028 are not FAIL while `VERDICT: FAIL-SAFE` is, and an A>B attest that claims pass over a tail of `VERDICT: FAILED` advances (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/attest.ts:128-133`; recorded in the `tails` and A>B cases of `internal/pabcd/attest/testdata/oracle-attest.json`); port: kept.
- `validatePlanArtifacts` is described as requiring a `devlog/_plan` unit of numbered files, but it accepts any directory, inside or outside the working directory (absolute or with `..`), that holds one entry named like `NNN_x.md`; a `planPaths` entry only has to exist, so `/etc/hostname` passes (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/plan-gate.ts:31` and `:46-76`, header comment `:5-8`; recorded as `absolute_outside_cwd`, `relative_dotdot_sibling`, `planpaths_outside_unit`, `unit_is_cwd_parent` and `cwd_symlink_physical_has_unit` in `internal/pabcd/attest/testdata/oracle-plan-gate.json`); port: fixed (CRW-425, a review finding of kind security fixed during the port by decision: a `planUnit` or `planPaths` entry that is not the working directory or below it, the symlinks of the entry and of the working directory followed, is refused; the replay in `internal/pabcd/attest/oracle_test.go` tags the five cases intentionally-changed).
- `validatePlanArtifacts` returns the unit as the lexical `relative(resolve(cwd), unit)`, so an absolute entry that follows `symlink/..` is validated as one directory and persisted as another, and the persisted spelling can name a directory outside the working directory (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/plan-gate.ts:79-80`; found while fixing the escape above and reproduced with `<workspace>/alias/../unit`, not in the recorded corpus); port: fixed (CRW-425: the unit returned is the first of its lexical relative spelling, its physical relative spelling and the real path that resolves from the working directory to the validated directory).
- `validatePlanArtifacts` counts a directory named like `NNN_x.md` as a numbered plan document, because it filters the unit's entry names with a regular expression and never asks what an entry is (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/plan-gate.ts:31` and `:61`; recorded as `dir_named_like_doc` in `internal/pabcd/attest/testdata/oracle-plan-gate.json`); port: kept.

## Found by the corpus replayer's SQLite handling

- The `json-epoch-ms` normalisation rule (`contract/schema/cxc/normalisation.json:79-83`) rewrites any JSON number of 13 digits that starts with 16 to 19 to the text `<MS>`, including the integer primary keys of the FTS5 shadow tables `msgs_fts_data` and `msgs_tri_data` in the recall index, so five recorded index dumps hold a string where the oracle's database holds an integer key and cannot be restored verbatim (fixtures `cli__chat__index_status`, `cli__chat__search_refresh_builds_index`, `hook__post-compact-injecting-recall-context__silent_even_with_history`, `hook__session-start-injecting-recall-context__compact_source_gets_recovery_directive` and `hook__session-start-injecting-recall-context__startup_with_index_injects_untrusted_block`); a defect of the recorder's normalisation, not of the oracle. The round-trip test rebuilds those keys as distinct numbers the same rule turns back into `<MS>`; port: kept.

## Found by the session-binding port

- With neither `CODEX_SQLITE_HOME` nor `CODEX_HOME` set and an empty `HOME`, `resolveNativeSession` looks for the native state databases in `.codex` under the working directory: `os.homedir()` is empty and `join` keeps the relative path (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/session-binding.ts:60`; the same family as the goal-active line above; checked with Node 24); port: kept.
- The newest-database lookup lists `home` as the operating system resolves it (`readdirSync`, which follows a symlink before `..`) but opens `resolve(home, name)`, which removes `..` lexically, so a `CODEX_SQLITE_HOME` or `CODEX_HOME` ending in `<symlink>/..` lists one directory and opens the file of another (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/session-binding.ts:61` and `:68`; checked with Node 24); port: kept.
- An INTEGER beyond ±(2^53-1) in any of the four columns `resolveNativeSession` selects makes `node:sqlite` throw, so a returned row that should be refused as archived, as not a root source or for its directory is reported as an unreadable database (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/session-binding.ts:81` and `:105-108`; checked with Node 24); port: kept (`safe` in `internal/pabcd/host/session.go`).
- Every string `resolveNativeSession` handles is a JavaScript string decoded from UTF-8 with U+FFFD for each invalid sequence: the stored cwd, the answers of `realpath` and `getcwd`, argv, the environment and the account home. A path holding invalid bytes is therefore named by its replacement spelling and never by the bytes on disk, so the oracle fails on a directory named with the stored bytes and succeeds on one named with U+FFFD, and the decoded strings of two directories that differ only in invalid bytes are equal, so they are one path (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/session-binding.ts:41-100`; checked with Node 24); port: kept (`decodeUTF8` where a path enters or comes back).
- Opening a WAL-mode state database with `readOnly: true` still creates its `-wal` and `-shm` files when they are absent, so the `never create` comment holds for the database file only (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/session-binding.ts:77-79`; checked with Node 24); port: kept (SQLite does the same through the driver).

## Found by the hook ingress library port

- `readStdin` turns any failure to read the hook's input into empty input, so a read error reads as an empty payload, which the handlers of every event but one answer with nothing to do, a `pre-tool-use` guard included (only the automation ownership gate denies an input it cannot parse, `automation-ownership-gate.ts:71` and `:90-92`), although the oversize branch beside it exists to refuse to bypass policy (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/cli.ts:83` and `:96-98`); port: kept (`ReadStdin`).
- The hook observation directory is `join(process.env.CODEX_HOME ?? ~/.codex, "codexclaw", ...)`, so a CODEX_HOME that is set but empty puts it under the working directory (source `plugins/codexclaw/scripts/hook-observation.mjs:17-18`); port: kept.
- A JSON string holding a lone surrogate escape is kept as written by JavaScript, and `JSON.stringify` of it keeps the escape, which is how the hook observation tells apart two actors whose ids differ only in lone surrogates; a Go string cannot hold one (the decoder gives U+FFFD), so the port records no actor whose id contains U+FFFD, a genuine one included (source `plugins/codexclaw/scripts/hook-observation.mjs:18` and `:30-31`); port: pending, the port differs on purpose until a lossless string form is chosen.
- Two session ids that differ only in lone surrogates share one observation directory, because `digest(sessionId)` hashes the string as UTF-8, which writes each lone surrogate as U+FFFD (source `plugins/codexclaw/scripts/hook-observation.mjs:14`, `:18` and `:70`); port: kept (the Go decoder gives the same U+FFFD).

## Found by the session ledger port

- `readInterviewEvents` accepts a row on its `event`, `roundId` and `contradictionCount` alone, so a row without `ts`, `sessionId` or `highContradictionCount`, and a row of another session in the same file, are returned although `InterviewEvent` declares those fields; and two session ids that `sanitizeKey` maps to one name share one scan ledger, so one session reads the other's scan rounds (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/state.ts:780-787`; cases `interview_read_mixed` and the `aliasFile` of `interview_events` in `internal/pabcd/state/testdata/oracle-writes.json`); port: kept.
- `appendInterviewEvent` writes whatever `event` string it is given, because the union type `InterviewScanEvent` is erased at run time, and `readInterviewEvents` then skips every row whose kind is not in `SCAN_EVENT_KINDS`, so a row of another kind is durable but never read back (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/state.ts:753-758` against `:783`); port: kept.
- One transition row is written in two key orders: `evidence` follows `reason` in `evidence-cli.ts:88` and in the hook's D-close rows, which spread the row `applyHumanTransition` builds, and comes last everywhere else, so the bytes of one transition depend on the caller; `hasPabcdCloseRow` parses the rows, so no behavior depends on the order (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/evidence-cli.ts:88-98`, `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/hook.ts:1147` and `:1335` spreading `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/orchestrate-apply.ts:111-118`, against `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/orchestrate-cli.ts:899` and `:1040`; cases `ledger_evidence_resolve`, `ledger_hook_dclose_with_evidence` and `ledger_cli_dclose`); port: kept (`LedgerEntry.EvidenceAfterReason` selects the order).

## Found by the hook dispatch port

- An input over 4 MiB makes every hook whose verb is not `pre-tool-use*`, `stop`, `subagent-stop` or one of the two permission verbs (`permission-request` and `session-start-permission-advisory` exit 0 in silence) print nothing and exit 1, which the host reads as a non-blocking error, so the hook neither allows nor blocks: `session-start`, `user-prompt-submit`, `post-tool-use`, `post-tool-use-render-observation`, `post-compact`, `worktree-guard`, `subagent-stop-review` and `worktree-guard-pretool`, the managed-worktree deletion guard, which does not deny an input it cannot read because its verb does not start with `pre-tool-use` (fixtures `hook__session-start-detecting-managed-worktree__oversized_stdin_exits_1` and `hook__pre-tool-use-guarding-managed-worktree-deletion__oversized_stdin_exits_1`; source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/cli.ts:101-117` and `:342-348`); port: kept.
- `pre-tool-use-automation-ownership` is called "fail-closed" but sits outside every try: an error in it reaches the generic handler, which prints `codexclaw cli failed` and exits 1, a non-blocking status, so a crash allows the automation update (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/cli.ts:408-411` and `:529-532`); port: kept (`crw cli failed`, exit 1).
- `buildContextOutput` cuts an over-long context at a UTF-16 unit, so an astral character on the cut leaves a lone high surrogate, which `JSON.stringify` writes as an escape (`\ud83d`) that is not valid text for the model (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/hook.ts:591-593`; checked with Node 24, `"a".repeat(31935) + "😀" + "b".repeat(100)` gives an output ending `\ud83d\n\n[truncated]`); port: kept (`ContextOutput` writes the same escape).

## Found by the recall query words port

- `koreanStem` skips an ending whose removal would leave a one-syllable stem and takes the next shorter ending that fits, so `집에서는` gives `집에서` although the comment above the ending list says the longest ending wins (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/recall/src/synonyms.ts:146-151`; case `집에서는` in `internal/recall/testdata/oracle-query-words.json`, pinned by `TestKoreanStemOracleQuirks`); port: kept.
- The 하-verbalizer tail rule takes the leftmost 했, 하 or 해 among the last four syllables and stops there, so a word that itself begins with one of them has no stem: `해결했지`, `해결하다` and `오하해하` give none while `결정했지` gives `결정` (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/recall/src/synonyms.ts:135` and `:152-156`; cases in `internal/recall/testdata/oracle-query-words.json`, pinned by `TestKoreanStemOracleQuirks`); port: kept.
- `isSymbolWord` takes any 7 to 40 characters of a to f and digits for a commit SHA without requiring a digit, so an English word spelled with those letters (`defaced`) is boundary-gated and, in a query of more than eight words, required (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/recall/src/query-words.ts:71`, `:103` and `:218-221`; case `defaced` in `internal/recall/testdata/oracle-query-words.json`); port: kept.

## Found by the goalplan record types and sub-record revival port

- `reviveSourceIdentity` fills in what is missing where the session state's rebuild of the same record refuses it: a `dirty` that is the string "yes", the number 1 or absent revives as a clean identity, an absent or non-text `commitSha` as "", an absent or non-text `capturedAt` as 1970-01-01T00:00:00.000Z, and a dirty identity without a `treeHash` is accepted, although `state.ts` states that coercing `dirty:"yes"` to false made a "clean" snapshot that refused a legitimate transition; the lane and final-gate identities of a plan therefore carry guessed values (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/goalplan.ts:415-431` against `state.ts:263-287`; cases `identity_dirty_string_reads_clean`, `identity_dirty_without_tree_accepted`, `identity_bare_kind_only` and `identity_commitSha_missing_defaults` in `internal/pabcd/goalplan/testdata/oracle-revive.json`); port: kept (`reviveSourceIdentity` in `internal/pabcd/goalplan` keeps the lenient rule; the strict `state.reconstructSourceIdentity` is unexported and is not called).
- `revivePlanFiles` keeps any non-empty `path`, an absolute one or one with `..`, and the staleness check reads each path as `resolve(cwd, path)`, so a plan file naming `/etc/hostname` or `../../x` has that file outside the workspace hashed on every check of the round, although the path is documented as relative to the plan directory and the command that writes it refuses a path outside the plan unit (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/goalplan.ts:400-411`, `freeze.ts:23-26`, `review-round-cli.ts:141-165` and `:309-316`; recorded case `files_path_absolute_and_dotdot_accepted` in `internal/pabcd/goalplan/testdata/oracle-revive.json`); port: fixed (CRW-330, a review finding of kind security fixed during the port by decision: `revivePlanFiles` drops the whole list when an entry is absolute or climbs out of the working directory with `..`, so the round reads as a pre-binding round, which nothing rehashes and the A to B check skips; the replay in `internal/pabcd/goalplan/oracle_test.go` tags the case intentionally-changed; the check reads the path text, so a symlink inside the workspace that points outside is not followed, which is left to the reader of the path).
- `revivePlanFiles` keeps any non-empty `sha256` text, so a hash that is not hexadecimal is revived as written (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/goalplan.ts:400-411`; case `files_sha256_not_hex_accepted`); port: kept.
- `reviveReviewRounds` keeps a round whose `planPath` or `planSha256` is the empty string (it tests only that each is text), although opening a round refuses an empty plan hash because "a round without a plan hash cannot be judged fresh or stale", so a hand-edited or damaged plan can hold a round that no command could have opened (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/goalplan.ts:371` against `review-round.ts:127-129`; case `keep_planPath_and_planSha256_empty`); port: kept.
- A round whose binding is missing, including one whose `planFiles` list `reviveReviewRounds` dropped because an entry was malformed, is a "pre-binding" round that the A to B check skips (`return null`), so its recorded verdict, a reviewer FAIL included, is not honoured, although the field comment says the gate reads a missing binding as a refusal and that nothing is grandfathered into approval (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/goalplan.ts:200-205` against `orchestrate-cli.ts:76-79`; cases `binding_empty`, `binding_mixed` and `files_one_bad_entry_null` in `internal/pabcd/goalplan/testdata/oracle-revive.json` show the fields dropped); port: kept.

## Found by the FSM and chat grammar port

- `parseOrchestrateCommand` looks the lower-cased verb token up in a plain object, so the token `constructor`, in any case, finds `Object.prototype.constructor` and the command's verb is the function `Object` instead of a phase or a control verb; the chat hook then passes it to `applyHumanTransition` as a phase and refuses it with a message that quotes the function's source, `illegal transition IDLE->function Object() { [native code] }` (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/orchestrate-grammar.ts:31-40` and `:118`, consumed at `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/hook.ts:896`; checked with Node 24); port: kept (`VerbConstructor`).
- `isLegalEdge` indexes `VALID_TRANSITIONS` with the current phase, so a phase that is a key of `Object.prototype` (`constructor`, `toString`, `__proto__`, `hasOwnProperty`) makes `isLegalEdge`, `canEnter` and `transition` throw `TypeError: (VALID_TRANSITIONS[from] ?? []).includes is not a function` instead of refusing; a state read by `readStateStrict` never holds such a phase (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/fsm.ts:35`; checked with Node 24); port: kept as a refusal (the thrown error is a refusal, as in the attest port; the case is listed in the package doc).
- COMMAND ends in `(.*)$`, whose dot does not match CR, U+2028 or U+2029, and `$` is not multiline, so a command line that still holds one of them after trim, in the text that follows the white space after the verb (inside an `--attest` JSON string, for instance), is not a command and the parser ignores it, while the same characters in that white space, or at either end of the line, are accepted (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/orchestrate-grammar.ts:45` and `:112-127`; checked with Node 24); port: kept.
- The chat hook never reads `attestError`, so a malformed `--attest` in a chat command is taken as no attestation and the free-pass transition goes ahead, although the parser builds the error text for it and its own comment says so (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/orchestrate-grammar.ts:93-96`, `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/hook.ts:860-896`; `attestError` is read by no other module but `orchestrate-cli.ts`, which fills its own variable); port: kept (`OrchestrateCommand.AttestError` is carried; no consumer is on dev yet).

## Found by the bg store port

- `atomicWrite` leaves its temporary file `<path>.tmp-<pid>-<ms>` behind when the rename fails (a directory at the final path gives EISDIR and the file stays; a failed write may leave a partial one), and nothing sweeps it (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/bg-wake/src/store.ts:55-57`; shown by running the unmodified `store.ts` under Node, not by a corpus fixture); port: kept.
- `listRecordIds` counts every entry whose name ends in `.json`, a directory and the bare file `.json` (id `""`) included (source `store.ts:94-96`); port: kept.
- `readJsonOrNull` accepts an array as the object it looks for (`typeof parsed === "object"`); only `isRecord` in `registry.ts:49-53` rejects one (source `store.ts:74`); port: kept.
- `appendLedger` lets an event's own `at` replace the write time (`registry.ts:245` passes one) and swallows every failure, so a store that cannot be made loses its rows without a trace, as the oracle's fail-open rule intends (source `store.ts:80-87`); port: kept.
- `removePath` cannot remove a directory, empty or not, because `rmSync` runs without `recursive` (source `store.ts:111-117`; shown by running the unmodified `store.ts`); port: kept.
- A JavaScript string can hold a lone surrogate, and `registry.ts:222` copies a record's `sessionId` into the ledger, so a record holding `"\ud800"` is logged as `\ud800`; a Go string cannot hold one and becomes U+FFFD, so the port keeps the oracle's bytes only for a caller that passes the raw JSON token (`json.RawMessage`, written compact as given: `JSON.stringify`'s normalising of escapes, numbers and repeated keys is not applied); port: kept.
- The path builders take any id, and `atomicWrite`, `removePath`, `ensureDir` and the ledger append take any path or directory, so an id such as `../../x`, a record file naming another `id` or `cwd` (`registry.ts:55` writes to `recordPath(rec.cwd, rec.id)`), or a `.codexclaw` or `bg` that is a symbolic link writes, deletes or appends outside `.codexclaw/bg`; `cli.ts:150` itself deletes `disabledPath(cwd)` through whatever `bg` is (source `store.ts:30-58` and `:111-117`; the issue names a removal outside `.crw/bg` a security finding); port: fixed for writes, removals, `ensureDir` and the ledger (the store refuses a path that is not a file directly inside the workspace's `.crw/bg`, judged on real paths, creates the temporary file exclusively and opens the ledger without following a link; no recorded fixture covers it; a hard link at a store file is a second name for its file and is not detected), kept for reads, which still follow links, so a planted `<id>.out` link makes `bg get` print another file.
- `bg removal` prints a checklist of the CXC repository's own layout (`removal.ts:9-17`), whose comment says eight touch points while the list and the hard-coded header have nine, and whose README and test say eight (fixture `cli__bg__removal_checklist`, whose note says nine despite the test's eight); port: not carried, the CRW checklist is rewritten (name-substitution 4.3) and its header counts its own steps.

## Found by the helper role store port

- `readRaw` turns every failure of a read into an empty config, so a store that exists but cannot be read (a directory, or a file the process may not open) reads as no overrides and a caller that consults it inherits the main model without a word, while only a write refuses it (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/subagent-config/src/store.ts:152-167`; case `store_is_directory` in `internal/role/testdata/oracle-store.json`); port: kept.
- A primary model is valid when its length is above zero, a fallback model only when its `trim()` is not empty, so a whitespace-only primary model is stored and a spawn would use it, while the same text is refused as a fallback (source `store.ts:86` and `:212` against `:100` and `:229`; case `model_whitespace`); port: kept.
- A stored role value that is not an object (null, an array, a string, a number) reads as the default role but still counts as an override sourced from the global store (source `store.ts:189-192`; case `read_role_shapes`); port: kept.
- `setRole` and `resetRole` read the store, change it in memory and publish it with no lock, so two concurrent writers lose one of the two updates (source `store.ts:271-289` and `:293-301`; read from the source, a race is not exercised); port: fixed by CRW-440 (a data-loss defect, fixed by decision under the parity rule revision of 2026-10-03: `SetRole` and `ResetRole` hold an exclusive lock file beside the store, `lockStore` in `internal/role/lock.go`; `TestLockedWritersLoseNoUpdate` stops one writer between its read and its rename and shows that the other writer's update is kept).
- A process killed between the temporary write and the rename of `writeRaw` leaves `subagents.json.<uuid>.tmp` behind, and nothing sweeps it (source `store.ts:253-263`; read from the source, a kill is not exercised); port: kept.
- `writeRaw` promises to preserve the members of the file it does not own but re-prints them through `JSON.parse` and `JSON.stringify`, so an integer a double cannot hold exactly loses digits on the first rewrite (`12345678901234567890` is written as `12345678901234567000` and `9007199254740993` as `9007199254740992`, while `9007199254740994` survives) and `-0` becomes `0` (source `store.ts:157-160` and `:258`; case `extras_number_forms`); port: fixed (CRW-193, a defect that loses record data under the parity rule revision of 2026-10-03: the port carries those members as the file wrote them, and `TestOracleReplay` in `internal/role` compares that case against the oracle's file with the members as written; the other re-printing differences, such as `1.0` becoming `1`, stay differences of the port and are listed in its package comment).
- `writeRaw` stringifies the whole document recursively, so a store whose unknown member nests arrays a few thousand levels deep (5,000 fails and 3,000 writes on the recording host; it depends on the stack) fails the write with `Maximum call stack size exceeded` and keeps the old file, although `JSON.parse` read it (source `store.ts:258`; checked with Node 24, not in the recorded cases); port: pending, the port differs on purpose and writes such a file.
- A string of a role that holds a lone surrogate escape (`"\ud800"`) is kept by JavaScript and written back as the same escape, but a Go string cannot hold one, so the port reads U+FFFD there (source `store.ts:86-101`; checked with Node 24, not in the recorded cases); port: pending, the same family as the hook ingress line above.

## Found by the subagent evidence attempts port

- `isPathInsideDirectory` refuses every relative path that starts with `..`, so a receipt named `..notes.md` directly under the evidence root, or one under a directory named `..d`, is judged outside it (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/subagent-evidence.ts:139`; cases `receipt_name_starts_with_dotdot` and `dotdot_directory_name` in `internal/pabcd/evidence/testdata/cases.json`); port: kept.
- `extractReceiptPath` takes the first `EVIDENCE_RECORDED:` that is followed by something, not the last line its comment names, so a message that quotes the directive's `EVIDENCE_RECORDED: <path>` before the real marker is judged on the quoted token, and a marker followed only by a newline takes the next marker's own text as its path (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/subagent-evidence.ts:130-133`; cases `first_marker_wins` and `empty_first_then_valid`); port: kept.
- `transcriptHasContextPressure` is exported and tested but no code calls it, and the gate never reads the child transcript, so a compaction marker in `agent_transcript_path` does not release the child, although the fixture id says it does (fixture `hook__subagent-stop-verifying-evidence__context_pressure_releases` records a block with attempt 1; source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/subagent-evidence.ts:175` and `:476-531`); port: kept.
- `readAttempts` reads a counter file that cannot be read or is not JSON as 0 attempts, so a truncated or garbled counter restarts the budget, while an object or array whose `attempts` is not an integer from 0 to 3 reads as the cap (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/subagent-evidence.ts:185-206`; cases `counter_garbage` and `counter_empty`); port: kept.
- `writeAttempts` leaves its temp file behind when the rename fails, for instance when a directory stands where the counter file belongs (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/subagent-evidence.ts:216-226`; case `write_counter_is_directory`); port: kept.
- `recordTombstone`'s second tier replaces a session file it cannot read with a default state that carries `unverifiedCorrupt`, so whatever the unreadable file held is lost (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/subagent-evidence.ts:303-309`; case `tombstone_unreadable_state`); port: fixed by CRW-435 (a state-loss defect, fixed by decision: nothing is written over an unreadable session file and the verdict goes to the marker writer, so completion stays denied; the case is tagged intentionally-changed in `TestTombstone`).
- `receiptClaimed` is cut at 256 UTF-16 units, so an astral character on the cut leaves a lone high surrogate, which `JSON.stringify` writes as an escape; a Go string cannot hold it and the port writes U+FFFD, as the state package documents (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/subagent-evidence.ts:280`); port: kept.
- `hasValidReceipt` resolves symbolic links of the evidence root itself, so a `.codexclaw/evidence` that is a link to another directory makes every non-empty file there a valid receipt, and a directory link below the root is accepted when its target stays inside the real root, while a link as the receipt itself is always refused; a path swapped between its checks and its later use is not defended (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/subagent-evidence.ts:155-170`; cases `symlinked_evidence_root` and `symlinked_state_dir`); port: kept.
- `attemptsPath` names a counter file from the sanitised session id and a digest of the agent and turn ids only, so two sessions whose ids sanitise alike (`a/b` and `a-b`) share one counter for the same agent and turn, and one session's retries or receipt change the other's budget; the session file's own path is keyed by the sanitised id the same way (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/subagent-evidence.ts:97-114` and `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/state.ts` statePath; cases `session_slash` and `session_dash` in `internal/pabcd/evidence/testdata/cases.json`); port: kept.
- `recordTombstone` appends without checking the 64-entry cap that the readers keep, so a 65th verdict is written and reported as recorded, while every later read keeps the first 64, flags the list as overflowed, and `hasTombstone` does not find the new entry (completion stays denied through the overflow flag, and the agent is released again at each stop because its counter stays at the cap) (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/subagent-evidence.ts:254-296` with `reconstructUnverified` in `state.ts`; case `record_65th_when_64_exist`); port: kept.

## Found by the config-guard TOML editor port

- The editor recognises only a bare `[table]` header and a bare key spelled as asked, so `setTableKey` on a table written `[ memories ]` or `["memories"]`, on a quoted key (`"dedicated_tools" = false`) or on a dotted key at the top level (`memories.dedicated_tools = false`) finds nothing and appends a second table or key line, which is invalid TOML (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/config-guard/src/toml-edit.ts:53`, `:154`); port: kept.
- A key-looking or header-looking line that is the inside of a multi-line string is taken for the key or the table: `setTableKey` rewrites it (a `"""` string holding the line `dedicated_tools = false` comes back holding `dedicated_tools = true`) and `restoreTableKey` deletes it, so a line of another setting's value is changed or lost, while a key or table that is really there is passed over (source `toml-edit.ts:63-78`, `:155-160`, `:218`, `:265-266`); port: fixed, under the parity rule revision of 2026-10-03 (a defect that loses settings): the port treats a line that starts inside a `"""` or `'''` string as not there, and the recorded oracle rows differ only on such texts (`internal/runtime/install/configguard`: `TestMultilineStringContentIsNeverEdited` and the neutral rows of `testdata/oracle-toml-edit.json`).
- The scan is line based and has no notion of an array: a line starting with `[` inside a multi-line array is taken for a table header or table start, so on the managed table `setTableKey` inserts the new key inside the array (invalid TOML, nothing deleted), and for a table spelled like the array element (a table named `1` against `a = [` then `[1]`) `restoreTableKey` deletes the root table's key of the same name; the managed-key list names no table that can be spelled that way, so the deletion is reached only through an install manifest whose `tableKeys` record was edited by hand (its parser reads back any table and key) (source `toml-edit.ts:68`, `:73-78`); port: kept.
- A key line that ends in a lone CR with no LF after it, or whose value is followed by U+2028 or U+2029, is not matched by the key pattern (its `.` excludes those characters), so the key reads as absent and `setTableKey` inserts a duplicate key line (source `toml-edit.ts:154`; the same CR-only final line that the `splitLines` entry above describes); port: kept.
- Any edit that changes the file rewrites all of it with the dominant line ending (CRLF only when it outnumbers LF, so a tie gives LF), and a line that ends in a lone CR loses that CR when another line is joined after it, because `withEol` collapses the CR and the joining LF: `[t]\r\nx = 1\r\r\ny = 2\r\n` comes back with `x = 1\r\n`, although the header comment promises that line endings are preserved (source `toml-edit.ts:182-237`, `text-lines.ts:38-41`); port: kept.
- `readTableKey` answers null both for an absent key and for a value form the editor refuses to rewrite (an array, an inline table, a multi-line or unterminated string), so a caller cannot tell the two apart (source `toml-edit.ts:292`); port: kept.

## Found by the session source binding port

- `bindSessionSource` creates its temp file `<binding>.<uuid>.tmp` with `writeFileSync` before the `try`/`finally` that unlinks it, so a write that fails part-way (a full disk) leaves the temp file in `.codexclaw/sources/` for good, although a half-written binding is never published (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/session-source.ts:187-192`); port: kept.
- The containment check of `bindSessionSource` is described in its comments as a defensive invariant that cannot fire, but it fires whenever git cannot see a repository from the session's directory, for example with `GIT_CEILING_DIRECTORIES` set to the repository root and the session in one of its subdirectories: the bind is refused with "Source root must not contain the session's own working directory" (source `session-source.ts:150-170`; port scenario `bind_native_inside_source` in `internal/pabcd/source/session/testdata/scenarios.json`); port: kept.
- `nativeGitIdentity` takes any failure of its helper `git rev-parse --git-dir` (git missing, output over the limit, an unreadable directory) as "the native cwd is not in a repository", although its comment says "ONLY" when it is genuinely not, and `resolveSessionSource` and `bindSessionSource` then skip the native-repository clause of their checks (source `session-source.ts:62-73`); port: kept.
- `gitProbeEnv` removes four git routing variables where `source-identity.ts` removes six, so an inherited `GIT_OBJECT_DIRECTORY` naming a directory that does not exist (git then takes the repository for none) makes every session-source probe fail with "Cannot resolve source Git worktree identity." while the source identity of the same tree still resolves (source `session-source.ts:41-45` against `source-identity.ts:61-66`; port scenario `env_object_dir_breaks_probes`); port: kept.
- `gitIdentity` and `canonical` read git's output and resolved paths as UTF-8 text, so a worktree whose path holds bytes that are not UTF-8 becomes a path that does not exist and can never be bound or resolved, and a target that names such a worktree is bound to another worktree whose name holds U+FFFD in its place, if there is one (source `session-source.ts:24-38`; port scenario `bind_path_with_invalid_utf8`); port: kept.

## Found by the interview answer ledger port

- `appendEvent` appends a row to a ledger whose last line has no line feed (a hand edit, or a crash or a full disk part-way through an earlier write) without a separator, so the new row is joined to that line; the joined line is not JSON, `readQaEvents` and `dimensionsBackedByAnswers` skip it, and both rows are lost while `captureInterviewAnswers` reports the new rows as written (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/interview-ledger.ts:219-224` against `:128-138` and `:177-186`; cases `capture_unterminated_valid_row` and `capture_unterminated_tail` in `internal/pabcd/interview/ledger/testdata/oracle-ledger.json`); port: fixed by CRW-339 (a record-file data-loss defect, fixed by decision: `appendEvent` in `internal/pabcd/interview/ledger` writes a line feed first when the file is a non-empty regular file that does not end in one or whose last byte it cannot read; the two recorded cases are replayed as intentionally changed).
- `appendInterviewEvent`, which writes the scan rows of the same `interviews/<id>.jsonl` file, has the same defect: a scan row appended after a final line without a line feed is joined to it and both are lost (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/state.ts:754-759`; `appendRow` in `internal/pabcd/state/ledger.go`); port: fixed by CRW-474 (a record-file data-loss defect, fixed by decision: `AppendInterviewEvent` in `internal/pabcd/state` writes a line feed first when the file is a non-empty regular file that does not end in one or whose last byte it cannot read, the same guard as `appendEvent` of the ledger package; no recorded oracle case appends to such a file, so none is retagged, and `AppendLedger`, the transition ledger, gets the same guard (next line). The two appenders still share no lock, as in the oracle: two appenders that meet one unterminated tail each add a line feed, which leaves a blank line that both readers skip, and a write that fails part-way after another appender's tail check can still leave a partial line that the next row is joined to).
- `appendLedger`, which writes the transition ledger `ledger.jsonl`, has the same defect, and there no reader skips the joined line: a transition row appended after a final line without a line feed is joined to it, and the readers `readJsonlObjects` of `orchestrate-cli.ts` and `hook.ts` call `JSON.parse` on every non-empty line without a guard, so `hasPabcdCloseRow` throws on the D close of a bound session and its close row is not written (the CLI exits 1, the hook swallows the error) (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/state.ts:696-701` against `orchestrate-cli.ts:431-453` and `hook.ts:622-644`; read from the source, with the throw of `JSON.parse` on a joined line probed under Node 24, not a run of the close); port: fixed by CRW-474 (a record-file data-loss defect, fixed by decision: `AppendLedger` in `internal/pabcd/state` writes a line feed first by the same guard as `AppendInterviewEvent`, through the shared `appendRow`; no recorded oracle case or corpus fixture appends a row to a transition ledger that lacks a final line feed, so none is retagged).
- `captureInterviewAnswers` checks `alreadyRecorded` and appends as two separate steps with no lock, so two captures of one round that overlap (a re-fired hook while the first still runs) can both find no row and both append, leaving one event id twice (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/interview-ledger.ts:215-224` and `:303-342`; read from the source, a race is not exercised); port: kept.
- A response that lists a question with no string answer makes `captureInterviewAnswers` write an `answer_recorded` row with an empty `answers` array (`plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/interview-ledger.ts:322-340`), which `dimensionsBackedByAnswers` ignores (`:194-198`) and the rescan coordinator counts as an answered pair (`plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/rescan-coordinator.ts:82-87`), so the question stops being pending while its dimension stays unbacked (cases `capture_empty_answers_array` and `read_basic` in `internal/pabcd/interview/ledger/testdata/oracle-ledger.json`); port: kept.
- The event id `deriveEventId` builds is turn, question and kind joined with colons, which is not injective: `("t:q", "r")` and `("t", "q:r")` give one id, so the second of two distinct rounds is taken as already recorded and nothing of it is written (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/interview-ledger.ts:111-113` and `:304-325`; case `capture_event_id_collision`; it needs a colon in the host's turn id, and UUIDs have none); port: kept.
- The ledger file is named by `sanitizeKey(sessionId)`, so session ids that sanitise alike (`a/b` and `a-b`) share one ledger, and a round of the second session with the same turn and question ids is dropped as already recorded; the cause is the one of the state-file line above (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/interview-ledger.ts:115-117` with `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/state.ts:244-247`; pinned by `TestSessionIDsThatSanitiseAlikeShareOneLedger`); port: kept.
- `appendEvent` writes through whatever sits at `.codexclaw`, `.codexclaw/interviews` or the ledger file: `ensureCodexclawDir` returns an existing entry of any type untouched, `mkdirSync` with `recursive` accepts a link to a directory and the final open follows a link, so a ledger can be written outside the project by whoever plants a link there; a session id cannot escape, because `sanitizeKey` removes every separator (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/interview-ledger.ts:219-224` and `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/codexclaw-dir.ts:13-23`; read from the source and the sibling writer of the same file, which behaves alike); port: kept, and fixed if a review of kind security names it.
- `readQaEvents` accepts a row only when its `eventId` is a string and `dimensionsBackedByAnswers` does not ask for one, so a hand-appended `question_asked` and `answer_recorded` pair without ids backs a dimension at the I to P gate while `scan record --derive`, which reads through `readQaEvents`, never sees it (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/interview-ledger.ts:133` against `:188-198` and `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/scan-cli.ts:240`; case `read_rows_without_event_ids`); port: kept.
- A lone surrogate escape inside a payload string (`"\ud800"`) survives `JSON.parse` and is written back by `JSON.stringify` as the same escape in a question text or id; a Go string cannot hold one, so the port reads and writes U+FFFD, and two ids that differ only in lone surrogates read as one (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/interview-ledger.ts:66` and `:223`; documented in the package comment of `internal/pabcd/interview/ledger` and pinned by `TestALoneSurrogateEscapeReadsAsTheReplacementCharacter`); port: pending.

## Found by the human transition application port

- A chat `orchestrate p` from I with `override: true` is accepted with no `did`: it opens the soft gate and appends the audit row without evidence, where the CLI's agent override refuses an empty or placeholder `did` (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/orchestrate-apply.ts:141-161` against `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/orchestrate-cli.ts:637-639`); port: kept.
- `unlockedFlag` has a C>D arm that `applyHumanTransition` can never reach: the human D returns at its own branch before the flag is asked for, so the arm is dead code (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/orchestrate-apply.ts:34-38` against `:103-120` and `:127`); port: kept.

## Found by the objective metrics port

- `parseMetricLine` and `recordObjectiveMetric` both normalise the metric name and the normalisation is not idempotent (it strips dots after `sanitizeKey` has stripped dashes), so `METRIC .-a=1` parses as `-a` and is recorded as `a`, and `.-.` becomes `-` and then `missing` (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/metrics.ts:66`, `:119` and `:177`; port scenarios `parse_lines` and `ingest_names_are_normalised_twice` in `internal/pabcd/metric/testdata/scenarios-metrics.json`); port: kept.
- An empty session id is falsy, so `readObjectiveMetrics(cwd, "")` returns the rows of every session: a record with it takes its baseline and best from other sessions, `readObjectiveKind` is `maximize` when any session has a row, `checkObjectivePlateau` mixes sessions, and its kind file is `missing.json` (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/metrics.ts:115`, `:120`, `:168` and `:54`; port scenario `record_empty_session_reads_every_session`); port: kept.
- `recordObjectiveMetric` does not check the value: NaN and the infinities are written as `null` (each of value, baseline and best that is not finite), the reader then drops the row, and the call returns the row as if it had been recorded; with no valid row before it, the next reading starts a new baseline (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/metrics.ts:87-92` and `:118-136`; port scenario `record_nan_and_infinities`); port: kept.
- `baseline` is copied from the first row's stored `baseline`, not from its value, and `best` is the larger of the last row's stored `best` and the new value, so a hand-edited row propagates (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/metrics.ts:121-130`; port scenario `record_best_follows_the_last_row`); port: kept.
- `checkObjectivePlateau` takes NaN and the infinities as options without checking them: a NaN `minRecords` judges the whole history and skips the length gate, `+Infinity` is never flat, a NaN `noiseFloor` is never flat and `+Infinity` always flat (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/metrics.ts:207-220`; port scenario `plateau_option_edges`); port: kept.
- A work phase id of `""` is kept as written, not replaced by `default`, because the default applies only when the id is absent (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/metrics.ts:126`; port scenario `record_empty_work_phase_is_kept`); port: kept.
- `readExplicitObjectiveKind` ignores the `sessionId` inside the kind file and honours the last of a repeated `kind` key, so two session ids that sanitise to one file name read the same kind (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/metrics.ts:156-163`; port scenarios `kind_raw_files` and `kind_file_names_and_bodies`); port: kept.
- A ledger that cannot be read for any reason is read as empty, so the next record starts a new baseline and is appended anyway (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/metrics.ts:69-75`; port scenario `read_missing_and_unreadable_ledger`); port: kept.
- `recordMetricsFromText` reads the whole ledger again for every METRIC line (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/metrics.ts:185-197`); port: kept.
- Two concurrent `recordObjectiveMetric` calls for one session and metric each derive baseline and best from the rows they read before either appends, so the later row in the file can carry the lower best (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/metrics.ts:118-136`); port: kept (the port locks the append, not the read before it).
- `recordObjectiveMetric` does not check `source`: a value other than `operator-entered` or `evaluate.sh` (reachable only from a caller that does not use the CLI's check; the Go zero value of the type is one) is written, the reader drops the row, and the call reports it as recorded (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/metrics.ts:134` against `:93`); port: kept.
- `writeObjectiveKind` replaces the kind file of another session whose id sanitises to the same file name (`a/b` and `a?b` are both `a-b.json`), also when the writes race, so the first session's setting is lost (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/metrics.ts:54` and `:138-153`; port scenario `kind_alias_collision`, tagged intentionally changed in the golden); port: fixed (a state file is lost: the port refuses the write and keeps the file when it holds another session's id, holds an id with U+FFFD, is not a regular file or can not be read, under a lock on the kind directory; a file read without a string `sessionId` is replaced as before; an id that holds U+FFFD can not rewrite its own file).
- An append onto a ledger whose last byte is not a newline fuses the unterminated row and the new one into one line the reader drops, so a valid final row is lost together with the new row (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/metrics.ts:134`; port scenarios `record_after_unterminated_row` and `record_after_partial_row`, tagged intentionally changed in the golden); port: fixed (a record is lost: the port starts the new row on its own line, also when it can not read the ledger to check, with the check and the write under a lock on the ledger).
- `Math.max(...values)` throws a RangeError when the plateau window holds more than about 1.2e5 rows and the length gate passes (`minRecords` NaN, or not above the window); with exactly 150000 matching rows 150000 and NaN throw, 150001 and `+Infinity` return early (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/metrics.ts:219`); the port computes the maximum and does not throw, since the limit is the platform's argument count and the Stop hook catches the throw as no progress; port: intentionally changed (the Go plateau check takes the maximum without spreading the window into arguments, so it does not throw; parent ruling 2026-10-03, as for the JSON depth limit of the FSM port).
- A lone-surrogate escape in a ledger string (for example a work phase id written as `\ud800`) survives in a JavaScript string, but a Go string cannot hold it and reads U+FFFD, so two rows the oracle keeps apart can merge in the plateau window (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/metrics.ts:213`; reachable only through a hand-edited ledger or a caller that builds such a string); port: kept as a platform difference (a Go string cannot hold a lone surrogate; parent ruling 2026-10-03, as in the subagent evidence port).

## Found by the goalplan slug validation and plan revival port

- `assertNotSymlink` asks `existsSync` before `lstatSync`, and `existsSync` follows the link, so a symbolic link whose target does not exist is not refused at the state root, the plans root or the slug directory, and a link swapped in between the check and the later use of the path is not defended; the writers fail while the link is dangling, but one made to point at an existing directory after the check is followed (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/goalplan.ts:309-313`, `:320-331`; test `TestGoalplanDirDanglingSymlinkIsNotRefused`); port: fixed by CRW-340 for reads and locks with non-following opens, fstat and descriptor-path binding, and port: fixed by CRW-357 for plan writes and ledger appends with descriptor-relative creation, open and rename; the lexical GoalplanDir helper remains unchanged.
- `goalplanDir` tests `dir.startsWith(plansRoot + sep)` and `validateGoalplanSlug` tests `"."` and `".."`, but both can never fire once the slug pattern holds (the pattern needs an alphanumeric first character); the port keeps the first test and omits the second (source `goalplan.ts:300-302`, `:326`); port: kept.
- `reviveGoalplan` skips a task that is not an object or lacks a text `id` or `title`, and drops the non-text entries of `criteriaIds`, without a diagnostic, although a bad `dependsOn` on the same task fails the whole plan, so a damaged plan reads as a smaller one and the next write persists the loss (source `goalplan.ts:597-600`, `:610-612`; cases `task_entry_*` and `phase_criteriaIds_mixed` of `oracle-plan.json`); port: fixed for writes (CRW-604: `WithGoalplanWriteLock` refuses to call its writer for a plan whose revival would drop or change a stored non-null value, naming the first such place, with Kind `unreadable`; the display readers still return the shrunk plan, as the recorded cases show).
- Only the keys the revival knows survive a read: an unknown key at the top of a plan, of a work phase, a task, a criterion, a decision or the host object is dropped, so a field written by another tool, or by a newer build of the same `schemaVersion`, is lost on the next write; the version refusal protects only against a declared newer version (source `goalplan.ts:583-675`; case `extra_keys_dropped`); port: fixed for writes (CRW-604: `WithGoalplanWriteLock` refuses to call its writer for a plan whose revival would drop or change a stored non-null value, naming the first such place, with Kind `unreadable`; the display readers still return the shrunk plan, as the recorded cases show).
- A `schemaVersion` of 0 or a negative number revives as written, and a fraction is floored (2.9 reads as 2), although a new plan's version is clamped into 1 to 3; 3.5 is refused as newer than 3 while its floor 3 would be accepted; `-1e999` is dropped, so the plan reads as version 1, while `1e999` is refused (source `goalplan.ts:580`, `:667-669` against `:988-993`; cases `version_v0`, `version_negative`, `version_fraction_3_5`, `version_huge` and `version_huge_negative`); port: kept.
- `validIsoTime` accepts only the exact text `toISOString` prints, so a decision whose `askedAt` or `decidedAt` is another valid time (`2026-01-01T00:00:00Z`, an offset form) fails the whole plan (source `goalplan.ts:522-526`; cases `decision_askedAt_no_millis` and `decision_askedAt_offset`); port: kept.
- A lone surrogate escape in a stored plan reaches the revivers as U+FFFD, because a Go string cannot hold one and the decoder substitutes it (see the `receiptClaimed` line above and the `revive.go` comment), where the oracle's strings keep it and write it back as written. A text field holding one (`objective`, a title, a scenario) is altered on the next write; two decision options that differ only by lone surrogates compare equal, so `reviveDecisionOptions` refuses a plan the oracle accepts; and an option and a recommendation that differ only by lone surrogates compare equal, so `reviveDecisions` accepts a plan the oracle refuses at `goalplan.ts:551`. The oracle's answers for such inputs are not recorded, because Go cannot reach them (source `goalplan.ts:532-536`, `:551`, `:652`; test `TestRevivalLoneSurrogatesReadAsReplacementCharacters`). The same substitution makes the error text of `validateGoalplanSlug` show U+FFFD where `JSON.stringify` shows the escape; that is unreachable from a command line on Linux. The reader of CRW-340 refuses unpaired surrogate escapes before returning a plan that could be written lossily; port: fixed for file reads, while the direct revival functions remain unchanged.

## Found by the helper role spawn resolution and settings API port

- `resolveSpawnConfig` looks the role's settings up without a guard, so a role name that is not one of the four makes it throw `TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'mode')` instead of refusing it (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/subagent-config/src/store.ts:363-364`; case `resolve_unknown_role` in `internal/role/testdata/oracle-settings.json`); port: kept as a refusal (`unknown role "x"`; the text is not V8's).
- `isTrackedProjectConfig` answers false when git fails, is absent or does not answer within 1.5 s, and `projectTrustWarning` then takes the config as untracked and uses it without the review token, so a Git-tracked project config is trusted whenever git is slow or missing: the check fails open (source `store.ts:318-327` and `:169-175`; read from the source, not exercised); port: kept (the helper is ported, nothing reads a project config any more).
- `isTrackedProjectConfig` and `projectConfigTrustToken` run git in the inherited environment, so GIT_DIR, GIT_WORK_TREE, GIT_INDEX_FILE and the other routing variables make `ls-files` and `rev-parse` answer about the repository they name, and the token then binds that root and not the checkout (source `store.ts:318-354`; read from the source); port: kept (the tests drop every GIT_ variable).
- `settingsResponse` answers every failure as a 400 with the message, so a store that cannot be read or written (a permission or I/O error) reads as a client error (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/subagent-config/src/settings-api.ts:28-30`; read from the source); port: kept.
- The messages of `updateSettings` and `getSettings` print `String(value)` of a role, scope, mode or effort of the wrong type, and for an object that has an own `toString` member that conversion throws `Cannot convert object to primitive value`, which `settingsResponse` reports as the 400 instead of the refusal the request deserves (source `settings-api.ts:12` with `store.ts:125`, `:210`, `:220` and `:237`; cases `get_scope_to_primitive` and `update_to_primitive`); port: kept (`jsString` reproduces the throw).
- A `model` of the wrong JSON type is accepted in default mode and stored as null, where the same value in model mode is refused as a missing model (source `store.ts:276-289` and `:212`; case `update_wrong_types`); port: kept.
- `updateSettings` publishes through `setRole` or `resetRole` and then reads the settings again with `readSettings`, so when two updates run at once the answer to one request can describe the state the other produced, although both writes are kept (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/subagent-config/src/settings-api.ts:19-25`; read from the source, not exercised); port: kept (a review finding on the port, answered as parity: the lock keeps the updates, not the snapshot each request returns).

## Found by the CRW-442 config-guard features and managed keys port

- A malformed duplicate row is ignored without clearing a prior valid value, so `hooks stable true\nhooks unknown` stays true even though the last row is unreadable (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/config-guard/src/features.ts:64-74`; recorded test `TestParseFeaturesListRecordedEdges/invalid_duplicate_keeps_prior`); port: kept.
- The feature-list parser validates only the first and last fields: middle columns can be arbitrary, a comment tail drops an otherwise valid row, and a bare CR or U+2028 separates fields rather than rows (`hooks false\rgoals true` reads hooks=true, and `hooks stable true<U+2028>goals stable false` reads hooks=false and omits goals) (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/config-guard/src/features.ts:64-71`; recorded tests `crlf_two_columns`, `comment_tail`, `bare_cr_joins_rows` and `unicode_separator_joins_rows`); port: kept.

## Found by the bg registry port
