- `markDelivered` returns the records as they were passed in, with their old `deliveredAt`, not the ones it wrote, and no caller of v0.2.40 reads the return value (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/bg-wake/src/registry.ts:217-229`, `hook.ts:69`, `:86` and `:102`; checked with Node 24 on the unmodified file); port: kept (`MarkDelivered` returns the same).
- `readExitCode` takes the integer prefix of the body (`parseInt`), so a half-written `1` of `127`, or `3x`, is a final code, although the comment above it says an unparseable or empty read means wait and a record that leaves running is never read against its exit file again (source `registry.ts:88-104` and `:111-118`; checked with Node 24: `1x` gives failed with exit 1); port: kept.
- A running record with no pid and no exit file whose `startedAt` does not parse stays running for ever, although the comment says age bounds it: `Date.parse` is NaN and `!Number.isFinite(age)` returns the record (source `registry.ts:139-140`; checked with Node 24); port: kept.
- `isRecord` tests four keys (id, cwd, command, status), so a record file may lack the others or hold values of other types, and the functions then work on JavaScript's undefined and its coercions: an absent `deliveredAt` counts as delivered (`undefined !== null`), so the record is never woken or adopted; an absent or fractional `pid` is not null and `process.kill` throws, so the pid reads as dead and the record fails; `command` elements that are not text are joined by coercion; and every writer rewrites what it read through a spread, which keeps absent keys, other types, unknown keys and the file's key order (source `registry.ts:48-52`, `:115`, `:142`, `:148`, `:200`, `:221`, `:239-243`, `spawn.ts:120` and `:173`; checked with Node 24 on the unmodified file); port: kept for the records the oracle writes (thirteen keys, typed, in one order). The Go record holds those thirteen keys typed, so an absent key or a value of another type reads as null and is written as null, an unknown key is kept as it was and written after the thirteen, and the key order is the canonical one (`BgRecord` and the package comment of `internal/relay/job/registry.go`).
- `reconcile`, `markDelivered` and `adoptOrphans` act on the `cwd` field of the record they read: the exit file is looked for in that directory and the record and the ledger are written there, so a record file that names another directory (a workspace that was moved or copied) is judged by the wrong exit file and its correction is written, or fails, elsewhere (source `registry.ts:55`, `:112`, `:117`, `:222` and `:245`; checked with Node 24: such a record made `listRecords` throw ENOENT); port: fixed by CRW-443 (the registry takes the workspace it is called for, as the bg store port's line on paths names; the record's own `cwd` is data only).
- `selectWake` sorts with `localeCompare`, which is ICU collation and depends on the host's locale, over the stamps as text, so two `endedAt` values with different UTC offsets sort by their spelling and not by time (source `registry.ts:208`; checked with Node 24: `-01:00` sorts before `+01:00`); port: kept for the UTC stamps the oracle writes, where byte order agrees; Go sorts any other spelling by byte.
- `Date.parse` reads `startedAt` and `endedAt`, accepts what V8's legacy parser allows (`2026-02-30`, `T24:00:00`, slash dates) and takes a zone-less date-time as local time, so the age and duration of a hand-edited record depend on the host's time zone (source `registry.ts:139` and `:264`; checked with Node 24); port: kept for ISO stamps; Go reads the standard spellings (a zone-less date-time as local time) and answers NaN for the rest.
- `disabledState` takes an unreadable `disabled` file (changed permissions, a directory in its place) for no file, because `readTextOrNull` answers null for every read error, so `bg off` stops holding and the wake resumes against it (source `registry.ts:165-170` and `store.ts:60-67`; checked with Node 24: a directory named `disabled` gives `{disabled: false}`); port: kept.
- `readRecord` returns a record under the id it was asked for whatever the record's own `id` says, and `reconcile`, `markDelivered` and `adoptOrphans` then read `exitPath(cwd, rec.id)` and write `recordPath(cwd, rec.id)`, so a record file that holds another record's id (a copied file) is reconciled as that record and its correction overwrites that record's file, and an id that is a path makes the write leave the store (source `registry.ts:58-61`, `:55`, `:112` and `:154-160`; checked with Node 24: `a.json` holding id `b` turned a complete `b.json` with exit 0 into a failed one with exit 7); port: fixed by CRW-443 (a defect that loses a record file: `ReadRecord` answers false for a record whose `id` is not the id asked for, so such a file is junk to every function; no recorded case holds one, so no case is tagged intentionally-changed).

## Found by the divergence mode and candidate archive port

- `readDivergenceCandidates` accepts a row whose `sourceUrls` is an empty array, although `recordDivergenceCandidate` refuses to write one, and it keeps the `worktree`, `metricName` and `note` of an empty string that the recorder never writes, so a hand-written row needs no grounding source (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/divergence.ts:171` against `:222-239`; port scenario `cand_reader_tolerance`); port: kept (the Go candidate carries those three as pointers so that the empty string survives).
- An empty session id is falsy, so `readDivergenceCandidates(cwd, "")` returns the rows of every session, and the mode file of the empty id is `missing.mode.json`, which the id `missing` shares; only the `sessionId` inside the file tells the two apart (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/divergence.ts:70`, `:147` and `:226`; port scenarios `cand_archive_and_session_filter` and `mode_empty_session_id`); port: kept.
- `slug` ends in `|| "candidate"`, which never runs because `sanitizeKey` returns `missing` for a value with nothing left, so an empty title gives the id `strong-1-missing`; the cut to 64 characters follows the sanitising, so an id can end in `-`, and two titles that differ only after their 64th character share an id; an explicit `id` is slugged as well, and nothing keeps ids unique, so a repeated title repeats its id (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/divergence.ts:97-99` and `:183`; port scenarios `cand_ids` and `cand_sources`); port: kept (the dead branch is dropped).
- `recordDivergenceCandidate` does not check `kind` or `status`, and `writeDivergenceMode` does not check `collapsePoint` (the types say so and the CLI parses them): a caller that does not use the CLI writes a row or a file that the reader then drops, and is told it was recorded; a `metricValue` that is not finite is left out without an error (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/divergence.ts:116-121` and `:183-192` against `:150` and `:219`; port scenarios `cand_unchecked_kind_and_status`, `mode_unchecked_collapse_point` and `cand_optional_fields`); port: kept.
- `discardStreak` orders the candidates with `ts.localeCompare`, which is ICU's collation, so `2026-07-01T00:00:00.000Z` sorts before `2026-07-01T00:00:00+09:00` (a time nine hours earlier), `t` before `T`, `10` before `9`, and rows whose `ts` differ only by ASCII control characters tie and keep their order (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/divergence.ts:252`; port scenario `streak_ts_order`); port: kept for ASCII (`CompareTimestamps`, checked against Node's `localeCompare` over 116,384 random pairs); a character beyond ASCII in `ts` is ordered after every ASCII character by code point, where ICU interleaves them, so a streak can differ for a hand-edited `ts`, or a caller-supplied `now`, that holds one: a difference of platform (Go has no ICU collation and the module has no `golang.org/x/text`), open for the parent's decision. The oracle's order also depends on the locale of the host (`ia` against `IZ` is -1 in en-US and +1 in tr-TR); the port has the root order.
- A line of the archive that is not JSON is dropped silently, as is a row that fails the shape test, and so is a line that holds U+00A0 before the JSON (the oracle's `trim` test passes it and its parse fails; a line of only U+00A0 is skipped as blank); a lone-surrogate escape reads as U+FFFD in Go, and a file nested deeper than the Go decoder allows (10,000 levels) reads as not JSON, where the oracle reads it (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/divergence.ts:145-152` and `:211-246`; port scenario `cand_reader_tolerance`; the depth case is a unit test, not a recorded one); port: kept as a platform difference (a Go string cannot hold a lone surrogate and the decoder has a depth limit; parent ruling 2026-10-03, as in the objective metrics port; the mode write refuses such a file instead of replacing it, see the fix below).
- The mode file's temp name `<mode file>.<pid>.<ms>.tmp` is longer than the final name, so a session id whose mode file name fits the 255-byte limit but whose temp name does not fails the write (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/divergence.ts:127`; port scenario `mode_session_id_too_long_for_the_temp_name`); port: kept (the port uses the same temp name).
- `writeDivergenceMode` replaces the mode file of another session whose id sanitises to the same file name (`a/b` and `a?b` both write `a-b.mode.json`), also when the writes race, so the first session's mode is lost and it reads `null`; its temp file is opened with `writeFileSync`, which follows a symbolic link at the predictable temp name and truncates its target (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/divergence.ts:70` and `:126-130`; port scenario `mode_alias_collision`, tagged intentionally changed in the golden); port: fixed (a state file is lost or a file outside the tree truncated: the port refuses the write and keeps the file when it holds another session's id, holds an id with U+FFFD, is not a regular file or can not be read, under a lock on the divergence directory, and creates the temp file exclusively; a file read without a string `sessionId` is replaced as before; an id that holds U+FFFD can not rewrite its own file; a file nested deeper than the decoder allows is refused too, because its owner can not be read).
- An append onto `candidates.jsonl` whose last byte is not a newline (a final `\r` included) fuses the unterminated row and the new one into one line the reader drops, so a valid final row is lost together with the new row (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/divergence.ts:198`; port scenarios `cand_append_after_unterminated_row`, `cand_append_after_partial_row` and `cand_append_after_cr_row`, tagged intentionally changed in the golden); port: fixed (a record is lost: the port starts the new row on its own line, also when it can not read the archive to check, with the check and the write under a lock on the archive).
- `appendFileSync` follows a symbolic link at the archive's path, so a link at `candidates.jsonl` makes the append write the row into the file it points at (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/divergence.ts:198`; port scenario `cand_append_through_a_symlink`, tagged intentionally changed in the golden); port: fixed (a file outside the archive is modified: the port opens the archive with `O_NOFOLLOW` and refuses; `appendRow` of the objective metrics port, which the divergence append repeats, still follows a link at the ledger's path, and a link at `.crw` or `.crw/divergence` is followed by both). The port also refuses an archive that is not a regular file: the oracle appends to a FIFO that has a reader (checked with Node 24, not in the recorded cases), but the port's check of the last byte would block on it.

## Found by the ensureState fallback fix

- The first temp file of `ensureState` (published by `linkSync`) and the temp file of `writeState` (published by `renameWithRetry`) are written with `writeFileSync` and never fsynced, and no `fsync` appears anywhere in `pabcd-state/src`, so a power failure after the publication can leave an empty or short state file at the final path (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/state.ts:389` and `:630-631`; read from the source, a power failure is not exercised); port: kept (the fallback fix of CRW-445 fsyncs its own temp file only, and whether the parity rule revision's data-loss exception reaches the missing fsync of the link path and of `writeState` is for the owner of the state writes to decide).

## Found by the subagent evidence markers, budget and directives port

- `unrecordableVerdictStatus` is a read that writes: through `markerDirWritable` it creates `.codexclaw`, its `.gitignore` and `evidence-unrecordable/`, and writes and removes a probe, on every check, so the goal gate's PreToolUse path changes the workspace (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/subagent-evidence.ts:354-366` and `:374-391`; fixture `hook__pre-tool-use-guarding-goal-complete__baseline_empty_workspace`, whose resulting tree holds the empty marker directory; cases `empty_workspace` and `empty_marker_dir` in `oracle-unrecordable.json`); port: kept.
- The marker lookup and `hasSpentBudget` match a name prefix, `<sanitised session>-`, and not a key, so the markers and the spent counters of a session whose id continues with a dash after this one's (`s1-x` for `s1`) or sanitises to it count for it and deny its completion, and a session id such as `.probe` matches a stale probe file (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/subagent-evidence.ts:377`, `:408` and `:418`; cases `marker_dash_prefixed_session`, `dash_prefixed_session_spent`, `sanitised_session_spent` and `stale_probe_dot_session`); port: kept (the denying direction).
- Two markers for one session and agent in the same millisecond collide on the exclusive create and the second is lost; the first stands, and presence is per session, so nothing is missed (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/subagent-evidence.ts:343-344`; case `same_ms_twice`); port: kept.
- When removing the probe fails for a reason other than the file being gone, the probe stays and `markerDirWritable` reports the directory unwritable for that check, and nothing ever removes the leftover; later probes take other names, so a leftover blocks nothing by itself, except for a session whose id is `.probe` (the line above) (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/subagent-evidence.ts:360-361`; read from the source, not exercised); port: kept.
- A counter file that is garbled or empty restarts the agent's budget in `readAttempts` (read as 0) but counts as spent in `hasSpentBudget`, so one file means two things (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/subagent-evidence.ts:185-206` against `:421-427`; cases `raw_truncated` and `raw_empty_file` in `oracle-unrecordable.json`, and the counter cases of `oracle-evidence.json`); port: kept.
- `resolveTombstone` compares the agent id and turn and ignores `resolvable`, so a valid receipt from an agent with no `agent_id` removes every tombstone with an empty agent id and the same turn, the verdicts of other agents without ids among them, which the `resolvable: false` mark exists to keep from being cleared by id (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/subagent-evidence.ts:435-440` with `:238-241`; case `no_agent_id_clears_idless_tombstones`, tagged intentionally changed in the replay); port: fixed by CRW-436 (security and data loss: other agents' unresolved verdicts were erased, letting completion pass without their evidence; the port returns false before the lock and writes nothing when the identity is not resolvable).
- The marker directory and its probe follow a symbolic link at `.crw/evidence-unrecordable` (or at the state directory above it): markers and probes are created and removed in the directory the link leads to, outside the workspace, with the user's privileges (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/subagent-evidence.ts:339-345` and `:354-366`; cases `marker_dir_is_symlink_out`, `marker_dir_symlink_to_dir` and `state_dir_is_symlink`, tagged intentionally changed in the replay); port: fixed by CRW-436 (security: a planted link made the hook create and remove files outside the workspace; the port refuses a link or a non-directory at the state directory and at the marker directory with Lstat, so the marker write fails and the writability probe reports not writable, and opens the files with O_NOFOLLOW besides O_EXCL; a link put in place after the check, and every other path of the state tree, are not defended and belong to the state-root hardening).
- `resolveTombstone` writes back the verdicts `readState` kept, so the verdicts past the cap of 64 and the entries the read cannot parse vanish from the session file, with only the corruption flag left to deny completion (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/subagent-evidence.ts:437-441`; cases `sixty_six_entries_resolve_first` and `malformed_entry_beside_resolved`, tagged intentionally changed in `TestResolveTombstone`); port: fixed by CRW-436 (a record is lost: nothing is written, and the call reports false, when the file holds more verdicts than the read kept; an intact list under the corruption flag is still resolved, as in the oracle).
- `escalationDirective` is exported but nothing calls it since EVIDENCE-TERMINAL-01 made the budget terminal, and its text still says verification "is now fail-closed" (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/subagent-evidence.ts:463-470`, devlog `260826_readonly_evidence_gate_trap` of the upstream repository); port: kept (ported, called by nothing).
- A counter whose JSON nests deeper than Go's limit of 10,000 levels reads as spent, where the oracle's `JSON.parse` has no such limit and finds the attempts beside the nesting unspent (case `raw_deep_sibling`); port: kept as a platform difference, on the denying side.
## Found by the source-bound receipt parser and check gate port

- `parseArtifactManifest` finds the verdict beside an identity by the `path.dirname` of resolved paths (which `resolve` cleans) but the verdict that needs an identity by the `path.dirname` of the manifest's own spellings, so an identity written `qa/D//artifact-identity.json` or `./qa/D/artifact-identity.json` beside `qa/D/verdict.json`, or a verdict written `qa/./D/verdict.json`, passes the first check and fails the second with "has no identity", while a trailing slash (`qa/D/verdict.json/`) and a directory named `..legit` or `a\b` are accepted (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/source-receipt.ts:117`, `:137-138` and `:162`; cases `mf_quirk_*`, `mf_path_dot_segment_ok`, `mf_path_trailing_slash`, `mf_dotdot_prefixed_dir_ok` and `mf_backslash_dir_ok` of `testdata/oracle-gate.json`, recorded with Node 24); port: kept (`posixDirname` is Node's algorithm).
- Each manifest file is checked by path four times (`lstatSync`, `realpathSync`, `statSync`, `readFileSync`) and a verdict is read by path twice more after its digest passed, and the receipt itself is read by path after `hasValidReceipt`, so a file replaced after a check was read, and a verdict replaced after its digest passed was parsed undigested, from wherever the replacement pointed, including a link to a file outside the workspace (source `source-receipt.ts:122-131`, `:142`, `:155` and `:196`; read from the source, a race is not recordable); port: fixed (security: a path escape and a digest bypass by a swapped file; every read opens the file with `O_NOFOLLOW|O_NONBLOCK` and tests that the descriptor is a regular file, the digested bytes of a verdict are the bytes parsed, and `readfile_test.go` holds the link and pipe cases; a link put in a directory above the file after the `realpath` check is not defended: that needs descriptor-pinned opens of every component).
- `parseArtifactManifest` takes `realpathSync` of the evidence root as the root, so a `.codexclaw/evidence` that is a link to another directory makes every regular file below its target a valid artifact, as it does for `hasValidReceipt` (source `source-receipt.ts:103` and `:124-126`; checked with Node 24: a manifest of a linked root outside the workspace parses); port: kept.
- `validateCheckReceipt` recaptures the tree with the `generatedPaths` that the receipt itself declares, so a receipt that declares a source directory excludes it from the capture it was written with and from the capture it is checked against, and no change there can fail the gate; the test of the oracle covers only a declared path that holds no change (source `check-gate.ts:66-77` and `source-receipt.ts:236-238`; read from the source, not exercised); port: kept (the receipt writer is the agent, and `source-receipt.ts:13-16` says the gate detects movement since the evidence, not that the evidence was produced).
- `createdAt` is judged by `Date.parse`, which takes what V8's ISO parser and its legacy fallback take: `2026-02-30T00:00:00Z`, `2026-01-01T24:00:00Z`, `2026-01-01t00:00:00z`, `+010000-01-01T00:00:00Z`, `5` and `Jan 1 2026` are valid and `2026-01-01T00:00:00,1Z` and `2026-01-01T00:00:00+24:00` are not (source `source-receipt.ts:221`; checked with Node 24); port: kept for the standard spellings of RFC 3339, a date, a month and a year, with and without a time, as `internal/relay/job/registry.go` does: Go refuses the first six and accepts the last two and `+00:60`, which changes which receipts count as dated (`CreatedAtProvided`, which only `ValidateCheckReceipt` reads, and never compares) and no tree they bind.
- A receipt or verdict is read as one string, which V8 caps at 0x1fffffe8 characters (an artifact digest is a buffer, capped at 2 GiB), and a lone-surrogate escape is a lone surrogate there (source `source-receipt.ts:129`, `:142`, `:155` and `:196`); the port refuses a file of more than 0x1fffffe8 bytes, artifacts included (a byte count for a character limit, where Node digests an artifact up to 2 GiB), reads a lone-surrogate escape as U+FFFD and refuses nesting deeper than the 10,000 levels of Go's decoder as not valid JSON, where V8 reads it; port: kept as a platform difference, on the denying side except for the surrogate.

## Found by the bg spawn port

- After twenty collisions `newId` returns `"bg" + Date.now().toString(36)` without asking again whether a record has that id, so it can return an id that exists, and the new job's record and files then replace the old job's; the id also leaves the `bg[0-9a-f]{6}` form that the id alias of the normalisation matches, which no reader of the ids requires (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/bg-wake/src/spawn.ts:31`; the collision checked with Node 24); port: fixed by CRW-444 (the fallback is asked again and, if taken, numbered; `TestNewID`). Twenty independent collisions of a 24-bit id are out of reach in practice, so this is a lost record in principle, fixed under the data-loss class of the parity rule.
- `runBackground` with an empty command builds the script `( ) > out 2>&1; ...`, which the shell refuses as a syntax error (exit 2, checked with dash): nothing runs and no exit file is written, so the record is later settled failed as "watcher vanished"; the CLI refuses an empty command first, `runBackground` does not (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/bg-wake/src/spawn.ts:85-91`, `plugins/codexclaw/components/bg-wake/src/cli.ts:103`); port: kept.
- A shell that cannot start because of ENOENT, EACCES, EAGAIN, EMFILE or ENFILE is reported on the child's error event after `runBackground` has returned: the caller, and `bg run --json` with it, gets a record that says running with pid null, and the file turns failed on the next tick (checked with Node 24: running and running at the return, running and failed one tick later); any other start failure (E2BIG, for one) is thrown before a record exists (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/bg-wake/src/spawn.ts:114-125` and `:143-145`); port: kept, with the same split and the same final state; the Go port writes the failed file before it returns, which is the one difference in timing.
- `cancel` writes cancelled whether or not a signal landed, because a failed `kill` is swallowed, and it never goes past SIGTERM, so a job that ignores it keeps running under a record that says cancelled (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/bg-wake/src/spawn.ts:161-176`); port: kept.
- `processStartToken` is read once, right after the shell has started, and answers null when `ps` fails or prints nothing, which is then recorded as no token; for such a record `reconcile` and `cancel` skip the check that the pid is still the same process (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/bg-wake/src/spawn.ts:136`, `plugins/codexclaw/components/bg-wake/src/registry.ts:68` and `:143`); port: kept.
- `cancel` signals the group of the pid in a record whose startToken is null (the Windows case, or a record edited by hand) without any ownership test, and a pid can be recycled between the comparison and the signal in any case (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/bg-wake/src/spawn.ts:160`); port: kept (the range guard this port adds does not prove ownership).
- A job whose record was never written keeps running unmanaged, and the exit file its shell writes is never read: a CLI that dies between `spawn` and `writeRecord` does it, and so does a `writeRecord` that throws (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/bg-wake/src/spawn.ts:114-143`); port: kept.
- `newId` takes the seed without looking at its shape, so a seed such as `x/../y` names a job whose files clean to `y` and whose record the oracle's `readRecord` returns under both names; in Go the confinement of the store refuses a path that leaves `.crw/bg` and `ReadRecord` refuses a record whose id is not the name asked for (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/bg-wake/src/spawn.ts:26-32`); port: kept (the CLI never passes a seed).
- The paths of `<id>.out` and `<id>.exit` are cleared before the start but written later by the shell, so a link planted in the directory in between is followed (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/bg-wake/src/spawn.ts:91` and `:111-114`), and `recordExists` and the publication of the record are not one step, so two runs can draw the same id (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/bg-wake/src/spawn.ts:26-32`); port: kept (outside what the store promises, as its header says).
- The record and the ledger keep the command as given, so a secret passed as an argument is stored in plain text in `<id>.json` and `ledger.jsonl` (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/bg-wake/src/spawn.ts:133` and `:144`); port: kept.
- The Windows branch of `buildShell` writes the helper with a plain write that follows a link at `<out>.helper.cjs`; the Go package builds on POSIX only, so the branch is only checked as text (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/bg-wake/src/spawn.ts:79-84`); port: kept.
- `reconcile` reads the exit file and only then asks whether the pid is alive and its start token still matches, so a shell that writes its exit file and exits between the two reads is settled failed with exitCode null ("watcher vanished") although its code is on disk, and a settled record is never read against the exit file again; the spawn tests met this as a flake while they polled `Reconcile` as a job ended and now wait for the exit file first (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/bg-wake/src/registry.ts:111-148`); port: kept (in `registry.go`).
- The shell of a job runs with the workspace as its directory but is handed `outPath` and `exitPath` as they were built, so a relative workspace resolves them a second time and the files land outside the directory the store confines (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/bg-wake/src/spawn.ts:104-114`); port: fixed by CRW-444 (a security-checklist fix: `RunBackground` hands the shell the absolute paths the store judged; `TestRunBackgroundWritesTheConfinedPathsOfARelativeWorkspace`; no recorded case has a relative workspace, so none is tagged intentionally-changed).
- `mv` is given the temporary exit path without `--`, so a relative path that starts with `-` is read as an option and the exit code is never published; theoretical on both sides once the CLI passes an absolute workspace (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/bg-wake/src/spawn.ts:91`; dash and GNU `mv` checked); port: fixed by CRW-444 (`BuildShell` writes `mv --`; `TestBuildShellKeepsADashedRelativePathAnOperand`).
- `<id>.exit.tmp` is not removed before the start, while `printf >` writes through a link planted there and truncates its target (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/bg-wake/src/spawn.ts:91` and `:111-112`; checked: the target's content became the exit code); port: fixed by CRW-444 (the removal list gains it; `TestRunBackgroundClearsStaleFiles`).
- `cancel` signals `-pid` for any pid a record holds: 0 is the caller's own process group and 1 becomes every process the user may signal (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/bg-wake/src/spawn.ts:160-168`); Node already rejects a pid above 2^31-1 before the call, but a Go `int` such as 2^32 or 2^32+1 would be cut by the kernel call to 0 or 1 before the sign is applied, so the guard also bounds the range; port: fixed by CRW-444 (`Cancel` signals only 1 < pid <= 2^31-1; `TestCancelNeverSignalsAnUnsafePid`). Under the parity rule of 2026-10-03 this reaches slightly beyond a reported security finding and a settings or record data loss; it is the trust-bypass item of the checklist, it changes nothing for a record the oracle's own spawn wrote, and it can be reverted on its own.

## Found by the CRW-341 prompt detector port

- `detectMemoryWriteRequest` scans quoted and negated text without the request-line filter, so both `"remember this"` and `Do not remember this` return true (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/memory-write-gate.ts:78-105`; recorded detector cases in `internal/pabcd/hook/detect_test.go`); port: kept.
- `detectAgbrowseSearchRequest` also scans the whole prompt and accepts ordinary implementation prose containing an action word, so `Do not use agbrowse to search`, `"agbrowse search"` and `Please describe the agbrowse hook with tests` return true (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/hook.ts:307-312`; recorded detector cases); port: kept.
- `requestLines` addresses each backtick command against the original line, so `Run ` + backtick-quoted `crw-loop` + ` then use ` + backtick-quoted `crw-pabcd` + ` to plan` keeps the first mode but strips the second and detects no phase (source `hook.ts:247-254`, with the declared name substitution; direct request-line case); port: kept.
- `requestLines` recognizes only triple-backtick fences, so an example between Markdown tilde fences still yields a loop request (source `hook.ts:239-242`; direct request-line case with `~~~`, a mode request, and `~~~`); port: kept.

## Found by the edit lint and IDLE advisory port (CRW-342)

This section supersedes the seeded `port: pending` statuses for native Write/Edit bypass and subagent edit skipping: both are now `port: kept`, proven by `allowed_edits` and `subagent_payload_skips_lint`. The latter is the shared harness's root-only exit, unchanged by this port; the historical seed lines are retained under this lane's append-only document scope.

- Native Write/Edit payloads carry `content` or `new_string`, but the lint reads only `tool_input.command`, so those edits bypass it even though their tool names are matched (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/comment-lint.ts:130-152`; corpus `allowed_edits`, tests `TestHandleApplyPatchLint`); port: kept.
- Static matches include tokens in comments and string literals, and a `justified:` marker exempts the whole line even without a reason (source `comment-lint.ts:44-45` and `:118-123`; tests `TestLintApplyPatch`); port: kept.
- Every line beginning with `+++` is treated as a header and skipped, including added source content beginning with two pluses; `+ +` content is also skipped (source `comment-lint.ts:90-96`; tests `TestAddedRecordsAndLines` and `TestLintApplyPatch/triple_plus_content_skipped`); port: kept.
- The advisory does not check `hook_event_name` and parses untrimmed JSON, so a non-PreToolUse edit payload may advise while a BOM-prefixed payload cannot, although the lint accepts that BOM (source `idle-edit.ts:74-83` against `comment-lint.ts:145-147`; tests `TestIdleEditAdvisorySuppressionAndPayloadQuirks`); port: kept.
- A failed counter write leaves the old count, so an eligible count repeats the advisory on later edits (source `idle-edit.ts:98-104`; tests `TestIdleEditCounterFailuresStayFailOpen`); port: kept.
- The advisory's read-default-write overwrites unreadable session JSON under an active goal, and its read-cap-write drops overflowed or malformed unresolved-subagent records (source `idle-edit.ts:87-100`, `state.ts:57-95`, `:494-510` and `:587-589`; Node-recorded cases `corrupt_goal`, `overflow` and `malformed`, intentionally-changed; tests `TestIdleEditPreservesLossyState`); port: fixed (data loss: a strict unreadable read is silent and nothing is written back from a corrupt/capped record list; the cosmetic count can remain unchanged).
- The unlocked counter update writes a whole stale state, losing records a participating writer added between its read and write (source `idle-edit.ts:87-100`; deterministic test `TestIdleEditRereadsAfterParticipatingWriter`, intentionally-changed); port: fixed (data loss: the counter re-reads inside the existing session lock; lock/write errors still allow the advisory, and writers that do not take the lock remain outside this guarantee).
- Reading a legacy D-close marker without a valid successor and writing that reconstructed marker loses its authoritative legacy distinction on the next read (source `state.ts:440-479` through `idle-edit.ts:87-100`; test `TestIdleEditPreservesLossyState/legacy_recovery`, intentionally-changed); port: fixed in this hook (data loss: its cosmetic update is suppressed for a legacy recovery marker; the shared state APIs remain unchanged).
- JavaScript preview slicing can end on a lone UTF-16 surrogate, and JSON input can contain one; Go strings/JSON replace it with U+FFFD, as the shared state port does (source `comment-lint.ts:123` and `:145`; Node-recorded preview boundary case, ordinary astral slicing covered by `TestLintJavaScriptTextSemantics`); port: kept as a platform difference.
- A valid hook payload with an unrelated array nested more than 10,000 levels deep reaches the oracle's lint but fails Go's JSON scanner before the command is read, yielding the fail-open empty answer despite remaining below the stdin limit (source `comment-lint.ts:145-151`; Node depth probe and `TestLintJSONDepthPlatformLimit`); port: kept as a platform difference (the static lint's documented bypasses remain, and the parser is not widened here).

## Found by the goalplan reads and mkdir lock port (CRW-340)

- `firstInvalidField` can report `(unknown)` for a stored/requested slug mismatch, a criterion with no id, or malformed nested steering entries; its host diagnostic can also name a defaulted host rather than the actual rejected nested field (source `goalplan.ts:875-913`; recorded cases `slug`, `criterion_id`, `steering_entry`); port: kept.
- Read diagnostics expose Node filesystem/JSON parser messages, which cannot be reproduced verbatim with Go errors; the diagnostic kind, path and invalid-shape field/detail remain the oracle's, while OS/JSON detail uses Go wording (source `goalplan.ts:709-725`; recorded cases `invalid_json`, `trailing_json`, `plan_symlink`); port: kept as a platform difference.
- Path checks before reads/locks are not bound to the opened inode, a dangling link passes, and a linked lock or owner can disclose outside metadata (source `goalplan.ts:702-710`, `:769-803`, `:818-838`; recorded cases `dangling_plan`, `dangling_root`, `lock_symlink`, `owner_symlink`, intentionally-changed); port: fixed by non-following descriptor walks, fstat and exact canonical path binding. FIFO/special-file reads are refused before reading.
- Recursive pathname cleanup can remove a lock another writer substituted during a callback (source `goalplan.ts:866-871`; `TestPinnedLookupAndObservedReplacement`, intentionally-changed); port: fixed for an observed replacement by descriptor-path/inode checks and non-following cleanup. The mkdir protocol still requires cooperating writers: removal/replacement between the final check and unlink, or directory relocation after the last binding check, is not atomic or prevented.
- Go JSON decoding substitutes U+FFFD for an unpaired surrogate escape and could lose stored text on a later write; the reader refuses such plans without changing bytes (source `goalplan.ts:721`, `:727`; recorded case `lone_surrogate`, intentionally-changed; paired surrogates and escaped-backslash controls pass); port: fixed for file reads.

## Found by the helper role native catalog port

- `nativeCatalogPath` decodes a TOML basic string with `JSON.parse`, so TOML-only escapes such as `\U0001F600` and `\e` make the configured catalog unavailable instead of selecting its valid TOML path (source `subagent-config/src/catalog.ts:67-75`; `TestNativeCatalogPaths/toml-unicode-escape`); port: kept.
- `readNativeCatalog` catches every read/parse failure and returns null, so an unreadable or malformed catalog is indistinguishable from a missing one (source `catalog.ts:88-105`; native oracle cases `invalid-shape`/`malformed` and the missing/directory path tests); port: kept.
- `nativeEntries` trusts an injected native list without deduplicating it, and `buildCatalog` rejects only empty provider ids rather than blank ids, so native duplicates and a whitespace-only provider id survive (source `catalog.ts:112-121,144-151`; oracle cases `native-duplicates`/`whitespace`); port: kept.
- `buildCatalog` and `readNativeCatalog` give the same cache entry different labels and only the latter retains its effort ladder (source `catalog.ts:102,119-120`; oracle native/build rows); port: kept.

## CRW-488 — Render observation and artifact ledger

- Appending a row to an unterminated render-ledger tail joins both records and the reader drops the joined line (source `pabcd-state/src/render-observations.ts:71-77`); port: fixed by CRW-488 (one guarded append preserves the tail; intentionally-changed oracle case `tail`).
- State-directory and ledger links can direct append or reset outside the workspace, where reset truncates an external record (source `pabcd-state/src/render-observations.ts:67-77,126-132`); port: fixed by CRW-488 (workspace-confined opens preserve the external target; intentionally-changed ledger-link cases).
- A QA declaration can name an outside screenshot, including through a symlink, and establish native evidence from it (source `pabcd-state/src/render-observations.ts:191-217`); port: fixed by CRW-488 (workspace-confined reads; intentionally-changed oracle case `outside`, internal absolute and relative links retained).
- Failed observation calls still record an ordinary row, and a failed computer-use response still records a structured app signal (source `pabcd-state/src/render-observations.ts:250-275`); port: kept (the ledger records invocation and declared metadata, not successful semantic inspection).
- The row reader trims JavaScript whitespace before parsing, but the native malformed checker parses the untrimmed line, so a BOM-prefixed valid row is readable and also malformed (source `pabcd-state/src/render-observations.ts:89-115,168-175`); port: kept.

## Found by the PABCD phase directive and assembly port

No new oracle defect was identified in the directive text and assembly of `pabcd-state/src/hook.ts:212-235,324-575` (CXC v0.2.40, commit 3c1459ac). The prefix-only resolver's unterminated-backtick matching and whole-string fail-open behavior are preserved and recorded as edge cases, not changed into a Markdown parser. The goalplan reader and active-phase selector are outside this port.

## Found by the CRW-490 bg hooks port

- Stop, prompt delivery and drain stamp records before handing their output to the caller, so discarded output or a broken stdout loses the notification while the job record/output files remain intact (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/bg-wake/src/hook.ts:67-70,85-87,101-103`, `cli.ts:72-77`); port: kept.
- A read error or input over the 1Mi UTF-16-unit limit becomes an empty payload, which can still wake completions through the process cwd and `CODEX_THREAD_ID` fallback (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/bg-wake/src/cli.ts:38-47,62-68`, `hook.ts:34-41`); port: kept.
- SessionStart adopts every undelivered terminal record not owned by this session without checking whether a previous adopting session is still alive, so a second session can take the first session's adopted completion; adoption still runs while automatic wakes are off (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/bg-wake/src/hook.ts:114-119`, `registry.ts:235-250`); port: kept.
- Selection, delivery stamping and adoption are separate unlocked operations, so concurrent hooks can select or adopt the same completion before either stamps it; a delivery write that fails is still described by the hook and can wake again later (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/bg-wake/src/hook.ts:63-70,99-103`, `registry.ts:217-250`); port: kept.

## Found by the CRW-345 job verb port

- `MAX_STDIN_BYTES` counts UTF-16 units, and `readStdin` reads the whole input before applying the bound (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/bg-wake/src/cli.ts:38-45`); port: kept.
- `flagValue` takes the first occurrence and accepts another flag as its value (source `bg-wake/src/cli.ts:81-85`); port: kept in the argv facade; the approved relay parser validates options and uses its existing last-value rule.
- A note equal to `--` becomes the command separator, and `--json` inside the command selects record output (source `bg-wake/src/cli.ts:101-107`); port: kept in the argv facade; the approved relay contract passes parsed metadata separately and selects JSON only from declared flags.
- `get` counts the final empty line of a newline-terminated output toward its tail and trims whitespace from the displayed output (source `bg-wake/src/cli.ts:123-127`); port: kept.
- Manual drain returns the selected completions even when a record cannot be stamped, and stamps before the caller emits the result (source `bg-wake/src/hook.ts:81-91`, `cli.ts:170-175`); port: kept.
- A failed `on` can lose the persistent off flag: it removes `disabled` before the `enabled-at` write (source `bg-wake/src/cli.ts:145-154`; oracle reproduction makes `enabled-at` a directory); port: fixed by writing the gate before removing the flag, covered by `TestCLIOnRetainsTheOffFlagWhenGateWriteFails` and the intentionally-changed case `testdata/cli-on-gate-failure.json`.

## Found by the recall storage primitives port

- Version suffixes use JavaScript Number, so state_9007199254740993.sqlite ties state_9007199254740992.sqlite and the earlier sorted name wins (source plugins/codexclaw/components/recall/src/paths.ts:35-36; TestRecallVersionedDB); port: kept.
- A matching directory or symbolic link is eligible as the newest database, because the resolver checks only its name (source recall/src/paths.ts:32-38; TestRecallVersionedDBKeepsNonFilesAndLexicalJoin); port: kept.
- A home containing a symlink followed by .. lists the OS-resolved directory but path.join cleans the returned path lexically, potentially naming another directory's database (source recall/src/paths.ts:32,38; TestRecallVersionedDBKeepsNonFilesAndLexicalJoin); port: kept.
- URI mode=memory overrides a read-only SQLite open and permits writes to the temporary database (source recall/src/sqlite.ts:29-31; Node-recorded case and TestRecallSQLiteOpens); port: kept. Ordinary file paths and the empty/:memory: paths remain read-only.
- The legacy-column retry catches every query error, so an unsafe INTEGER in git_origin_url drops origin metadata silently when the shorter SELECT succeeds (source recall/src/threads-db.ts:41-49; TestRecallThreadMetaLegacyAndUnsafeFallback); port: kept.
- SQLite resolves column names without case sensitivity, but row keys keep their declared spelling; an ID column rather than id makes every metadata row disappear without warning (source recall/src/threads-db.ts:52; TestRecallThreadMetaWarningsAndCase); port: kept.
- node:sqlite accepts the minimum signed 64-bit INTEGER as a rounded JavaScript number, while neighboring unsafe integers throw, consistent with an absolute-value overflow in its range check (recorded Node case; recall/src/sqlite.ts:29-36 delegates to DatabaseSync; TestRecallSQLiteMinimumIntegerOracleOverflow); port: kept.
- An ambiguous bare named-parameter map throws on its first construction but leaves a partial per-statement alias cache, so repeating Get/All/Run can bind the first alias while the other remains NULL (recorded Node case; recall/src/sqlite.ts delegates to DatabaseSync; TestRecallSQLiteNamedOrderAndCachedAmbiguity); port: kept.
- Named SQLite binding keys containing NUL resolve by the prefix before NUL, while unknown-name diagnostics retain the original key (recorded Node case; recall/src/sqlite.ts delegates to DatabaseSync; TestRecallSQLiteNamedOrderAndCachedAmbiguity); port: kept.

## Found by the agent-thread TOML scanner port (CRW-343)

- A space-separated date-time is rejected by the bare-token scan despite being in the scalar grammar (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/agent-thread-permissions.ts:227,306-308`; recorded spaced date-time); port: kept.
- Year 0000 uses Date.UTC's year-1900 calendar, rejecting its leap day (source `agent-thread-permissions.ts:79`; recorded `0000-02-29`); port: kept.
- Second 60 is accepted at any minute and date, without checking whether a leap second occurred (source `agent-thread-permissions.ts:83`; recorded `12:00:60`); port: kept.
- Raw form feed passes all four string scanners, and raw carriage return passes the multiline forms (source `agent-thread-permissions.ts:246-247`; recorded control-character strings); port: kept.
- The first triple closing mark ends a string, so the TOML four/five-quote closing forms are rejected (source `agent-thread-permissions.ts:245`; recorded adjacent closing quotes); port: kept.
- JavaScript whitespace such as vertical tab, form feed and non-ASCII spaces is accepted between value tokens although TOML whitespace is narrower (source `agent-thread-permissions.ts:230`; recorded whitespace arrays); port: kept.
- A table implicitly created by a dotted assignment may later be explicitly declared, which TOML forbids (source `agent-thread-permissions.ts:182-188,212-216`; recorded dotted-assignment/table sequence); port: kept.

## Found by the skill-search library port (CRW-278)

- The cache catches write failures as well as fetch failures and calls both a network failure in its stale-cache warning (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/skill-search/src/cache.ts:47-60`; `TestCacheWriteFailureKeepsWholeFile`); port: kept.
- A future cache mtime is fresh until that time plus the TTL, because the age test has no lower bound (source `skill-search/src/cache.ts:40`; `TestCacheThresholdRefreshAndZero/future`); port: kept.
- The cache overwrites its file in place, so a failed partial write can truncate the previously usable cache (source `skill-search/src/cache.ts:50`); port: fixed by the assignment's temp-and-rename requirement through `crwdir.Publish`; write failure keeps the whole old file. The shared writer checks write/sync errors before rename; its fault tests exercise failures before publication steps, not a simulated short write.
- Remote bodies, entry paths and skill identifiers are accepted without a size/path boundary, and ClawHub keeps non-text display names/summaries (source `skill-search/src/sources.ts:21-38,59-77,87-108`); port: fixed by the assignment's bounded, strict untrusted-input requirement: 4 MiB bodies, typed collection envelopes and safe name/path components. Recorded `testdata/boundaries.json` cases are intentionally-changed with their reason; one refused row refuses the catalog. Transport read limits remain the injected fetcher's responsibility.
- Tied ranks use the host's default ICU locale, so ordering can change across hosts (source `skill-search/src/scoring.ts:49`); port: kept for ASCII root collation through the existing `metric.CompareTimestamps`. Non-ASCII ids are ordered by code point after ASCII, whereas ICU interleaves them; this is the existing platform limitation, not a full Unicode parity claim.
- `JSON.parse` keeps lone surrogate escapes where Go's default JSON reading replaces them with U+FFFD; the catalog has the same documented platform limitation as the other JSON ports (source `skill-search/src/sources.ts:21,89`; `pyjson.Loads` default reading); port: kept as a platform difference. Valid paired Unicode text is preserved.

## Found by the config-guard activation and manifest port (CRW-333)

- Backup/config/manifest content writes are not published atomically: interruption can leave a partial backup or truncated settings/records (source `config-guard/src/activate.ts:217`, `:278`, `:299`; intentionally-changed recorded case `atomic_config_publication_failure` in `testdata/activation-changes.json`, driven by `TestActivationIntentionallyChangedCases`); port: fixed (data loss: reuse fsynced temporary-file publication and rename; failed config publication leaves the old bytes whole, and backup staging preserves source permissions).
- `readPriorManifest` swallows a read failure and activation replaces the unreadable file, losing original managed-key restoration values (source `config-guard/src/activate.ts:154-162`, `:270`, `:282`, `:299`; intentionally-changed recorded case `unreadable_prior_manifest` in `testdata/activation-changes.json`, driven by `TestActivationIntentionallyChangedCases`); port: fixed (data loss: unreadable existing config, manifest and backup destinations are refused without overwriting them; readable malformed manifests remain absent as in the oracle).
- A hard feature-enable failure leaves earlier enabled flags and the backup but publishes no new install manifest, so an initial failed activation has no manifest-based revert record (source `config-guard/src/activate.ts:233-246`, `:299`; `TestActivateFailurePaths/hard`); port: kept.
- Repeated activation carries managed-key ownership and original values but recomputes flag prior state and ownership from live flags, so flags enabled by an earlier activation become recorded as pre-existing (source `config-guard/src/activate.ts:220-231`, `:279-286`; `TestActivateRerunKeepsManagedPriorAndOwnership`); port: kept.

## CRW-334: staged skill catalog formatting

- CXC:plugins/codexclaw/skills/dev/references/skill-catalog.md:2-273 retains 33 space-only lines with trailing whitespace. The staged catalog is the unedited name-substituted oracle copy; full-range git diff --check reports those lines, while the manual-edit diff is clean. This is formatting debt, not a runtime behavior change. port: kept.

## Found by the managed worktree detection and guidance hooks port (CRW-198)

- `canonicalize` cuts the missing rest of a path with `abs.slice(cur.length + 1)`, so when the nearest existing ancestor is the filesystem root it also drops the first character of the first missing segment (`/nonexistent` becomes `/onexistent`, a root `/x/worktrees` becomes `/worktrees`), and a ghost cwd or root can be taken for another real directory (source `pabcd-state/src/worktree-guard.ts:67-70`; `TestCanonicalize`, `TestMappedGhostCwdGetsNoMarker`); port: kept (detection follows it as the oracle does; the marker is only written under a cwd whose own real path exists, so a mapped directory is never written to). When the first character is astral, JavaScript leaves half of its surrogate pair and a Go string cannot hold that, so the port drops the whole character; port: kept as a platform difference.
- The once-per-session marker is named `sanitizeKey(session_id).json`, so session ids that sanitize alike (`a/b` and `a-b`) share one marker and the second session never gets the rename guidance (source `worktree-guard.ts:514`; `TestUserPromptSubmitRenameGuidance`, sanitize collision); port: kept.
- `markInjected` makes the marker directory and writes the marker with `mkdirSync` and `writeFileSync`, and `ensureCodexclawDir` writes `.codexclaw/.gitignore` the same way, all through whatever links the workspace holds: a link at `.codexclaw` or `worktree-guard` to a directory outside makes the hook create `worktree-guard/<session>.json` there, a dangling link at the marker makes it create the link's target, and a marker link swapped in after the `existsSync` check is truncated by the write (source `worktree-guard.ts:513-538` and `codexclaw-dir.ts:7-26`; checked with Node 24 on the recorded oracle build for the first two; `TestMarkerStaysInsideTheWorkspace`; no corpus fixture plants a link there); port: fixed by CRW-198 (a security and record-truncation defect: the marker's state directory is made, and the marker created exclusively, through an `os.Root` opened on the real path of the cwd; links that stay inside the workspace are followed, one that leaves it leaves the guidance without a marker).
- `ensureCodexclawDir` removes a state directory it just made when its `.gitignore` cannot be written, so the next run tries again; `ensureStateDir`, which makes the directory through an `os.Root` that has no directory-only removal, leaves it, and no later `EnsureDir` publishes the `.gitignore`, so files kept there show as untracked (source `codexclaw-dir.ts:18-24`; `TestEnsureStateDir`; the failure itself is not injected in a test); port: kept as a platform difference on an I/O-failure path (the marker is not written when the directory step fails, as the oracle's throw skips it).
- A managed cwd that does not exist, or a `.codexclaw` that is a file, makes the marker step fail silently, so the rename guidance repeats on every rename prompt of the session (source `worktree-guard.ts:525-538`, the swallowed `catch`; `TestUserPromptSubmitRenameGuidance`, ghost cwd; `TestMarkerStaysInsideTheWorkspace`, regular file); port: kept.
- `parseRaw` and the context carry a lone surrogate escape of the payload's cwd as JavaScript does, so `"\ud800"` is printed as the escape, while a Go string cannot hold one and the port reads and prints U+FFFD (source `worktree-guard.ts:505` and `:553`; `TestSessionStartContextOfALoneSurrogateCwd`); port: kept as a platform difference, the same family as the lines of the hook ingress library port.
- A payload nested deeper than Go's limit of 10,000 levels, in a field the hook never reads, is not JSON for `parseRaw`, so the hook stays silent where the oracle's `JSON.parse` reads it and injects the context (source `worktree-guard.ts:505`; `TestHandleWorktreeGuardIgnoresWhatItCannotRead`, nesting row; checked with Node 24); port: kept as a platform difference, on the silent side.
- `findCheckoutRoot` joins and cleans the path at every level on its way up, which is quadratic in the length of the cwd, so a cwd of tens of thousands of missing levels (about 65 KB of input, far under the hook input limit) holds the hook for seconds (source `worktree-guard.ts:87-100`; checked with Node 24: 8,192 levels took 1.0 s and 32,768 levels 14.8 s; `TestDetectManagedWorktree`, the 65,000 missing levels case); port: fixed with the same answers (no stat of a path over 4,096 bytes can succeed, so the ancestors that long are skipped).

## Found by the map and compact affordance port

- The cxc-ops hook ingress reads stdin without a size bound, unlike the PABCD and background-completion components, so a large input can consume unbounded memory; the shared invocation observation refuses input above its own bound while the affordance handler still runs (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/cxc-ops/src/cli.ts:19-25,116-133`; `TestIngressOwnUnboundedPolicyAndObservationName`); port: kept.
- Recovery checks `.codexclaw` and `affordance-recovery` with `lstat`, then creates or removes the marker by pathname, so a concurrent local directory swap can race the static symlink checks (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/cxc-ops/src/map-affordance.ts:242-255,263,274-276`; static symlink refusal is pinned by `TestRecoveryPathScopeSymlinksAndNonfiles`, not race-proof confinement); port: kept.
- SessionStart accepts an unbounded, unvalidated session ID and interpolates it into its binding text, so embedded newlines/backticks can alter the guidance and a long ID grows the envelope without truncation (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/cxc-ops/src/map-affordance.ts:158-169,306-308,320`; `TestSessionStartThresholdFallbackAndPointers` and the escaped binding golden); port: kept.

## Found by the CRW-502 recall repository-key port

- Local-looking colon paths such as `C:\repo` are accepted as scp remotes and identify `c/repo`, rather than falling back to cwd scope (source `recall/src/repo-key.ts:38-41`; recorded base oracle); port: kept.
- Decoding `%2F` before packing collapses an encoded path separator with a literal separator, so `a%2Fb` and `a/b` share a key (source `recall/src/repo-key.ts:46,56`; recorded base oracle); port: kept.
- Platform difference: the bounded URL host parser does not NFC-normalize decomposed Unicode labels before RFC3492 encoding (source `recall/src/repo-key.ts:44` delegates to Node WHATWG URL; recorded NFC grid); port: kept.
- Platform difference: UTS46 compatibility mappings, including long s and fullwidth letters, are not reproduced by lowercase plus RFC3492 (source `recall/src/repo-key.ts:44`; recorded compatibility grid); port: kept.
- Platform difference: UTS46 disallowed-code-point checks beyond the URL forbidden-domain set and strict UTF-8 decoding are not reproduced (source `recall/src/repo-key.ts:44`; recorded disallowed grid); port: kept.
- Platform difference: UTS46 Bidi checks are not reproduced (source `recall/src/repo-key.ts:44`; recorded Bidi grid); port: kept.
- Platform difference: UTS46 ContextJ joiner checks are not reproduced (source `recall/src/repo-key.ts:44`; recorded ContextJ grid); port: kept.
- Platform difference: existing `xn--` labels are passed through rather than decoded and re-validated; the recorded malformed ACE label is also passed through by this Node build, so this declared limit is latent in that row (source `recall/src/repo-key.ts:44`; recorded ACE grid); port: kept.

## Found by the agent-thread permission hook port (CRW-494)

- The config containment check treats project children whose names begin with `..` as outside the project, letting a project-controlled opt-in or full-access config grant permission (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/agent-thread-permissions.ts:98-99`; reproduced for both config readers, regression `TestAgentThreadBoundaries/intentionally-changed_project_child_starting_with_two_dots`); port: fixed (security: compare path components, preserving the literal default-at-home exception; oracle cases intentionally-changed).

## CRW-496 — skill-search CLI

- Unknown options (including `--help`) and dangling flags become query/id text rather than help or a flag error (`skill-search/src/cli.ts:32-43` at v0.2.40); port: kept.
- Catalog cache keys retain only the first 24 base64url URL characters, discarding the remainder of the URL identity (`skill-search/src/cli.ts:63-67` at v0.2.40); port: kept.
- Search source, gh launch/auth and malformed-gh-JSON failures can produce empty results with exit 0 (`skill-search/src/cli.ts:101-135,170-194` at v0.2.40); port: kept.
- `show --source gh` searches jaw, hermes and clawhub instead of GitHub, while unsupported show sources fall through to a no-skill message (`skill-search/src/cli.ts:204-229` at v0.2.40); port: kept.
- Show suppresses catalog errors, but a matching row's body-fetch failure stops fallback; fetched skill bodies have no size cap (`skill-search/src/cli.ts:209-220` at v0.2.40); port: kept.
- The default fetch transport reports only `fetch failed` for a network failure, discarding its cause (`skill-search/src/cli.ts:46` at v0.2.40, Node fetch transport); port: kept.

## Found by the config-guard deactivation and marker port (CRW-497)

- Managed-key restoration writes settings in place and an interrupted write can truncate them (source `config-guard/src/deactivate.ts:155`; `TestDeactivatePublicationFailureKeepsWholeSettings`); port: fixed (data loss: reuse protected fsynced temporary-file publication; failure leaves old settings whole and does not reach the feature CLI).
- With a null post-activation hash, an unreadable config is skipped and the later feature-disable CLI can overwrite settings that were never read (source `deactivate.ts:58-65,131-133,184`; intentionally-changed recorded cases `unreadable_config` and `unreadable_config_no_table_keys` in `testdata/deactivation-changes.json`, driven by `TestDeactivateUnreadableSettingsRefusedBeforeCLI`); port: fixed (data loss: refuse unreadable settings before any CLI call, including manifests without managed table keys).
- An unreadable self-heal marker is treated as absent and opt-out overwrites its healed-key consent and cache fields (source `config-guard/src/self-heal.ts:263-287`; intentionally-changed recorded case `unreadable_marker` in `testdata/deactivation-changes.json`, driven by `TestSelfHealMarkerUnreadablePreservesConsent`); port: fixed (data loss: direct marker functions return the read refusal and leave the record untouched).
- Marker publication uses a shared fixed .tmp name without fsync, so competing writes can overwrite staging records and interrupted publication can lose consent data (source `self-heal.ts:272-277`; `TestSelfHealMarkerAtomicPublication`); port: fixed (data loss: reuse the exclusive, fsynced temporary-file publisher; it preserves permission bits and follows existing links as the shared writer specifies, with no lock or directory-fsync guarantee).
- Manifest and backup read failures are reported as absent or unverifiable, without the read error (source `deactivate.ts:58-65,117-120,134-139`; `TestDeactivateEarlyReturnsAndV1`, `TestDeactivateOverrideMissingConfigAndBackup`); port: kept (neither record is overwritten).
- A failed feature-disable exit is omitted from disabled without a failure field, and marker failure is swallowed even on early uninstall returns (source `deactivate.ts:99-103,184-185`; `TestDeactivateFlagPathsAndOrdering`, `TestSelfHealMarkerUnreadablePreservesConsent`); port: kept.

## Found by the CRW-344 provider detection and map launcher port

- The provider dispatcher ignores every argument, including help and unknown verbs, and still probes status (source `bin/codexclaw.mjs:256-260,606-608`; `TestProviderMapCommandIngress` and the provider-help corpus cases); port: kept (this completes the earlier pending provider argument finding).
- A whitespace-only Python override is skipped by the preferred rung but is reused as a literal command by the fallback, and its truthiness suppresses bootstrap (source `bin/codexclaw.mjs:338,357,386`; `TestRepoMapLadderFromBin/blank-bare`, `TestRepoMapBootstrapWithFakesOnly/blank-suppresses-bootstrap`); port: kept.
- Explicit bootstrap can create a venv and invoke pip even for help, before help bypasses uv/the venv in selection (source `bin/codexclaw.mjs:386-406`; `TestRepoMapBootstrapWithFakesOnly/help-still-bootstraps`); port: kept.
- A failed pip run removes the whole rebuildable venv directory, including pre-existing partial cache contents when its interpreter was absent (source `bin/codexclaw.mjs:383,386,397-399`; `TestRepoMapBootstrapWithFakesOnly/pip-fails`); port: kept (the cache is disposable; no settings, state or record path is written by the launcher).
- The uv availability probe always runs, even for help or an explicit Python override, and has no timeout (source `bin/codexclaw.mjs:404-406`; the map ladder/ingress tests and replayed calls); port: kept.
- Any numeric listen.port is accepted, including negative/fractional values and overflow to Infinity, which renders as null; no valid-port range is checked (source `provider-bridge/src/detect.ts:54`; Node-recorded `testdata/oracle.jsonl`, `TestDetectNodeRecordedStatusCases`); port: kept.

## CRW-501 — Helper role live catalog

- Cache validation does not validate `state`, accepts blank entry IDs/labels and arbitrary string effort values, and echoes unknown members (source `subagent-config/src/live-catalog.ts:64-68,112`; `TestLiveCatalogValidation`); port: kept.
- The source key omits the resolved native home, so changing `HOME` with an explicit unchanged catalog home and absent `CODEX_HOME` can reuse a list from the old native home (source `live-catalog.ts:55-58,86-93`; `TestLiveCatalogSourceIdentityAndClock`); port: kept.
- The 30-second TTL only limits fresh reuse; a validated last-success list is returned stale without an age limit while discovery keeps failing (source `live-catalog.ts:92-93,110-113`; `TestLiveCatalogCache`); port: kept.
- `fetchedAt` is declared a string but cache validation coerces truthy numbers through `Date.parse`, which also normalizes February days 30/31 and `24:00` (source `live-catalog.ts:15,65`; `TestLiveCatalogValidation/numeric-date` and `TestLiveCatalogOracleDates`); port: kept.

The pinned oracle already publishes through an exclusive 0600 temporary file and rename (`live-catalog.ts:71-79`). This port preserves that invariant: the forced-rename failure case retains the previous bytes and removes its own temporary file. No new truncation defect or intentionally-changed fix is claimed.

## Found by the CRW-487 recall rollout port

- Listing checks only the `.jsonl` suffix, so directories and links can be listed as rollout files; reading a listed directory then throws, and day/archive enumeration errors escape the whole listing while `safeDirs` swallows its own errors (source `recall/src/rollout.ts:159-160,169-174,190-197,235-250`; recorded listing oracle and `TestRolloutHeadReadAndFailures`); port: kept.
- Symlinked year/month/day directories are skipped by `isDirectory`, although the sessions root is followed (source `recall/src/rollout.ts:152-156,192-194`; recorded listing oracle); port: kept.
- Directory dates and archive filename dates are compared without calendar validation, so invalid dates sort and prune lexically; positive days outside the Date range produces `NaN-NaN-NaN` and prunes every file (source `recall/src/rollout.ts:135-137,148,156-157,173`; recorded listing oracle and `TestRolloutLocalMidnightAndInvalidDays`); port: kept.
- A metadata first line beyond 1 MiB is truncated for parsing and falls back to all-null main metadata, even when the complete JSON would identify a subagent (source `recall/src/rollout.ts:203-228,238-247`; recorded metadata oracle); port: kept.
- A present `source.subagent` key classifies a session as subagent even when its value is null or false (source `recall/src/rollout.ts:215`; recorded metadata oracle); port: kept.
- A function call with empty name and arguments emits an empty tool-log entry, although empty messages and outputs are skipped (source `recall/src/rollout.ts:310,318-325`; recorded parser oracle); port: kept.
- A text/name/arguments object with its own non-callable `toString` makes String coercion throw outside the JSON catch, aborting the entire parse rather than returning earlier entries (source `recall/src/rollout.ts:272,295-299,307,319`; recorded parser oracle and `TestRolloutKeptParserDefects`); port: kept.
- The literal response-item prefilter skips valid JSON with an escaped type such as `response_\u0069tem`, and the whole-file prefilter can miss a decoded message whose text is JSON-escaped, such as `\u0043I` for `CI` (source `recall/src/rollout.ts:262-265,293`; recorded parser/prefilter oracle and `TestRolloutKeptParserDefects`); port: kept.
- Platform differences inherited from the Go JSON boundary: a lone UTF-16 surrogate escape becomes U+FFFD and a line nested beyond 10,000 levels is skipped, where Node preserves the surrogate and parses the deep line (source `recall/src/rollout.ts:296,307`; Node 24 probes and `TestRolloutJSONPlatformBoundaries`); port: kept as platform differences, outside the well-formed-string and bounded-JSON parity domain.
- Platform difference: coercing a value of 8,000 nested arrays exhausts the pinned Node/V8 call stack and aborts the whole parse, even after earlier entries, while Go can stringify that valid JSON and return both entries; the precise V8 stack budget depends on the engine/call context and is not emulated with an arbitrary depth cap (source `recall/src/rollout.ts:307,319` and JavaScript Array string coercion; Node 24 recorded `RangeError` cases in `testdata/rollout/platform-limits.json`, classified intentionally-changed with reason, and `TestRolloutCoercionStackPlatformDifference`); port: kept as an engine stack-limit difference, outside the parity domain.

## CRW-335: staged development-router limitations

- The architecture decision-tree line has a trailing space, and the design-trends snapshot uses two Markdown hard-break lines; the tool-staged copies keep those bytes, so the full-range whitespace check reports three lines (CXC:plugins/codexclaw/skills/dev-architecture/SKILL.md:344; CXC:plugins/codexclaw/skills/dev-uiux-design/references/design-trends.md:13-14); port: kept.
- AI-review guidance calls the AI stage a deterministic gate, then says unavailable or failed AI review is additive and may be bypassed by human review; repository/user review authority must settle the conflicting wording (CXC:plugins/codexclaw/skills/dev-code-reviewer/references/ai-assisted-review.md:12-32); port: kept.
- The oracle describes worktree GC/list as a future contract rather than an implemented command; staging the renamed examples does not provide that implementation (CXC:plugins/codexclaw/skills/dev-devops/references/local-gc.md:87-105); port: kept.
- Historical oracle references name a source-only search-policy test, repository-bootstrap workflow and CodexClaw issue; name substitution preserves the references without importing those upstream artifacts or proving their CRW counterparts exist (CXC:plugins/codexclaw/skills/dev-code-reviewer/SKILL.md:380-384; CXC:plugins/codexclaw/skills/dev-devops/references/repo-bootstrap.md:23; CXC:plugins/codexclaw/skills/dev-devops/references/native-desktop-acceptance.md:188-189,267); port: kept.
- Four upstream references have an extra blank line at EOF; staging preserves it, so the full-range whitespace check reports four additional locations (CXC:plugins/codexclaw/skills/dev-frontend/references/core/product-density.md:53; CXC:plugins/codexclaw/skills/dev-frontend/references/core/soft-3d-asset-gates.md:70; CXC:plugins/codexclaw/skills/dev-frontend/references/core/ux-writing-ko.md:62; CXC:plugins/codexclaw/skills/dev-uiux-design/references/design-system-bootstrap.md:133); port: kept.

## Found by the orchestrate argument library port (CRW-359)

- The CLI verb table is a plain object, so `constructor` and `__proto__` (including uppercase spellings) find inherited function/object values instead of an unknown-verb error; the port retains the two values as `fsm.VerbConstructor` and `cli.VerbProto` with JavaScript interpolation in `cli.VerbText` (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/orchestrate-cli.ts:143-145`, `:228-230`; recorded argv and hint cases); port: kept.
- A help token anywhere in argv, even the value of `--session`, returns help before other flags are parsed and keeps the input cwd, so a session named `help` cannot be selected through this parser (source `orchestrate-cli.ts:169-170`, `:226`; recorded help/value cases); port: kept.
- Unknown flags and `--flag=value` forms are ignored, while a value flag consumes the next token even if it is another flag; a missing `--session` value is undefined and a missing `--cwd` value falls back to the input cwd without a parse error (source `orchestrate-cli.ts:247-270`; recorded ignored/eaten/missing flag cases); port: kept.
- Duplicate `--session` and `--cwd` flags use their first occurrence on an unknown verb, but their last occurrence on a recognised verb, so diagnostic context can point at a different session/directory than the command path would select (source `orchestrate-cli.ts:173-175`, `:233-234`, `:265-268`; recorded first/last cases); port: kept.
- A valid later `--attest` does not clear an earlier parse error, and a bad later value does not clear a prior coerced attest; a valid attest file likewise leaves a prior missing-file-argument error set, so the result can contain both evidence and an error and the caller refuses it (source `orchestrate-cli.ts:251-258`, `:261`, `:285-286`; recorded duplicate/stale-error cases); port: kept.

## Found by the CRW-510 recall memory-status port

- Required-column checks do not validate timestamp values: nonnumeric TEXT/BLOB maxima become NaN while state remains `ok`, serialize as null, render `NaNd ago`, and never trigger the stale notice (source `recall/src/memory-status.ts:134-157,181-186,224-225`; recorded scalar cases and `TestMemoryStatusOracle`); port: kept.
- Every jobs-read failure is called an unsupported store schema by the notice, including corruption and an INTEGER too large for Node's number representation; partial counts are discarded (source `recall/src/memory-status.ts:170-171,217-218`; recorded corrupt/unsafe-integer cases and `TestMemoryStatusOracle`); port: kept.

## Found by the CRW-498 managed-worktree deletion guard port

- The guard cuts a command into segments only at `&&`, `||`, `;` and `|`, so a newline, a single `&`, a brace group and a subshell do not cut; a segment that starts with `cd` is skipped whole (the `cd` branch continues before the destructive-verb test), so `cd /tmp⏎rm -rf <slot>` is allowed even with the slot path spelled out, and a removal after `&`, in `(...)` or in `{ ...; }` is invisible to the verb branch (source `pabcd-state/src/worktree-guard.ts:191-218` and `:463-469`; checked with Node 24 on the oracle; `TestExtendedWalkAddsDenies`); port: fixed (security class: a command shape the oracle allows that deletes a protected worktree; a second walk runs when the oracle's walk allows, cuts at a newline, a single `&` and braces, scopes parentheses so a subshell's `cd` does not leak, and joins a backslash-newline pair; the oracle's walk and its reasons are unchanged; the walk also denies a removal line inside a heredoc body or a function definition body, accepted as false positives because the guard cannot tell that the text is not run, or is run later).
- The destructive-verb hint accepts only whitespace or `/` before the verb, so a verb that follows a quote, a parenthesis, a brace or a backtick never arms the slot-mention fallback: `bash -c "rm -rf <slot>"` is allowed with the slot path spelled out (source `pabcd-state/src/worktree-guard.ts:448-451` and `:474-483`; checked with Node 24; `TestExtendedWalkAddsDenies`); port: fixed (security class; the second walk widens the lead set, which also denies `echo "rm -rf <slot>"`, accepted as a false positive).
- The ancestor test is `cwd.startsWith(resolved + sep)`, which for the filesystem root is `startsWith("//")` and never holds, so `rm -rf --no-preserve-root /` and `rmdir /` are allowed although the root is an ancestor of every cwd (source `pabcd-state/src/worktree-guard.ts:287`; `TestIsProtectedTarget`); port: fixed (security class; the second walk compares against `TrimSuffix(resolved, "/") + "/"`, the oracle's walk keeps its own test and reasons).
- Windows removal verbs (`rd`, `Remove-Item`, `del`, `erase`, `ri`) are not ported, by the rework decision (POSIX removal verbs only), so `rd .` and `Remove-Item -Recurse .`, which the oracle denies with the verb's reason, are allowed unless the command spells out the slot, the worktrees root or the slot id, and then the fallback denies them with the unresolvable-target reason (fixture `hook__pre-tool-use-guarding-managed-worktree-deletion__windows_verbs_on_posix`, steps 0 and 1 intentionally changed; `TestFallbackForUnresolvableTargets`); port: kept as a decision, not a repair.
- Prefix handling understands only `sudo`, `command`, `builtin` and `env NAME=value`; any option (`sudo -n`, `sudo -u root`, `env -i`, `env -u X`) or other wrapper (`nice`, `nohup`, `time`, `timeout`, `xargs`) hides the verb, so `sudo -n rm -rf .` is allowed while the same command with the slot path spelled out is denied by the fallback (source `pabcd-state/src/worktree-guard.ts:263-278`; checked with Node 24); port: kept.
- The guard has no shell semantics: a nested shell with a relative target (`bash -c 'rm -rf .'`), a target held in a variable or command substitution (`$PWD`, `$(pwd)`), a glob, `find . -delete`, `mv` and `git worktree move` of the checkout are not seen, and only a literal mention of the slot, the worktrees root or the slot id reaches the fallback (source `pabcd-state/src/worktree-guard.ts:453-485`; checked with Node 24); port: kept.
- `git` global options other than the exact tokens `-C <dir>` and `-c <kv>` (`--no-pager`, `--git-dir=...`, `-C<dir>`) leave `worktree` out of first place and out of the hint's `git (-C dir)? worktree remove`, so `git --no-pager worktree remove <checkout>` is allowed; and `rm` knows only the exact `--recursive`, not the unambiguous abbreviations `getopt_long` accepts (`--rec`) (source `pabcd-state/src/worktree-guard.ts:376` and `:420-445`); port: kept.
- A target is compared with the segment's effective directory (after `cd` or `git -C`), not with the session's: `cd /tmp && rm -rf .` and `git -C /tmp worktree remove .` are denied although `/tmp` is not the worktree, and the protected set moves with every `cd` (source `pabcd-state/src/worktree-guard.ts:280-287`; checked with Node 24; `TestEffectiveDirectoryIsWhatIsCompared`); port: kept.
- The guard fails open on input it cannot parse, and the shared `parseRaw` refuses a document nested deeper than Go's 10,000 levels, in a field the hook never reads, where the oracle's `JSON.parse` reads it and denies; unreachable from a command string (the host builds the payload, a JSON string cannot add structure), and the same platform difference as the detection hooks' line above (source `pabcd-state/src/worktree-guard.ts:503-509`); port: kept as a platform difference, on the silent side.
- A lone surrogate escape in the command is read as U+FFFD by Go and so printed in the deny reason as U+FFFD, where the oracle's `JSON.stringify` prints the escape back (source `pabcd-state/src/worktree-guard.ts:498` and `:568-594`); port: kept as a platform difference, the same family as the lone surrogate line of the detection port above.
- The oversize exit 1 and the slot-id substring fallback seeded above (fixtures `hook__pre-tool-use-guarding-managed-worktree-deletion__oversized_stdin_exits_1` and `..._slot_substring_denies_unrelated_target`) are reproduced by this port, the first by the shared ingress (a claim of the hook entry-point issue) and the second by the fallback in `internal/pabcd/hook/worktreedel.go`; both stay `port: kept`, their seeded lines unchanged.

## Found by the recall index database port (CRW-350)

- A schema-version reset deletes the non-rebuildable injection history together with the derived cache (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/recall/src/index-db.ts:109`); port: fixed, keep `recall_hit_counts` when rebuilding the derived tables.
