- A FIFO at the skill-file path can block the oracle's synchronous read without a deadline; the port rejects an observed nonregular file but a concurrent regular-to-FIFO replacement can still block before the opened-file type check (source `subagent-config/src/spawn-attach-hook.ts:652,819`; rooted reads preserve containment); port: kept (availability limitation).

## Found by the SubagentStop evidence gate port

- An internal gate error records `MAX_ATTEMPTS` in its terminal verdict regardless of the persisted counter, so an error before the first block looks like an exhausted budget (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/subagent-evidence.ts:521-529`; `TestSubagentStopInternalErrorAndDirectPolicy` injects a failing environment read and observes attempts 3 with counter 0); port: kept.
- A valid receipt releases even when counter deletion or verdict resolution fails, leaving the parent denied by the outstanding budget or tombstone until storage recovers and resolution is retried (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/subagent-evidence.ts:488-495`; `TestSubagentStopReceiptRecoveryIOFailures` holds the session lock or denies counter-directory deletion); port: kept (the review finding is kind bug, with no security or record-loss finding).

## CRW-565 recall rebuild transaction

- `chat index --rebuild` clears the derived index with `DELETE FROM msgs; DELETE FROM files;` as one `exec`, so each statement autocommits and a failure of the second delete leaves msgs empty while files keeps every fingerprint: the command exits 1 with `chat index failed: <reason>`, and every later plain `chat index` skips all files by fingerprint, so the derived index silently loses messages it never restores (source `recall/src/cli.ts:275-277` at v0.2.40); port: fixed, intentionally-changed by the assignment (data-loss exception): both deletes run in one transaction, a failure rolls both back and leaves every msgs and files row as it was, with the same `chat index failed:` prefix and exit 1; the reason is the original one, and a trigger raising ROLLBACK makes SQLite end the transaction itself, so the transaction's own ROLLBACK finds nothing to roll back: that rollback failure is dropped and the original reason is kept, while any other rollback failure is appended after it (CRW-596). A failure of the ingest that follows a committed rebuild still leaves a partial index, as in the oracle.

## Found by the review-round argument port

- `collectPlanFiles` and `recomputed` judge a plan file by the spelling of its path, so a link inside the workspace that points outside it, a plan unit that is such a link, or a unit outside the working directory makes them read and hash a file outside the workspace (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/review-round-cli.ts:141-165` and `:308-317`; the revival of a round's plan files drops absolute and `..` paths but leaves a link to the reader of the path, a review finding of kind security); port: fixed, both read an entry only when its real path, links followed, lies inside the real working directory and treat any other as missing, and read it through the working directory opened once as an `os.Root`, so a link swapped in between the check and the read, or the directory itself or an ancestor replaced by a link after the open, cannot lead the read out either (a review finding of kind security), and the tests tag three collect layouts and six recomputed entries as intentionally changed (`internal/pabcd/cli/review_round_args_test.go`).
- `collectPlanFiles` throws a raw read error for an existing plan file it cannot read, which the CLI reports as a generic failure instead of the "is not a readable regular file" refusal written for that case (source `review-round-cli.ts:161`); port: kept, the Go function returns it as an error value and not as a refusal.
- The aggregate hash's comment says it is the construction `freeze.ts` uses, but `planFilesHash` hashes "path, NUL, sha256" joined by NUL in the order given, while `computePlanHash` hashes the files' sha256 values alone, sorted by `localeCompare`, so a stale check built on one cannot read the other's value (source `review-round-cli.ts:167-170`, `freeze.ts:50-57`); port: kept.
- `renderOpenPacket` chooses its dispatch sentence by `v2SpawnSurface()` but both branches are the same text, so the config the probe reads never changes the packet, although the function's comment says the dispatch text adapts to a v1 caller (source `review-round-cli.ts:196-200`); port: kept.
- The numbered-document check says a plan path must be "directly inside" the plan unit, but `NUMBERED_DOC_RE` accepts a document in a subdirectory such as `000_a/b.md` (source `review-round-cli.ts:34` and `:152-154`); port: kept.
- `parseReviewRoundCliArgs` skips an unknown flag or stray token silently, so a mistyped `--plan_path` or `--sesion` is ignored and the run fails later on a missing value instead of naming the flag (source `review-round-cli.ts:115-124`); port: kept.
- `collectPlanFiles` stores each key relative to the working directory as spelled, so with a working directory that is a link and an entry spelled by its physical path the key climbs out (`../ws/devlog/_plan/u/000_plan.md`), which the goalplan revival drops together with the whole list of the round (source `review-round-cli.ts:158`); port: fixed, such a key is stored as the entry's own spelling relative to the real working directory, which keeps the links of the plan unit the round is bound to (the real path of the file only when that spelling climbs out too), and the tests tag one collect layout as intentionally changed.
- A plan path through a link inside the bound unit that names a directory elsewhere in the workspace is accepted although its file lies outside the unit, because the unit check compares spellings only and this port's real-path rule is against the working directory (source `review-round-cli.ts:147-153`); port: kept.
- `recomputed` and the collection read a plan file with a plain read, so a plan file replaced by a FIFO after the round opened blocks the read for good, where the unreadable-file path would have answered "missing" in `recomputed` (the collection refuses a non-regular file first; source `review-round-cli.ts:161` and `:312`); port: kept.

## CRW-563 recall relative paths against the physical working directory

- The port resolved relative recall paths with `filepath.Abs` and `os.Getwd`, which honour a logical `$PWD`, while the oracle's `path.resolve` joins `process.cwd()`, the physical directory, so from a directory reached through a symlink a `..` segment named another directory: `memory status --home <relative>`, `memory requeue --apply --home <relative>` (which could change the jobs of a different store), a relative `CODEX_HOME` and the cwd the three recall hook rows use when the payload has none each followed the logical path (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/recall/src/cli.ts:313,344,404,410` and `paths.ts:16`); port: fixed (a divergence of the port, not of the oracle: no recorded oracle case changes; `RecallPhysicalAbs` joins the symlink-resolved working directory with the caller's own, unresolved path, as `memorySearchBuildCwdScope` already did).
- The CRW-563 fix took the physical working directory with `os.Getwd` followed by `filepath.EvalSymlinks`, which lstat-walks every ancestor, so a process already inside a directory whose ancestor has lost search permission got an error from `RecallPhysicalAbs` (a relative `CODEX_HOME` failed, the cwd of the three recall hook rows fell back to empty and `memory search --cwd <relative>` failed) where the oracle's `process.cwd()` is the kernel getcwd and answers (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/recall/src/paths.ts:16`, `cli.ts:313,344,404,410` and `memory-search.ts:309-315`; test `TestRecallPhysicalAncestorWithoutSearch`, no recorded case); port: fixed (a divergence of the port, not of the oracle: `RecallPhysicalAbs` takes the directory from `syscall.Getwd`, the kernel getcwd, and `memorySearchBuildCwdScope` calls it, so the caller's own path is still joined unresolved and no recorded oracle case changes).

## Found by the memory write gate root fix (CRW-557)

- The protected memories root is cleaned lexically (`resolve(join(home, "memories"))`), so a `CODEX_HOME` or home that holds a symlink followed by `..` names the sibling of the link's own directory, while the open follows the link and reaches the memories directory under the directory the link leads from; a Write, apply_patch or shell redirection to that directory is allowed without a grant (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/memory-write-gate.ts:112-115`, compared in `isMemoryPath` at `:122-126`; tests `TestMemoryGateProtectsTheRootBehindALinkAndDotDot` and `TestMemoryGateRelativeRootKeepsItsTextAndFollowsItsLinks`, no recorded case); port: fixed (security: the root stays uncleaned and is read two ways, as its cleaned text and as the place an open of it reaches, and a destination whose text path or followed path, with or without its last entry followed, lies in either root is a memory write; the deny text is unchanged).

## Found by the CRW-556 state rewrite guard

Source: `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/state.ts` at v0.2.40, abbreviated below as the reader, and `memory-write-gate.ts` (the gate). No recorded oracle case holds a stored record the reader changes, so none is tagged; the cases are the new Go tests named per line.

- The guard of the memory gate and of the commands that write a rebuilt state back (`memory allow-write`, `scan record`, `evidence resolve`) compared only the number of stored unverified-subagent records with the number the reader kept, so a record the reader changes was accepted and written back changed: a `receiptClaimed` of 257 characters was cut to 256, a field of the wrong type (`attempts` as text) was replaced by its default, and an `attempts` that the writer prints as another number (`9007199254740993` or `1000000000000000128`) lost its value (the reader :57-95 through the gate :272 and :284; tests `TestMemoryGateRefusesToRewriteARecordTheReaderChanged`, `TestCLIRefusesToRewriteARecordTheReaderChanged` and `TestRewriteKeepsUnverifiedJudgesEveryHandledField`); port: fixed (data loss: `state.RewriteKeepsUnverified` compares every handled field of every stored record with the rebuilt one, and the gate denies and keeps the authorization, the commands exit 1, with the file untouched).
- The same count-only comparison remains in the evidence package: `storedVerdicts` guards the `resolveTombstone` write-back by count alone, so a stored record that the reader cuts or retypes is rewritten changed when a verdict is resolved, and `RecordTombstone` writes the rebuilt list back with no guard at all, so a record the reader cuts, retypes or drops is rewritten changed when a verdict is recorded (`internal/pabcd/evidence/budget.go` and `tombstone.go`; found by the architect check of CRW-556, outside its edit region); port: pending (a follow-up issue should call `state.RewriteKeepsUnverified` there).
- A lone surrogate escape in a stored receipt (written by the Node oracle) reads as U+FFFD on both sides, so the guard sees no difference and the rewrite writes U+FFFD in its place; unknown keys of a record are dropped on a rewrite as they are everywhere in the session file and are not judged (the package comment of `internal/pabcd/state`); port: fixed for the lone surrogate (data loss: `state.RewriteKeepsUnverified` now refuses before decoding, see the section on the CRW-609 lossless rewrite guard below), kept for the unknown keys.
## Found by the review-round id fix (CRW-573)

- Replacing a changed round replaces every list entry that has its `roundId`, whatever the purpose or launch id, so in a plan that holds one id twice a launch, verdict, abort or hash refresh overwrites the other round with a copy of the changed one (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/review-round.ts:93-94`, used at `:139`, `:228` and `:351`); port: fixed (CRW-573, data loss: the replacement targets the one entry the transition selected, found by its position in the list, so entries that repeat the id, the purpose and even the launch id are kept apart).
- Reviving a plan keeps any non-empty `roundId`, including one that repeats another round's id and one at 2^53 or above, so a hand-edited or damaged plan can hold ids the two entries above describe; rounds on such a plan are still found and ordered by first match (`reviewRoundOrder` ties at 2^53 and above, `RoundByLaunchID`, `StalenessOf`, the final gate's lookup by round id) (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/goalplan.ts:369` and `:1828`; Go revival `internal/pabcd/goalplan/revive.go` keeps them too); port: kept (CRW-573 only stops opening and changing rounds from losing a stored round on such a plan).
## Found by the CRW-544 review-round runner port

- `open` and `abort` read the goalplan through revival, which drops a review round it cannot read (a non-object entry, a missing or empty `roundId`, an unknown `purpose` or `status`, a missing `planPath` or `planSha256`, or a malformed `lane`), and then write the revived plan back, so the next write deletes that round from the file without a word (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/review-round-cli.ts:230-267` and `:278-283` over `goalplan.ts:363-394`; recorded cases `open_drops_an_unreadable_round` and `abort_drops_an_unreadable_round` in `internal/pabcd/cli/testdata/review_round_run/oracle.json`); port: fixed (CRW-544, a record data-loss defect, fixed by decision under the parity rule revision of 2026-10-03: `RunReviewRoundCli` counts the rounds stored in the file as the reader decodes it against the revived ones, under the lock and before any change, and refuses with `the goalplan holds review rounds that this build cannot read; refusing to rewrite it` and code 1, leaving the file as it is; `show` only reads and still answers; the two recorded cases are replayed as intentionally changed, `reviewRoundRunChanged` in `review_round_run_test.go`). The other things revival drops on the same write (tasks without `id` or `title`, unknown keys) are recorded in the goalplan sections above as port: kept and belong to the goalplan package, which every goalplan writer shares.
- `open` stamps the session binding (owner, work-phase, unit, epoch, plan files) on the opened round by `roundId` alone, so a plan that holds another entry with the same id (a `final_gate` entry with the same number, a hand edit, or the id repeat recorded in the review sections above) gets the binding on every such entry and loses the other's own (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/review-round-cli.ts:246`); port: fixed (CRW-544, a record data-loss defect, fixed by decision under the parity rule revision of 2026-10-03: `reviewRoundRunBound` changes the single entry the library selects by purpose and launch id and leaves the plan it was given untouched, `TestReviewRoundRunBindsOnlyTheSelectedRound`; the review package's own replacement by id is fixed there by PR #545).
- `open` writes the goalplan and only then renders the packet, whose multi_agent_v2 probe can throw (the home directory cannot be resolved), so the command fails after the round is already in flight and the launch id, which only the packet prints, is never shown (source `review-round-cli.ts:267-268` against `:67-90`; `TestReviewRoundRunPacketErrorComesAfterTheWrite`); port: kept (`RunReviewRoundCli` returns the error after the write; `show` reads the launch id back).
- `open` that refreshes an older pending round of the same document while a newer round exists is refused with `round r1 was superseded before this verdict arrived`, a sentence about a verdict from a command that records none, and the cursor stays on the stale round until `abort` closes it (source `review-round-cli.ts:259` over `review-round.ts:137-141` and `:197-212`, the cursor defect recorded above; `TestReviewRoundRunRefusesAStaleReusedRound`); port: kept.
- `abort` and `show` act on the live or latest `plan_audit` round of the bound goalplan whoever opened it: `open` stores `ownerSessionId`, and neither reads it back, so a second session bound to the same goalplan closes the first one's round (source `review-round-cli.ts:243-252` against `:278-289`); port: kept.
- `open` reads the session state at the top and takes the goalplan lock later, with no session lock between, so a re-plan that mints a new `planEpoch` in between binds the new round to the old epoch (source `review-round-cli.ts:216` against `:226-252`; read from the source, the race is not exercised); port: kept.
## Found by the CRW-593 rewrite guard callers

Source: `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/subagent-evidence.ts` (`recordTombstone` :272-322, `resolveTombstone` :434-446) and `idle-edit.ts` :85-110 at v0.2.40, through the same reader as the CRW-556 section. No recorded oracle case holds a stored record the reader changes, so none is tagged; the cases are the new Go tests named per line.

- `recordTombstone` (both tiers), `resolveTombstone` and the idle-edit counter write each read the session file with the strict reader and write back what it rebuilt, and the reader keeps 64 unverified-subagent records, cuts each `receiptClaimed` to 256 UTF-16 units and defaults a field of the wrong type, so after the tombstones of 66 agents the file held the first 64 and the 66th and the 65th was gone, and a receipt of 257 characters or an `attempts` stored as text was written back changed (tests `TestRewriteGuardKeepsThe65thVerdictWhenThe66thArrives`, `TestRewriteGuardRefusesAListTheReaderChanges`, `TestRewriteGuardSentinelTierRefuses`, `TestIdleEditSkipsTheCounterOverARecordTheReaderChanges` and `TestIdleEditRefusesARecordStoredMeanwhile`); port: fixed (data loss: each call asks `state.RewriteKeepsUnverified`; `RecordTombstone` writes neither tier and sends the verdict to the marker, returning false, `ResolveTombstone` returns false and the counter write is skipped, each leaving the file byte for byte as it was; this replaces the `port: pending` line about the evidence package in the CRW-556 section, which is left as written).
- The same writes still rewrite a session whose D-close recovery marker is `legacy`: the marker is persisted as an explicit `nextWorkPhaseId: null`, the defect recorded under "Found by the session state port"; the guard added here judges the unverified-subagent list only, and the idle-edit counter and the memory gate already refuse such a state while the two evidence writes do not (source `state.ts:440-481`); port: fixed by CRW-648 (the shared reader now restores the stored `legacy` flag, so `RecordTombstone` and `ResolveTombstone` keep the marker's distinction instead of dropping it; tests `TestRecordTombstoneKeepsALegacyDcloseMarker` and `TestResolveTombstoneKeepsALegacyDcloseMarker`).

## Found by the shell write escape port (CRW-583)

The same fail-closed rule as the CRW-517 section applies: a destination the oracle misses that a write reaches is a security finding, so the port appends it after the oracle's answer (the oracle's answer is kept first and unchanged). The recorded case `python3 -c "open('/m/a\nb','w')"` is tagged intentionally-changed in `internal/pabcd/hook/testdata/shellwrite/verbs-oracle.json`.

- A string literal written with escapes is named letter for letter, so a path hidden by an escape is never named: `open("\x2fm/a", "w")`, `open("\057m/a", "w")`, `Path("\u002fm/a").write_text(...)` and node's `writeFileSync("\x2fm/a", ...)`, `writeFileSync("\u{2f}m/a", ...)` and template literals report `\x2fm/a` and so on while the write reaches `/m/a` (source `shell-write-destinations.ts:535-549`); port: fixed (security: a Python literal is decoded as Python reads it, with line continuation, the one-character escapes, octal, `\xhh` and, in a str literal, `\uXXXX` and `\UXXXXXXXX`; an unknown escape keeps its backslash, a raw literal stays literal, and a literal Python rejects or whose value holds NUL has no decoded path; an open call (each reading with its own mode) and the first literal argument of a Path call with a single argument or a dynamic part are named in the earlier reading too, which kept every escape but a backslash before the quote or another backslash as written, so the decoded reading adds to what was named and never replaces it; a JavaScript string or template literal is read as JavaScript reads it and its decoded value is appended when it differs from the raw text, and a template with an unescaped placeholder has no decoded value; the oracle's answer is kept first).
- A `\N{name}` escape is valid Python that the reading does not resolve, because it needs the Unicode name table, so `open("\N{SOLIDUS}m/a", "w")` names `\N{SOLIDUS}m/a` and not `/m/a` (source `shell-write-destinations.ts:535-549`); port: kept (follow-up). A mode written with such an escape counts as writing (it cannot be read, so the gate fails closed), and the earlier reading of the path is still named.
- A `Path` built with the `/` operator (`Path("/m") / "a"`) names no path, because the call pattern takes the one string literal inside `Path(...)` (source `shell-write-destinations.ts:535-549`); port: kept (follow-up).
- A Python literal written with three of its quote (a triple-quoted literal) is no literal at all to the reading, which closes on the first of the three and then refuses the junk after it, so the path it names is missed: `Path("/tmp", """review""", "memories", "a").write_text("x")` reaches `/tmp/review/memories/a` and `open("""\x2fm/a""","w")` reaches `/m/a`, while the reading named only `/tmp` and nothing (source `shell-write-destinations.ts:535-549`; the oracle's `(['"])(.*?)\1` body cannot span the quote run); port: fixed (security: in the decoded reading a literal whose quote after the prefix is `'''` or `"""` has its body run to the first unescaped repeat of the same triple quote, followed only by spaces, and is handled as a single-quoted body is — raw kept as written, otherwise decoded; the earlier reading and the oracle's answer stay first and unchanged, so no destination named before is lost).
- A field-free f-string is decoded without folding its doubled braces, so the reader names `/m/a{{b}}` where Python's value is `/m/a{b}`: `Path('/m', f'a{{b}}')` writes `/m/a{b}` (source `shell-write-destinations.ts:535-549`; the fold is Python's source-level rule, applied before escapes are decoded, so `f'\x7b\x7b'` keeps its two braces); port: fixed (the decoded value of an f-prefixed literal with no replacement field has `{{` and `}}` folded to `{` and `}`; `shellWriteEscapeField` is unchanged and still decides the Path dynamic part, so an f-string with a field stays dynamic).
- A triple-quoted body that holds a physical CR or CRLF is named as written, while Python's source decoding turns both into LF before the literal is evaluated, so `open("""/m/a<CRLF>b""","w")` writes `/m/a\nb` and `open(r"""/tmp/a<CR>/n.md""","w")` writes `/tmp/a\n/n.md`, while the reading names `/m/a\r\nb` and `/tmp/a\r/n.md` (source `shell-write-destinations.ts:535-549`; the oracle names nothing here, and the base of this change named nothing either, so the miss is neither introduced nor widened by it; found by the Devin Review and the Codex Code Review on this change's PR); port: fixed (security: a triple-quoted body is read with the physical line breaks Python's source decoding gives it, so a CRLF and a lone CR both read as LF before the escape decoding, the raw return and the field-free f-string brace fold; `shellWriteTripleScanNewlines` in `internal/pabcd/hook/shellwrite_verbs.go` makes the one-pass copy, and a single-quoted body, which cannot hold a physical line break, is unchanged; the oracle names nothing for these inputs and no recorded case holds one, so none is tagged).
- The outer scanners that produce a call's argument spans (`shellVerbOpenWrites`, `shellVerbWithoutComments`) track one quote kind, so a triple-quoted body holding an odd number of the same quote desyncs them before the literal is read: `open("""/review/memories/"x""","w")` writes `/review/memories/"x` and the reading names nothing (source `shell-write-destinations.ts:535-549`; the oracle names nothing here either; found by the Codex Code Review on this change's PR); port: fixed (security: `shellWriteTripleScanRegion` reads a string region as Python does (a triple-quoted literal runs to the first unescaped run of three of the same quote, a single-quoted region to the first unescaped quote, and a character after a backslash closes neither) and both scanners use it, so a triple-quoted literal is one region; `TestShellWriteTripleScanRegions` and `TestShellWriteTripleScanUnits` pin it; the oracle names nothing for these inputs and no recorded case holds one, so none is tagged).

## Found by the hook trust identity port (CRW-347)

- `identityHash` resolves the event through a plain object lookup (`EVENT_LABELS[eventName]`), which also finds the members JavaScript inherits from `Object.prototype`, so a hook document whose event key is `constructor`, `toString`, `valueOf` or another inherited member is accepted although it is not one of the ten event labels: the eleven function members hash with no `event_name` in the identity and `__proto__` hashes with an empty `event_name` object, while `listHookEntries`'s own `in` guard lets such a key through too (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/cxc-ops/src/hook-trust.ts:109` and `:181`; recorded as `proto_constructor`, `proto_toString`, `proto_valueOf`, `proto_proto` and the two matcher variants in `internal/runtime/doctor/testdata/hooktrust/identity-oracle.json`); port: kept.
- An object value whose own `toString` member is not callable makes the type check's template literal throw the raw TypeError `Cannot convert object to primitive value` instead of reporting an unsupported handler type, so a hook document with `"type": {"toString": null}`, or such an object inside an array, fails with an engine message that names no handler field (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/cxc-ops/src/hook-trust.ts:111-112`; recorded as `type_toString_null`, `type_toString_number` and `type_array_toString_object` in `internal/runtime/doctor/testdata/hooktrust/identity-oracle.json`); port: kept.

## Found by the CRW-574 created-check fix for archived children

- The native created check refused a thread the host had archived as "host thread identity is unavailable", so a parent that lost its created report could not record the real agent id of a finished child and the later stopped close was refused too (source `internal/role/created_check.go` at the managed spawn port, lines 204-211 before this fix; code of this repository with no oracle counterpart, because the oracle's dispatch accepts any agentId; no corpus fixture holds an archived row); port: fixed by CRW-574 (the query and the identity test no longer read the archive flag, the thread_spawn parent and subagent checks are unchanged; `TestCreatedArchivedNativeCreated` and `TestCreatedArchivedRecoveryThenClose` pin it, and the archived case of `TestCreatedCheckNativeDatabaseRefusalsAndOrdering` that asserted the refusal now asserts the acceptance).
- The native created check never fills the identity's Status, so the stopped close's "recorded child is active" refusal can only fire on the App Server path; the production caller (`internal/role/dispatch_cmd.go:29`) passes no host, so on that path the close is never refused for the child being active, archived or not; its other refusals (agent id, executionState, reconciliation, attempt) still apply (source `internal/role/created_check.go:174` and `:261`; `TestCreatedArchivedAppServerParity` covers the App Server path only); port: kept (a follow-up would read the runtime status from the host, which this issue's edit region does not allow).
- A created report could name any real child of the session, so with archived children accepted a later dispatch could report the id of a finished child as the agent it had just spawned and skip the spawn the check exists to prove; the archive refusal had blocked that for archived children only, and nothing blocked it for unarchived ones (source `internal/role/created_check.go` before generation 2, where `createdCheckRead` takes the agent id alone; probed on the base with two dispatches of one session that both accepted the same unarchived child); port: fixed by CRW-574 generation 2 (before a created report is published and before the host is asked, `createdArchivedReplay` reads the session's dispatch records and refuses an agent id that another attempt holds, of another dispatch or an earlier attempt of the same one; a record that cannot be read as a regular dispatch file fails the check; `TestCreatedArchivedReplayAcrossDispatches`, `TestCreatedArchivedReplayAcrossAttempts`, `TestCreatedArchivedReplayMakesNoHostCall` and `TestCreatedArchivedSiblingRecords` pin it, and the completion dispatch of `TestDispatchCommandHostReportsAcrossProcesses`, which reported a child the first dispatch had recorded, was given a child of its own).
- The created check proves a real child of this session that no other attempt of the session holds, not that the child was spawned for this attempt: a child spawned outside any dispatch and never recorded is accepted as the created agent of the first dispatch that reports it (source `internal/role/created_check.go` `createdArchivedReplay` and `createdCheckNative`, which read only the ledger and the spawn marker's parent); port: kept (binding a report to its own spawn needs the child's creation time or an attempt marker in the host row or the ledger, outside this issue's edit region; follow-up direction: compare the host row's creation time with the moment the attempt was claimed, which the ledger would have to record).
- The replay guard holds only the per-dispatch lock (`internal/role/dispatch_ledger.go:440`), so two dispatches of one session that report the same new id at the same instant can both pass; and an id recorded by a wrong report that was closed as stopped, left by an earlier crash, or reused by the host stays held by its record, so no other dispatch of the session can report it, and `failed` with `not_created` is a way out only when no child was really created, otherwise the operator repairs the ledger; and a record swapped for a named pipe between the directory listing and its read blocks that read while the dispatch lock is held, because the shared reader checks no file type on its own descriptor (source `internal/role/created_check.go` `createdArchivedReplay` and `internal/role/dispatch_ledger.go:262`; it needs write access to the directory); port: kept (a session-wide lock would add a stale-lock artifact to the directory the next dispatch change pins, and a descriptor-based read belongs to that change).

## CRW-582 — the receipt test's first interrupt (port-introduced defect, fixed)

- The oracle runs a receipt test's command through `spawnSync`, which blocks until the child ends and defers the signal: the recorded Node run died by the deferred SIGINT (rc -2) only after the child had completed, so it wrote no receipt (source `pabcd-state/src/receipt-cli.ts:120-137` at v0.2.40). The same signal against this port used to keep waiting for the child and publish a success receipt — a port-introduced defect, not an upstream one (the invocation context was dropped before `exec.CommandContext`); port: fixed by CRW-582, with the timing difference tagged intentionally-changed (the port stops the child at once instead of after it completes) and the same observable outcome: exit 1, "terminated by signal ... no receipt written", no receipt written. No corpus fixture covers this case, so no recorded case is claimed for it.
- A SIGINT that lands after the check command has already exited (for example during the second Git source capture, just before publication) used to still publish the receipt, where the oracle's deferred signal ends the process before it writes; port: fixed (generation 2 of CRW-582): `RunReceiptCLI` (`internal/pabcd/cli/receipt.go`) now checks the caller's context at the last moment publication can be skipped - after the command returns and after the second capture, before the receipt directory is created - and takes the refusal path with `receipt test: the command did not run to completion (interrupted); no receipt written`, exit code 1, writing nothing; `internal/pabcd/cli/receipt_late_cancel_test.go` proves it through the unexported `receiptLateCancelHook` seam. A cancellation that lands after that check still publishes, because the check precedes the directory work.
- An interrupted check kills only the command process (`exec.CommandContext`'s default cancellation), so a shell-launched descendant can outlive the refusal; port: kept (parity: the oracle never kills the check at all on SIGINT, and `ReceiptRunOptions` documents that cancellation kills only the process the call started); a process-group kill is a follow-up proposal with its own test.

## Found by the CRW-609 lossless rewrite guard

Source: `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/state.ts` (`reconstructUnverified` :57-88, the cut at :82) at v0.2.40, through the same reader as the CRW-556 section. No recorded oracle case holds a stored lone surrogate, invalid UTF-8 or an exponent form of `attempts`, so none is tagged; the cases are the new Go tests named per line.

- The guard of the CRW-556 section compared the stored `unverifiedSubagents` list with the one the reader rebuilt, but both decode with `encoding/json`, which reads an unpaired UTF-16 surrogate escape (`"\ud800"`, or the `\ud83d` that Node's 256-unit cut of `receiptClaimed` leaves after 255 characters) and each invalid UTF-8 byte as U+FFFD, so the two sides agreed and the rewrite wrote U+FFFD over the stored text; the oracle keeps the lone surrogate and `JSON.stringify` writes it back as the same escape (source `state.ts:57-88`; tests `TestRewriteLosslessRefusesWhatTheDecoderWouldAlter` and `TestMemoryGateRefusesToRewriteTextTheDecoderWouldAlter`); port: fixed (data loss: `state.RewriteKeepsUnverified` refuses before decoding when the bytes are not valid UTF-8 or hold an unpaired surrogate escape, so the memory gate denies and keeps the authorization, the commands exit 1, `RecordTombstone` writes the marker and the other callers write nothing; a valid pair, and an escaped backslash before the letters of an escape, are kept).
- The refusal covers the whole file, not only the records the guard compares, so a lone surrogate in a key the reader ignores, in the slug, or in a repeated key that a later one shadows also stops the rewrite of an otherwise intact list; the rewrite would have replaced it too, so this is over-refusal and never loss; port: kept.
- The guard compared an `attempts` literal by value only when its exponent text had at most three characters, so `3e0000`, a form of the number 3 that the reader keeps and the writer prints, was read as a loss and the memory authorization, `scan record` and `evidence resolve` of an intact record were refused; the oracle has no guard (tests `TestRewriteLosslessComparesExponentFormsOfOneValue` and `TestMemoryGateSpendsOverTextTheDecoderKeeps`); port: fixed (a Go-port defect: the exponent digits are counted after the sign and the leading zeros, so `3e0000`, `3E+0000` and `3e` followed by sixty-two zeros reach the exact comparison, while `1e1000` and a literal over 64 bytes stay losses).
- A writer of the session file that does not ask the guard still replaces a lone surrogate with U+FFFD, and the unknown keys of a record are dropped on every rewrite (source `state.ts:57-88`); port: kept (every caller of `state.WriteState` outside the state package asks the guard today; a later port that adds a writer, such as the orchestrate transitions, must ask `RewriteKeepsUnverified` too).

## Found by the goalplan write lock loss refusal (CRW-604)

- A sub-record reviver drops data inside a record it keeps: a review round whose `planFiles` list holds one malformed entry revives with no `planFiles` at all (one bad entry drops the whole list), the round count still matches, and the next write persists the loss, so the file list of an approved round is deleted for good (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/goalplan.ts:363-394` and `:400-411`; test `TestRevivalLossRefusesWhatARewriteWouldDrop`, case "plan files with one malformed entry"); port: fixed for writes (CRW-604, a record data-loss defect fixed by decision under the parity rule revision of 2026-10-03: `WithGoalplanWriteLock` encodes the plan the file revives to the way the writer encodes it and refuses to call its writer, with Kind `unreadable` and the first uncovered place as the reason, when the file holds a non-null value that encoding does not cover; reads are unchanged).
- Revival also changes stored values on the way through, which the next write persists: an unknown work-phase or task status reads as the first one, a `dirty` that is not a boolean reads as false, a criterion `surface` outside the four known ones and a `presented` other than `native` are dropped, a padded task `outcome` is trimmed and a blank one dropped, a `tasks` or `criteriaIds` that is not a list reads as `[]`, a fractional `schemaVersion` is floored (source `goalplan.ts:583-675`; the cases of `oracle-plan.json` and `oracle-revive.json`, 61 of the 211 readable plan recordings among them); port: fixed for writes (CRW-604, the same check; the display readers still return the revived plan; this supersedes the remark in the CRW-544 section above that the other things revival drops on a write stay port: kept).
- A goalplan file whose bytes are not valid UTF-8 is read through the decoder's replacement, so the invalid bytes become U+FFFD and the next write stores that character in their place; Node's `readFileSync(path, "utf8")` does the same (source `goalplan.ts:712`); port: fixed for writes (CRW-604: the lock refuses with the byte offset, before the comparison, so a replacement character can never compare equal to itself; an unpaired surrogate escape is still refused by the read).
- A key that one object of a goalplan file states twice keeps only its last value in the decoder, so the earlier value is lost on the next write, a whole `reviewRounds` list included; Node's `JSON.parse` does the same (source `goalplan.ts:721`); port: fixed for writes (CRW-604, a review finding of the PR: the lock scans the decoded text for a repeated key and refuses with its path before the comparison; reads are unchanged).
- The check compares the file with the re-encoding of the plan revival made of it, so it does not see what happens to a plan after the writer callback changes it, a null in the file may differ from the re-encoding, the top-level `updatedAt` is not compared because every write refreshes it; a caller that builds a plan by hand with an empty or unknown status, host source or verdict can store a plan its next locked write refuses; port: kept.
- `BuildGoalplan` with a pointer to an empty `surface` writes `"surface":""` (the oracle's `buildGoalplan` defaults a missing surface only, `goalplan.ts:996-1012`), which revival drops, so the plan the writer made is refused on its next locked write; the writer test plan `criteria-host` is that case and is excluded by name from the round-trip test `TestRevivalLossLetsWhatTheWriterWroteThrough`; no caller of `BuildGoalplan` outside tests exists; port: kept.
- A direct `WriteGoalplan` outside `WithGoalplanWriteLock` is not guarded by this check; the two callers of the Go port (`review_round_run.go`, open and abort) run inside the lock callback; port: kept.
- The review-round runner appends `; retry` to every non-ok result of the lock (source `review-round-cli.ts:271`, `:287`), so the refusals of this change, which no retry can clear until the file is repaired, read as transient; port: kept.
## Found by the dispatch ledger directory pin (CRW-559)

Source: `plugins/codexclaw/components/subagent-config/src/fallback-dispatch.ts` at v0.2.40 (the directory walk :74-78, the temporary write, rename and removal :112-114, the lock :137-139 and :167, the managed issuance :242-266), and the Go ledger before this fix (`dispatchDirectory`, `dispatchSave` and `dispatchRun` in `internal/role/dispatch_ledger.go`, `createdCheckStop` and `createdArchivedReplay` in `internal/role/created_check.go`, `IssueManagedSpawn` in `internal/role/managed_spawn.go`).

- The ledger checks `.crw`, `dispatches` and the session directory with lstat and then keeps only the path string, so the lock mkdir, the state read, the exclusive temporary file, the rename and the recursive lock removal follow that string again: a directory swapped for a link between the check and the use sends a claim, a stopped close or a managed issuance to the linked directory (outside the workspace included) and succeeds, the original record stays unclaimed and the original lock stays; and a session directory moved aside and replaced by a link after the record was read lets the save overwrite a record of the same name there and the cleanup delete a lock directory of the same name there, with its contents (source `fallback-dispatch.ts:74-78`, `:112-114`, `:137-139`, `:167` and `:257-266`; the Go port followed the same strings; the recorded top-directory link case only covers a link that is already in place); port: fixed (security: a path escape that writes and deletes outside the workspace, fixed by decision under the parity rule revision of 2026-10-03. The workspace is opened with `os.Root` and `.crw`, `dispatches` and the session directory are opened one step at a time and pinned after an identity check against the lstat result, a link, another directory or a non-directory answering `dispatch directory must not be a symlink`; the lock, the read, the temporary file, the rename and both cleanups go through the pinned directory, and a held lock still answers `mkdir <path>: file exists`. The swapped cases cannot be recorded from the oracle, which has no seam between a check and its use, so no recorded case is retagged and the cases are Go tests: `TestDispatchPinnedRefusesSwapAfterCheck`, `TestDispatchPinnedStoppedCloseRefusesSwap`, `TestDispatchPinnedSaveStaysInTheCheckedDirectory`, `TestDispatchPinnedWithoutSwapBehavesAsBefore`).
- A record, or the directory itself, swapped for a named pipe between the check and the open blocks the open for good while the dispatch lock is held (the shared reader looked at no file type on its own descriptor; recorded as port: kept in the CRW-574 section, last line, which this line replaces for the pinned reads), and a record replaced by a relative link to itself passed an lstat taken before the open; port: fixed (the pinned reader opens with O_NONBLOCK and requires the opened descriptor to be the regular file that was looked at and the name to still lead to it, and a directory is opened through `name/.`, so a pipe or file fails at once; `TestDispatchPinnedSiblingTurnedIntoPipe`, `TestDispatchPinnedRecordSwappedForLinkToItself` and the pipe rows of `TestDispatchPinnedRefusesSwapAfterCheck`).
- `crwdir.EnsureDir` still creates `.crw` and its `.gitignore` by path, so a `.crw` swapped for a link between its mkdir and the exclusive `.gitignore` write puts that one file into the linked directory; only when `.crw` did not exist, and creating a file of fixed content (source `codexclaw-dir.ts`, `internal/pabcd/crwdir/crwdir.go:25-43`); port: kept (the package is shared by every state writer and is outside this issue's edit region).
- `ManagedSpawn`, the read-only selection that the managed-spawn hook runs before it issues, still reads the record by path (`dispatchRead`: an lstat of the file and a read through `.crw/dispatches/<session>`, whose components are not checked), so a link or a named pipe put there can redirect or block that read, which writes nothing (source `fallback-dispatch.ts:242`, `internal/role/managed_spawn.go:37`); port: kept (the issuance that follows takes the pinned directory and rereads the record under the lock; a follow-up would read the selection through the pinned directory too).
## Found by the hook trust entry listing port (CRW-601)

- `listHookEntries` skips a group whose matcher `new RegExp(matcher)` refuses, which is V8's grammar; Go has only RE2, so the port accepts a matcher that `regexp.Compile` accepts and one it refuses only for lookaround or a backreference (the lookaround opener is rewritten to a plain group and the backreference to one character, then the pattern is compiled again), and the two grammars still differ. A matcher JavaScript accepts that the port skips: `[]`, `[^]`, `\u0041`, `\u{41}`, `\xZ`, `\cJ`, `\e`, `\q`, `\Z`, `\k<`, a repeat count above 1000 (`a{1001}`), a group name with a non-ASCII letter (`(?<é>x)`), a lone surrogate,  and a class range that ends in an escape the rewrite turns into a smaller character (`[0-\9]`, `[\x02-\7]`, `[a-\k<z>]`). A matcher JavaScript refuses that the port keeps: inline flags (`(?i)a`, `(?s)a`), `(?P<n>a)`, duplicate group names, a quantified anchor or lookbehind (`^*`, `$*`, `\b+`, `(?<=a)*`), an unknown `\k<x>` beside a named group, and a reversed octal range (`[\3-\1]`); measured against Node 24 over 96 patterns (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/cxc-ops/src/hook-trust.ts:191-197`; recorded as the `matcher_*` cases and the fourteen `matcher_residual_*` cases of `internal/runtime/doctor/testdata/hooktrust/entries-oracle.json`, where the oracle's answer is kept and the replay expects the port's); port: kept.
- `listHookEntries` writes the event into the key through a template literal of `EVENT_LABELS[eventName]`, so an event key that `Object.prototype` defines, which the oracle's own `in` guard lets through (see the hook trust identity entry above), is spelled as the source of the inherited function, `function toString() { [native code] }` (`function Object() { [native code] }` for `constructor`), or `[object Object]` for `__proto__`, in place of an event label (source `hook-trust.ts:181` and `:206`; recorded as the `proto_*` cases); port: kept.
- A hook document whose `hooks` member is a non-empty string or array is read through `Object.entries` as an object with index keys and fails with `unsupported hook event: 0`, a number or boolean `hooks` member is silently an empty list, and a document or manifest that is JSON `null` fails with V8's raw TypeError `Cannot read properties of null (reading 'hooks')` that names no file; `Object.entries` also lists integer-like event keys before the others, so a file with an index key and another defect reports the index key first (source `hook-trust.ts:165-166` and `:177-179`; recorded as the `doc_hooks_*`, `doc_null`, `manifest_top_level_null` and `int_key*` cases); port: kept.
- `listHookEntries` strips a leading `./` and `containedPluginFile` strips another before it resolves the path, so `././x.json` is keyed `./x.json` but read from `x.json`, and `././/x.json` resolves as the absolute path `/x.json` and is refused as escaping the plugin root with the message naming `.//x.json` (source `hook-trust.ts:140-156` and `:175-176`; recorded as `ref_double_dot_slash`, `ref_triple_dot_slash_then_slash` and `ref_triple_dot_slash_then_absolute`); port: kept.
- The plugin manifest was read without the containment check the hook files get (a `.codex-plugin/plugin.json`, or a `.codex-plugin` directory, that is a symlink was followed wherever it points), and a hook file was opened after its path was checked, so a symlink swapped in between was followed too. Devin reported both as security findings on this port, so the port applies the same containment to the manifest and opens each file through an `os.Root` of the plugin root and reads from the opened handle, which refuses a link that leaves the root at the moment of the open (source `hook-trust.ts:165` and `:175-176`; the oracle's answer for a manifest that leaves the root is recorded as the `intentionally_changed_*` cases of `internal/runtime/doctor/testdata/hooktrust/entries-oracle.json`, and the swap is covered by `TestListHookTrustEntries_swapAfterTheCheckIsNotFollowed` and `TestHookTrustEntriesReadContained_rootOpen`; the first version of this fix opened the file by its resolved path, resolved the path a second time and compared the two observations with `os.SameFile`, which a link swapped to a file outside the root at the open and again at the stat defeated, CRW-631); port: fixed.
- A FIFO in place of the manifest or of a hook file that nothing holds open for writing blocks the open, in the oracle and in the port (source `hook-trust.ts:165` and `:175-176`; not recorded, a case would hang); port: kept.
- A plugin root that is the file system root rejects every reference as escaping it, because the prefix test appends the separator to a root that already ends with one and so expects `//` where every path starts with `/` (source `hook-trust.ts:149-150` and `:153-154`; not recorded, the recorder cannot create files at `/`); port: kept.
- The Go JSON reader refuses a document that nests containers more than 10,000 deep, with an error, where V8's `JSON.parse` reads a million levels (source `hook-trust.ts:165` and `:177`; probed with Node 24 and not recorded, since the fixture would be megabytes); port: kept.
## Found by the CRW-612 spawn recursion grant port

Source: `plugins/codexclaw/components/subagent-config/src/spawn-attach-hook.ts` at v0.2.40 (`SUBSPAWN_GRANT_RE` :280, `isSubagentSpawner` through `consumeRecursionGrant` :351-408), ported to `internal/role/spawn/grant.go`. No recorded fixture holds a grant file (the three spawn-hook fixtures that mint or consume one need the whole hook), so the cases are the oracle calls recorded in `internal/role/spawn/testdata/grant/oracle.json`, replayed by `grant_test.go`. The first line is oracle behaviour the port fixes, the next three are oracle behaviour the port keeps, and the last four are differences the port introduces.

- The recursion grant lives under a predictable path, `<tmpdir>/codexclaw-subspawn-<uid>/<sha256(cwd NUL session)>`, that `mintRecursionGrant` creates with `mkdirSync` recursive and mode 0700 and that `consumeRecursionGrant` renames and reads without looking at what is there: an existing directory of any mode or owner, or a link to another directory, is used as it is, so another user of the same host who pre-creates the name can plant a grant the hook then honours or read the grants it holds (source `spawn-attach-hook.ts:364-368`, `:375-388`, `:392-406`; the `intentionally-changed` rows of `unsafe` in the recording: directories of mode 0755 and 0770 at either level and a link at either level); port: fixed (a security finding under the parity rule revision of 2026-10-03). `grant.go` opens `<tmpdir>` as an `os.Root` and uses the per-uid and the key directory only when each is a real directory, not a link, owned by the current uid with mode 0700, checked before it is opened (opening a FIFO another user planted would block) and again on the opened handle; otherwise minting yields no grant and consumption answers false, and a grant file is read only when it is a regular file. A directory another user pre-creates under the per-uid name stays refused, so it can deny this uid the feature but is never trusted.
- The marker pattern `/\[CXC-SUBSPAWN-GRANT:([a-f0-9]{64})\]/gi` ignores the case of its letters and of the digits, but the captured nonce is hashed as written, so `[cxc-subspawn-grant:<nonce>]` consumes a grant while an upper-case spelling of the same nonce never names a minted file (source `spawn-attach-hook.ts:280`, `:392-396`; recorded sequences "prefix case folds, nonce case and non-ASCII look-alikes do not"); port: kept (the prefix is matched without case for ASCII letters only, as the oracle's non-Unicode flag does).
- `consumeRecursionGrant` accepts any JSON number as `expiresAt`, a fractional one included, and an exponent that overflows reads as infinity, so a grant file holding `1e999` never expires (source `spawn-attach-hook.ts:400-401`; recorded planted bodies); port: kept (the file is written only by the minting hook, which writes an integer).
- A consumed grant is renamed to `<digest>.json.claimed-<pid>-<random>` and removed in a `finally` block, so a process that dies between the rename and the removal leaves the claimed file in the key directory, and nothing sweeps it, as nothing sweeps the unused grants of the line above (source `spawn-attach-hook.ts:396-405`); port: kept.
- The oracle calls `grantScope` outside the `try` of `consumeRecursionGrant` (source `spawn-attach-hook.ts:396`), so a working directory that cannot be read (`process.cwd()` throws) makes the consume throw into the hook, where `ConsumeRecursionGrant` answers false and `MintRecursionGrant` yields no grant; port: pending (the hook issue that calls it decides what a failing call prints).
- An ill-formed raw UTF-8 sequence in the hook input reaches the digest as one U+FFFD per byte (a raw `ED A0 80` as one), where Node's decoder gives one U+FFFD per maximal ill-formed subsequence (three for `ED A0 80`); an escaped lone surrogate is U+FFFD in both and is recorded (source `spawn-attach-hook.ts:365-366` hashing a decoded string); port: pending (decided where the hook reads its stdin).
- The oracle creates a missing temp root itself, because `mkdirSync` with `recursive: true` makes every missing parent of `<tmpdir>/codexclaw-subspawn-<uid>/<key>`, so a call whose `os.tmpdir()` names a directory that does not exist still mints (source `spawn-attach-hook.ts:380`; reproduced with Node); `MintRecursionGrant` opens the temp root it is given as an `os.Root` and answers no grant when it does not exist, and `ConsumeRecursionGrant` answers false; port: pending (`os.TempDir` and Node's `os.tmpdir()` name an existing directory in every normal run, and the hook issue that passes the root decides whether it must be created first).
- `JSON.parse` of a claimed grant has no nesting limit of its own, so a grant file whose `expiresAt` is in the future and that also holds an ignored array nested 10,001 levels deep is consumed; Go's `encoding/json` refuses nesting beyond 10,000 levels, so `ConsumeRecursionGrant` answers false for that file (reproduced with Node and with the Go decoder; the minting hook writes only `{"expiresAt":<ms>}`); port: pending.

## Found by the CRW-367 spawn classifier port

Source: `plugins/codexclaw/components/subagent-config/src/spawn-attach-hook.ts` at v0.2.40, through the
recorder and replay in `internal/role/spawn/testdata/classify/`.

- `stripControlMarkers` collapses only runs of three or more LF, so a CRLF-separated message keeps
  its blank-line runs (source `subagent-config/src/spawn-attach-hook.ts:409-414`; the CRLF case is
  recorded in `internal/role/spawn/testdata/classify/oracle.json`); port: kept.
- The spawn tool-name set accepts `collaboration.spawn_agent` and `collaboration_spawn_agent`,
  spellings codex-rs never emits, as defensive aliases (source
  `subagent-config/src/spawn-attach-hook.ts:579-584`; recorded in
  `internal/role/spawn/testdata/classify/oracle.json`); port: kept.
- The review-keyword fallback is a substring search, so a task that merely contains a keyword
  fragment ("preview the diff") infers reviewer (source
  `subagent-config/src/spawn-attach-hook.ts:511-516`; recorded in
  `internal/role/spawn/testdata/classify/oracle.json`); port: kept.

## Found by the CRW-623 session lock for created reports

- The replay guard of the created check held only the per-dispatch lock, so two dispatches of one session that reported the same new id at the same instant could both pass, and the id of an attempt that the stopped close had closed stayed held by its record, so the dispatch that really spawned that child could never report it and only an operator editing the ledger could free it (source `internal/role/created_check.go` `createdArchivedReplay` and `createdCheckStop`, and `internal/role/dispatch_ledger.go` `dispatchPinnedRun`, at the base of CRW-623; code of this repository with no oracle counterpart, because the oracle's dispatch has no created check, and no corpus fixture drives either case); this supersedes the locking remark of the CRW-574 section and the first two parts of its replay-guard line (its named-pipe part was already superseded by the CRW-559 section); port: fixed by CRW-623 (a created report and the stopped close also take a `.session.lock` directory in the pinned session directory, before the lock of their own record and until after their write, and a held lock is the same immediate busy answer, never stolen; `createdArchivedReplay` skips the last attempt of a stopped dispatch that failed with a reconciliation, which only the stopped close writes, and the record keeps that id as evidence; the refusal says whether a close can free the id; `TestCreatedSessionLockAdmitsOneReportOfAnAgent`, `TestCreatedSessionLockStoppedCloseFreesTheAgent`, `TestCreatedSessionLockEarlierAttemptKeepsItsAgent`, `TestCreatedSessionLockOtherEndsKeepTheirAgent` and `TestCreatedSessionLockBusyAndReleased` pin it); two limits come with it and stay: a process that dies while it holds the session lock leaves `.session.lock` behind and nothing removes it, so every created report and stopped close of that session is refused until an operator removes the directory by hand, a wider reach than a stale record lock, which stops one dispatch; and the stopped close frees the id on the caller's reconciliation, which the native check cannot confirm against the runtime (the CRW-574 line on the missing runtime status stays kept).

## Found by the metric ingest interrupt port (CRW-620)

- The oracle has no SIGINT handler, so its process dies on the first interrupt and a `metric ingest` that is still waiting for its input records nothing; the port's row ignored the invocation's context, kept the stdin read running and wrote the METRIC records once stdin closed (source `pabcd-state/src/cli.ts:156-181` at v0.2.40; tests `TestPabcdMetricIngestEndsOnTheFirstInterrupt`, `TestPabcdMetricIngestStopsOnContextEndWhileWaiting` and `TestPabcdMetricIngestChecksTheContextWhenTheReadHasEnded`); port: fixed (port-introduced, not an upstream defect: the ingest read runs in a goroutine under the invocation's context, as the hook leg's interrupted read does, and the row answers `Interrupted` (130) with nothing written and nothing recorded when the interrupt lands first or the read finishes as it arrives; the other metric rows and every uninterrupted run are unchanged). No corpus fixture drives an interrupt, so no recorded case is claimed for it.
- The last check sits before `cli.RunMetricCLI`, where the issue's criteria put it, so an interrupt that lands while the ingest records is not observed: the row waits out `appendRow`'s exclusive lock (source `internal/pabcd/metric/metrics.go:237`), appends the rows, answers 0 and prints `metric ingest: recorded N METRIC line(s)` where the oracle's process would have died at the signal (the oracle reads and does one lock-free `appendFileSync`, `pabcd-state/src/metrics.ts:119-137`, so its own write window is unordered; the port's ledger lock is what makes the window long); port: kept (raised as a red finding and a P1 in this pull request's review; follow-up: give `RunMetricCLI` and `RecordMetricsFromText`/`appendRow` the invocation's context, with a cancellable lock wait and a rule for the rows already appended when the interrupt lands between them, in the issue that owns that library).

## CRW-346 — the doctor text renderer stderr slice

- The 160-unit slice of a features-probe stderr can end inside a surrogate pair: the oracle text
  report writes U+FFFD there while its `--json` report keeps the lone surrogate escape, and the
  port holds U+FFFD in both, the same platform difference the lone-surrogate lines of the
  manifest-targets section record (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/cxc-ops/src/doctor.ts:137`
  with the JSON writer `plugins/codexclaw/components/cxc-ops/src/cli.ts:84-86`; the recorded case
  `stderr_slice_cuts_a_surrogate_pair` of the CRW-346 recorder, checked on Node 24); port: kept.

## Found by the deletion guard quoting and comment reading (CRW-611)

- The deletion guard's oracle grammar reads a command with quotes only: a backslash outside single quotes, a `#` that opens a comment and the quote removal that drops an escaping backslash are not known, so seven spellings that bash runs as `rm -rf ../repo` from the checkout were allowed: a quote inside a comment, an escaped quote and an escaped quote inside double quotes (`echo a # it's`, `echo \'`, `echo \"` and `echo "a\"b"`, each followed by a newline and the removal), and a target spelled with backslashes (`rm -rf ..\/repo`, `rm -rf \.\./repo`, `rm -rf ../re\po`); the $'...' forms of the same target (the dollar before the quote, the numeric and `\c` escapes, a NUL that ends the string) were allowed too (source `pabcd-state/src/worktree-guard.ts:191-248`, `splitSegments` and `tokenize` at v0.2.40, the first walk of `internal/pabcd/hook/worktreedel.go`); port: fixed in the extended walk only (security class). The first walk is unchanged and still allows them; the extended walk now judges every reading a second time over bash's own reading of quotes, comments, backslashes and `$'...'` escapes (`worktreeDelQuoteSegments`, `worktreeDelQuoteTokenize`), after the grammar it had, so nothing it denied is allowed and each earlier deny keeps its reason. No recorded fixture runs these commands, so no oracle case is tagged intentionally-changed.
- The CRW-585 scan reading keeps a backslash-newline pair inside single quotes, as the outer shell does, and its second reading (the plain removal) exists only for a command with a here-document operator or a backtick, so a program string handed to a shell (`sh -c 'r\<newline>m -rf ../../<slot>'`, `bash -c`, `eval`) kept the r and the m apart although the inner shell removes the pair and runs `rm`: the commands were denied by the fallback before CRW-585 and allowed after it (the CRW-585 regression found by the operator's pair evaluation of PR #557; source: `worktreeDelReadings` in `internal/pabcd/hook/worktreedel.go` at CRW-585; the oracle's tokenizer has no continuation handling, `pabcd-state/src/worktree-guard.ts:221-248`); port: fixed in the extended walk only (security class): the quote-aware pass judges the program string that a shell word hands to `-c` (or to the long `--command` and `--session-command` of `su` and `fish`, also abbreviated), or the arguments of `eval`, as a command of its own, so the inner shell's reading is applied (every later word of the shell that holds a blank or a separator is judged as a program, so the shell's own option parsing, which differs between shells and has forms such as `-co posix`, `+c`, `-n +o noexec`, `zsh -oshwordsplit -c` and `&>f`, cannot hide it; reading a word that was only an argument can deny too much, never too little) and a relative target in it (`bash -c 'rm -rf .'`) is denied too. A program string handed to a shell through a wrapper that is not in the list (`sudo`, `env`, `nohup`, `xargs`, `command`, `exec`, `time`, `timeout`, `nice` and a few more), or to something other than a shell word, `su` or `eval` (`ssh`, `find -exec` without a shell word, `watch`) is still read once, by the outer shell's rules.
- The same differential run against bash found two more spellings of the same hiding, fixed in the extended walk only: a redirection operator glued to a target (`rm -rf ../repo>/dev/null`, `rm -rf 2>&1 ..\/repo`, where the `&` ended the command), and a standalone brace after a removal verb, which is an argument in bash (`rm -rf { ..\/repo`) but cut the verb from its targets; port: fixed in the extended walk only. The oracle's first walk still reads `rm -rf ../repo>/dev/null` as one word and allows it.
- Both grammars read a here-document body, a backtick body and a command substitution inside double quotes as the same flat shell text, so a quote in a here-document body (`cat <<EOF`, a line with an apostrophe, `EOF`, then `rm -rf ../repo`), a backslash-quote inside a backtick body, or `echo "$(rm -rf ../repo)"` hides an `rm` that bash runs (source `pabcd-state/src/worktree-guard.ts:191-248`; the flat reading is documented at `worktreeDelJoinContinuations`); port: kept (follow-up).
- A word that bash accepts before the verb hides it: the negation `!`, `time`, `coproc`, the bodies of `if`, `while` and `for` after `then` or `do`, and a leading redirection (`>&2 rm -rf ../repo`, `2>&1 rm -rf ../repo`); `if true; then rm -rf ../repo; fi` and `! rm -rf ../repo` are allowed (source `pabcd-state/src/worktree-guard.ts:262-278`, the prefix list of `sudo`, `command`, `builtin` and `env`; the wrapper part is also in the CRW-498 section above); port: kept (follow-up).
- The destructive-verb hint reads the raw segment, so a verb that quoting or an escape hides (`r\m -rf "$X"`) does not arm the slot-mention fallback, and a target made of a variable or a substitution is still not resolved (`../repo$x`, `../repo${x#y}`); both are known limits of the fallback (source `pabcd-state/src/worktree-guard.ts:450-452 and :467`); port: kept.
- A `#` right after a `)` is read as the start of a comment, as after any parenthesis; bash keeps it in the word when the `)` closes a command substitution (`echo $(printf x)# '`), so the guard can deny a command bash runs harmlessly, never the other way; taking the first `)` after a `$(` for its closer would hide a real comment after a case pattern (`echo $(case x in x)# it's`, newline, `rm -rf ../repo ;; esac)` runs rm); port: kept.
- A cd that `eval` runs moves this shell, and the guard does not follow it (`eval 'cd ..'; rm -rf repo` removes the checkout from its parent and is allowed), as it does not follow `source`, `pushd` or a function that changes the directory; only a plain `cd` in the walked text moves the directory of the later commands, as in the oracle (source `pabcd-state/src/worktree-guard.ts:191-248`); port: kept.

## Found by the CRW-613 spawn hook assembly port

Source: `plugins/codexclaw/components/subagent-config/src/spawn-attach-hook.ts` at v0.2.40 (`runSpawnAttachHook` :849-987 and `runtimeSkillsDir` :54-73), ported to `internal/role/spawn/hook.go` (`spawnHookAssemble`). The corpus fixtures that run this code need the whole hook, so the cases are the oracle runs recorded in `internal/role/spawn/testdata/hook/oracle.json` and replayed by `hook_test.go`. The first four lines settle the four `port: pending` lines of the CRW-612 section; the rest are new. Only the first is `port: fixed`.

- A subagent spawn whose message holds exactly one grant marker, with a relative, empty or missing `cwd` while the hook process's own working directory is unreadable, throws out of `consumeRecursionGrant` (`grantScope` sits outside its `try`, :396) into the hook's outer catch (:1111), which prints nothing, so the spawn is allowed untouched and the recursion deny is bypassed with a marker nobody minted; zero or two markers, or an absolute `cwd`, are denied, and a root spawn with a missing `cwd` prints nothing at :888 (recorded cases "unreadable working directory"). `spawnHookAssemble` denies the subagent spawn in every one of those cases and keeps the empty output for the root spawn, which recurses nothing; port: fixed (security: a failed grant check must not allow recursion; the recorded subagent cases are intentionally-changed with that reason).
- An ill-formed UTF-8 sequence in the hook input reaches the grant digest as one U+FFFD per maximal ill-formed subsequence, as Node's decoder gives it: the hook reads its input through `harness.ReadStdin`, whose decoder has the same rule (`TestReadStdinDecodesAsNodeDoes` in `internal/harness` pins `ED A0 80` as three), so no difference remains; port: kept.
- The oracle creates a missing temp root, because `mkdirSync` with `recursive` and mode 0700 creates every missing directory (probed with Node 24, recorded as "missing temp root"); `spawnHookAssemble` calls `os.MkdirAll(root, 0o700)` before minting, and only for a grant scope that resolves, since the oracle evaluates the scope first and creates nothing otherwise (recorded: a relative `cwd` with a recursion request leaves no directory). Consuming from a missing root answers false in both; port: kept.
- A grant file whose `expiresAt` is in the future and that nests an ignored array more than 10,000 levels deep is consumed by `JSON.parse` and refused by Go's decoder; the hook writes only `{"expiresAt":<ms>}`, so the case cannot arise through it; port: kept.
- `runtimeSkillsDir` tries `CXC_SKILLS_DIR` and then the plugin's skills directory next to its module (:54-73), a path a Go binary does not have; the port tries `CRW_SKILLS_DIR` and then `<PLUGIN_ROOT>/skills`, the directory Codex names to the plugin's hook commands. With neither resolving there is no skills directory, so mentions are neither normalized nor inlined and a v2 message gets no affordance. The oracle's fallback cannot be recorded, so the Go paths are tested directly (`TestSpawnHookSkillsDir`); port: kept (a decision of the port, not an oracle defect).
- A hook applied twice to a recursion request stacks guards and loses the capability: the first pass strips the token and puts the coordinator guard and the grant marker in front; the second pass has no token, so its plain guard does not match the coordinator guard in front of it, and `stripControlMarkers` removes the marker from the first pass's own instruction line, which then ends "capability in that spawn message: " under a leaf guard that says "Do NOT spawn" (source :969-985 and :409-414; recorded case "reapplied grant request"); port: kept.
- A `message` member that is JSON null counts as present, so a v1 request with `"message": null` beside valid `items` reads no items: it is allowed untouched, or denied when a subagent sends it, where the same items without the member are guarded (source :874; recorded in "fail-open no-ops"); port: kept.

## Found by the config.toml hook trust reader port (CRW-602)

Source: `plugins/codexclaw/components/cxc-ops/src/hook-trust.ts` (`TomlSection` :70-75, `TomlLine` :77-82, `TrustedHashLine` :84-88, `updateMultilineState` :217-256, `isEscaped` :260-264, `tomlLines` :266-281, `sections` :283-293, `readInstalledPluginKeys` :299-315, `trustedHashLines` :317-323, `exactHookSections` :325-328, `diagnoseHookTrust` :330-343) at v0.2.40, through the port in `internal/runtime/doctor/hooktrust_config.go`.

- A `config.toml` that exists but cannot be looked at reads as absent: Node's `existsSync` is false for a dangling symlink or a path an ancestor directory refuses to search, so `readInstalledPluginKeys` answers no key and `diagnoseHookTrust` leaves every hook untrusted, where a reader might report the unreadable file (source `hook-trust.ts:300` and `:331`); port: kept (it fails closed, toward untrusted).
- An empty `trusted_hash` value counts as a record: `trusted_hash = ""` reads as the value `""`, which is not null, so that hook is `drifted` with an empty actual where one might expect `untrusted` (source `hook-trust.ts:317-323` and `:330-343`; recorded as the `empty_trusted_hash_value` case of `internal/runtime/doctor/testdata/hooktrust/config-oracle.json`); port: kept.
- The line reader splits on LF alone, so a file whose newlines are bare CR is read as one line: no section header matches and every hook reads untrusted (source `hook-trust.ts:266-281`; recorded as `cr_only_line_endings`); the header pattern is also lenient about brackets inside the header (`\[[^\r\n]+\]`), so a line such as `[a] [b]` reads as one section header, which exact section equality then refuses to match (source `hook-trust.ts:283-293`); port: kept.
- The offsets the reader returns (`TomlLine.start`/`end`, `TrustedHashLine.start`) are UTF-16 code-unit offsets in the oracle and byte offsets in the port; each is used only to slice its own language's string, so the reader answers the same where the two encodings differ, but the F6 retrust port must do its arithmetic in bytes (source `hook-trust.ts:266-281` and `:317-323`); port: kept.

## Found by the dispatch lock owner record (CRW-628)

- A dispatch lock is a bare directory that does not say who holds it, and nothing recovers one: the oracle's record lock has no stale-lock path (source `fallback-dispatch.ts:137-139` at v0.2.40; the recorded case `cli__subagents__dispatch_held_lock_fails_closed` drives a held lock), and the `.session.lock` that CRW-623 added to this port has the same gap with a wider reach, since a process that dies holding it refuses every created report and stopped close of the session, which is the first of the two limits that section left standing; port: fixed (every lock now holds an `owner.json` with the pid, process start, host and creation time, and `crw role helper dispatch-lock-clear` removes a session or record lock only when it is a real directory whose owner is confirmed gone on this host, and appends the removal to `lock-clears.jsonl`; `TestDispatchLockRecordsItsOwner`, `TestDispatchLockWithAbandonsOnlyItsOwnLock` and the `TestDispatchLockClear` tests pin it; no recorded oracle case drives a stale lock and an ownerless lock is refused exactly as before, so no case is retagged); limits that stay: a lock without an `owner.json` (a process that died between creating the lock and writing the record) is refused and only an operator can remove it; the process start is compared for equality only (Linux ticks since boot from `/proc`, Darwin `p_starttime`; the Darwin read is compiled and vetted but has not run on a Darwin host), so a pid in another pid namespace under the same host name can be judged wrongly; and a lock that another process puts in the place of the judged one after the judgment is found out after the rename that claims the lock, and is put back (or left at a `.clearing-` name the error reports), never removed, but while it is moved away its name is free: a process that takes the name then holds a lock beside the replaced lock's live holder, whose own release later removes the lock now at the name (the replaced lock stays at the `.clearing-` name), and when nobody takes the name the put-back brings the lock back even if its holder released it meanwhile, which leaves a stale lock for a later clear; rename(2) also replaces an empty directory that appeared at the name, so a taker between its mkdir and its owner write fails closed; a crash between the claim and the removal leaves a `.clearing-` directory that an operator removes.

## Found by the metric ingest record-window port (CRW-627)

- The oracle has no lock and no signal handler, so its process dies at the first interrupt and keeps the lines `appendFileSync` had already written (source `pabcd-state/src/metrics.ts:119-137` and `pabcd-state/src/metric-cli.ts:92-151` at v0.2.40). The port's `metric ingest` ended on an interrupt only while it read stdin (the CRW-620 section above); once it recorded, it ignored the signal: `appendRow` waited for the exclusive ledger lock without a deadline (in the red run of `TestPabcdMetricIngestEndsOnTheFirstInterruptWhileTheLedgerIsLocked` the built crw held at that lock was still waiting 5 s after the SIGINT) and wrote every remaining METRIC line once the lock came free; port: fixed (port-introduced, not an upstream defect; this closes the `port: kept` line of the CRW-620 section): `metric.RecordMetricsFromTextContext` checks its context before each METRIC line and asks for the lock with `LOCK_EX|LOCK_NB`, again every 20 ms while another writer holds it, so the wait ends with the context (the file is closed without a write and the context's own error is returned); `cli.RunMetricCLIContext` returns that error as it is (`errors.Is(err, context.Canceled)`), and `metricVerb` answers `Interrupted` (130) with nothing printed when an ingest ends by cancellation or its context has ended after the run. The rows recorded before the signal stay, as the lines the oracle's process had appended do; a SIGINT that lands after the last check lets the run finish and answer normally, as the oracle's process does when it finishes first. `RecordMetricsFromText`, `RecordObjectiveMetric` and a context that can never end keep the blocking wait, so every uninterrupted run and every other caller is unchanged, and a contended writer with a live context polls every 20 ms where it used to wait in the kernel. Tests: the five `TestRecordMetrics...` cases of `internal/pabcd/metric/metrics_context_test.go`, `TestPabcdMetricIngestEndsOnTheFirstInterruptWhileTheLedgerIsLocked`, `TestRunMetricCLIContextEndsWithTheContextWhileTheLedgerIsLocked` and `TestPabcdMetricIngestAnswersInterruptedWhenTheContextEndsAfterRecording`. No corpus fixture drives an interrupt, so no recorded case is claimed for it.
- The other writers of these files still wait for their locks without a deadline and ignore the first interrupt: `metric record` (`RecordObjectiveMetric` under `context.Background()`), `metric kind` (`WriteObjectiveKind`'s lock on the kind directory) and `divergence` (the mode file's directory lock in `WriteDivergenceMode` and the candidate archive's lock in `appendCandidateRow`), where the oracle's process dies at the signal in each of them (source `pabcd-state/src/metric-cli.ts:103-116` and `:136-144`, `pabcd-state/src/metrics.ts:138-153`, `pabcd-state/src/divergence.ts:112-140` and `:169-200`); port: kept (outside the ingest this change covers; follow-up: give each the invocation's context the way the ingest has it, with its own test).

## CRW-622 — the receipt test's publication window (port-introduced defect, fixed)

- The oracle has no signal handler and no withdrawal: in the recorded interrupt its deferred SIGINT killed the process before the single in-place `writeFileSync`, and a kill during that write can only leave the truncated record already recorded at `:613`. The port refuses a cancellation it observes before publication (CRW-582), but a cancellation that landed after that check still published the receipt and returned its path with exit code 0, so a cancelled invocation could leave a success receipt where the oracle leaves none. `crwdir.PublishContext` now reports the context's error at the rename step, the last point at which the publish can still be skipped, so the existing cleanup removes the temp file and no final file appears; `RunReceiptCLI` publishes the receipt through it, maps that cancellation to the existing refusal, and after a successful publish checks the context once more and withdraws the receipt the rename beat it to, answering the same refusal; a withdrawal that fails returns its `unlink` `PathError` instead of the refusal, so a surviving receipt is never reported as "no receipt written" (tests `TestPublishContextCancelAtTheRenameStepLeavesNothing`, `TestPublishContextAlreadyCancelledLeavesNothing`, `TestPublishContextUncancelledMatchesPublish`, `TestReceiptPublishCancellationWithdrawsTheReceipt`, `TestReceiptPublishCancellationReturnsInterruptedRefusal`, `TestReceiptPublishCancellationWithdrawalFailure`); port: fixed.
## CRW-631 — hook trust reader opens files inside an os.Root
