- The plugin root is the anchor of the containment: `hookTrustEntriesReadContained` resolves its path once with `filepath.EvalSymlinks` and opens the result with `os.OpenRoot`, which follows links in the root's own name, so a link swapped into a parent directory of the plugin root between those two calls makes the `os.Root` hold another directory and the file is read from there; once the root is open, no swap under it leads outside it, and the oracle has the same window between its `realpathSync` calls and its `readFileSync` (source `hook-trust.ts:145-155` and `:175-176`; not recorded, the recorder cannot pause between two calls; a follow-up would open the root by a descriptor walk that follows no link); port: kept.
- The `os.Root` that each file is opened through is narrower than the oracle's `realpathSync` and plain read in six ways that follow from the security fix of the CRW-601 entry above: an absolute link is refused as leaving the root even when its target is inside it, a relative link that leaves the root and comes back (`../plugin/h.json`) is refused too, a chain of more than 8 links is refused (`filepath.EvalSymlinks`, which the first version used, followed up to 255), a link whose target lies outside the root reports the escape where the oracle reports ENOENT when that target does not exist, a file that is not regular (a named pipe that another process holds open, a device) is refused after the open where the oracle reads it, and a plugin root that the process may search but not read (mode 0111, say) is refused with the permission error, because `os.OpenRoot` opens the directory for reading where the oracle and the first version opened the file by its path, which needs search permission only (source `hook-trust.ts:145-155`, `:165` and `:175-176`; the recorded cases use relative links only and pass unchanged; covered by the rows of `TestHookTrustEntriesReadContained_rootOpen`); port: fixed.
- A FIFO that nothing holds open for writing still blocks the open itself, before the type check can refuse it (the CRW-601 entry above; the test holds the pipe open to reach the refusal); port: kept.
## Found by the CRW-614 spawn hook routing port

Source: `plugins/codexclaw/components/subagent-config/src/spawn-attach-hook.ts` at v0.2.40 (`runSpawnAttachHook` :849-852 and :991-1115), ported to `internal/role/spawn/hook_route.go` (`RunSpawnAttachHook`). The corpus fixtures that run this code need the hook leg, so the cases are the oracle runs recorded in the `route`, `deny` and `itemsCap` sections of `internal/role/spawn/testdata/hook/oracle.json` and replayed by `hook_route_test.go`. Only the second line is `port: fixed`.

- `spawnHookAssemble` stops where the oracle applies role routing, and the port's final-gate check (:1066-1073) and managed dispatch (:889-907, :1075-1114) are not ported yet, so a packet that marks itself as the final gate is allowed without its receipts check and a message that opens with a dispatch marker is routed as an ordinary message; the hook is not registered, so nothing reaches a live spawn; port: pending (the later issue that connects the hook leg ports both).
- The port's `DenyEnvelope` (CRW-367) wrote a lone surrogate in the deny reason as three U+FFFD, because it went through encoding/json, where the oracle's `JSON.stringify` writes the escape (`"\ud800"`); the deny and allow envelopes now go through `pyjson.Dumps` with the escape kept, and a high surrogate that a low one follows is written as the one character it is in JavaScript (stripping a control marker can bring two halves together, and a Go string keeps their two WTF-8 sequences apart) (found by the operator's pair evaluation of PR #574; recorded as the `deny` cases and the "lone surrogates" route case); port: fixed.
- A tracked project config that is not trusted prefixes the oracle's message with a `[CXC-CONFIG-IGNORED]` warning and ends the ciphertext notice with it; the project layer is dropped (decision 7), so the port prints neither and answers as the oracle does without the project config (recorded as the "trust warning" case, intentionally changed); port: kept. The prefix rule itself is ported and checked through a synthetic assembly (`TestSpawnHookRouteTrustPrefix`).
- A hook applied twice to a message inserts the role's `promptOverride` again: the items form skips a prompt that already follows the guard (:1021), the single-message form does not, so a reapplied message grows `guard, prompt, prompt, task` and always prints an envelope (source :1021-1029 and :1048; recorded in "prompt override"); port: kept.
- The prompt is inserted with `String.prototype.replace` and a replacement string, so the operator's prompt text is read for the patterns `$$` (one `$`), `$&` (the guard again), `$'` (the task again) and `` $` `` (the text before the guard), while `$1` and `$<x>` stay as written; the port expands the same four patterns (source :1026-1029; recorded in "prompt override dollar patterns"); port: kept.
- A single message that is exactly the guard (an exact reapplication of a bare guard) has no `guard + blank line` for `replace` to find, so the prompt is not inserted, yet the prompt still counts as a change and an envelope with the message unchanged is printed (source :1026-1029 and :1048; recorded in "prompt override"); port: kept.
- The oracle's :1030-1046 branches for an empty guard (an existing guard marker's block, else a prefix) cannot run, because the guard is never empty; they are ported in `spawnHookRoutePrompt` and tested directly with expectations read off the code, not recorded; port: kept.
- The caller's numbers pass through `JSON.parse` and `JSON.stringify`, so an integer past 2^53 loses digits (`12345678901234567890` becomes `12345678901234567000`), `1.0` and `1E2` are written `1` and `100`, `-0` is `0`, an overflow (`1e999`) is `null`, and a key that is a canonical array index below 2^32-1 moves in front of the others in ascending order, in every object of the answer (recorded in "input forms and bounds"); the port respells and reorders the same way; port: kept.
- `JSON.parse` reads any depth, but `JSON.stringify` throws a RangeError near 4,458 levels at Node 24's default stack (9,103 with a 2 MB stack), which the hook's outer catch turns into no output, even for a spawn that would have been allowed; the threshold depends on the stack size. The port reads any depth as well (a document nested past pyjson's 10,000 levels is checked against the JSON grammar by an explicit-stack scan, which a recursive parser could not do for the 2 million levels 4 MiB holds, and cut to its first 4,402 levels), so a subagent that carries a deep junk field is still denied and a malformed deep document prints nothing; it prints nothing for a `tool_input` nested past 4,400 levels, so a depth in 4,400-4,458 differs, and the writer is not recursive past that bound, because a Go stack overflow cannot be recovered (recorded in "deep nesting" and "deep documents"; `TestSpawnHookRouteMaximumNesting` runs the deepest payload 4 MiB holds); port: kept.
- The 256 KiB cap on the skill blocks of an items spawn is measured on the renamed text, whose guard and skill lines are shorter than the oracle's, so the exact edge differs by the length difference; the port finds its own edge and checks the oracle's outcomes at it (`itemsCap`), and far from the edge the answers are identical (hash steps); port: kept (a consequence of the name substitution).
- A role's `promptOverride` that holds a lone surrogate escape is read as U+FFFD by the role store (the existing note in `internal/role/role.go`, I8), so a message the oracle would write with the escape is written with U+FFFD; the surrogate cases of this port use messages, not role strings; port: kept.

## CRW-615 — the doctor install checks

Source: `plugins/codexclaw/components/cxc-ops/src/doctor.ts` at v0.2.40 (commit 3c1459ac), through
the recorder and replay in `internal/runtime/doctor/testdata/harness/install/`.

- `cxc doctor` aborts the whole report with an uncaught `EACCES` when the project's
  `.codexclaw/sessions` directory exists but cannot be enumerated, instead of reporting a WARN
  (source `doctor.ts:171`; the recorded case `unreadable` holds the thrown message, and the CLI's
  own top-level boundary prints "cxc-ops error: EACCES: permission denied, scandir '<path>'" with
  exit 1); the port keeps the abort as a panic at the same seam (`HarnessPabcdCheck`), which the
  CLI boundary of the assembly issue catches as `cli.ts` does; port: kept.

## CRW-594 — a failed divergence candidate save (port-introduced defect, fixed)

- The candidate add branch printed Go's `*os.PathError` spelling where the oracle prints the Node
  runtime's error message: with a regular file at the `--cwd` path, so `<cwd>/.crw` cannot be
  created, the port answered `divergence candidate add: mkdir <cwd>/.crw: not a directory` where the
  oracle answers `divergence candidate add: ENOTDIR: not a directory, mkdir '<cwd>/.crw'`, exit 1,
  nothing written (source `pabcd-state/src/divergence-cli.ts:153`, the `err.message` of the
  `mkdirSync` failure at `divergence.ts:197`; recorded with Node 24 for the PR, and no corpus fixture
  drives a failed candidate save). `internal/pabcd/cli/nodeErrorMessage` now spells a
  `*os.PathError` whose errno `planErrno` names as `<NAME>: <description>, <op> '<path>'`, with the
  path left out for write and close, and `planFailure` and the candidate add branch both call it, so
  plan init's output stays byte-identical (pinned by the recorded cases of `plan_test.go`);
  port: fixed (port-introduced, not an oracle defect: the oracle text is the reference and the port
  now matches it; no recorded case is retagged).
- The table the conversion reads named thirteen errnos and left EFBIG out, so a candidate write past
  RLIMIT_FSIZE still answered `divergence candidate add: write <archive>: file too large` where the
  oracle prints `divergence candidate add: EFBIG: file too large, write` (both recorded with a
  512-byte limit and Node 24 for the oracle; the limit needs a lowered `ulimit -f`, so no corpus
  fixture reaches it). EFBIG with its verified description is added to `planErrno`, and
  `TestDivergenceCliCandidateAddReportsTheFileSizeLimitFailure` pins the end-to-end text; the other
  plan init outputs are unchanged. port: fixed (port-introduced, the same class as the line above).
- The divergence CLI's other error path keeps Go's spelling: the mode write (the oracle's one
  uncaught path, `divergence-cli.ts:115`) is reported by its caller as `crw cli failed: <PathError
  text>` where the oracle prints `codexclaw cli failed: <err.message>` (`src/cli.ts:530`; the
  difference in the reporting path itself is the divergence-CLI section's line above), and this
  conversion is not applied there, because this issue's criteria keep every other divergence output
  unchanged; port: kept (follow-up: apply the same conversion at that boundary with its own test).
## Found by the dispatch lock mutual exclusion (CRW-635)

Source: `internal/role/dispatch_ledger.go` (`lockWith` and its release) and `internal/role/dispatch_lock_clear.go` (`dispatchLockClear`) at the base of CRW-635. Code of this repository with no oracle counterpart: the oracle's record lock has no stale-lock path and no clear (`fallback-dispatch.ts:137-139` at v0.2.40), so no recorded case drives either line and none is retagged.

- The exclusive lock that `dispatch-lock-clear` held on the session directory only kept clears apart: taking a lock (`Mkdir`) and releasing it (`RemoveAll` by name) took no part in it, so a clear that had read one holder's `owner.json` could judge that holder gone after it exited and move the lock of the next holder to its tombstone, a third process could take the free name, and two processes then held one lock and passed the same replay guard; the earlier holder's release removed whatever lock stood at the name. The CRW-628 section above lists the free name and the put-back as limits; port: fixed (taking a lock, from the `Mkdir` to the end of the `owner.json` write, and releasing it each hold a shared `flock` on the pinned session directory, a descriptor of their own on it, and a clear holds it exclusively from before its judgment to the removal of its tombstone, so none of them runs in the middle of a clear and a clear never meets a lock being taken or released; a shared wait polls for two seconds and then answers like a held lock for an acquisition (`mkdir <path>.lock: file exists`, with the clear named) and with an error that removes nothing for a release; the release removes the directory its holder created and nothing else, an `Lstat` that is not the same file, a link or a missing lock being an error with nothing removed; `TestDispatchLockMutexClearExcludesAcquireAndRelease`, `TestDispatchLockMutexBusyAnswersAfterTheBound` and `TestDispatchLockMutexReleaseRemovesOnlyItsOwnLock` pin it, the first with a seam that stops the clear after its judgment and again after its claim). The tombstone and the put-back stay and can no longer be reached by a lock that changed after the judgment.
- A `lock-clears.jsonl` that was another name of a dispatch record in the same session directory (a hard link; the identity checks only compared the file with itself) took the audit JSON after the record's own object, which the reader then refused as trailing data; port: fixed (the clear refuses, removing nothing, when the log it opened has a link count other than one, a count it cannot read included; `TestDispatchLockClearRefusesALogWithOtherLinks` pins it, with the record left unchanged and the same clear succeeding once the extra link is gone).
- What stays: the lock is advisory, so a hand edit of the directory and a runtime that does not carry this change (an older installed one) are not kept out, and a filesystem that refuses `flock` makes every lock operation fail instead of proceeding; `SameFile` compares device and inode and a filesystem may give a removed directory's inode to the next directory made, so a release whose lock was removed by hand (a clear cannot remove a live holder's lock any more) can still remove the lock that the next holder made with that inode, and the test keeps the judged lock open for that reason; the directory object of a running clear takes no shared lock for a lock taken through that same object (`dispatchLockMutexHeld`), which only the CRW-628 seam test does and no production caller; `managed_spawn.go` drops the error of its release, so a release refused there is not reported; and a lock without an `owner.json` is still refused and only an operator removes it.

## CRW-636 — the receipt withdrawal removes a receipt another run published (port-introduced defect, fixed)

- The oracle's process is killed by the interrupt before it writes and it has no withdrawal (source `receipt-cli.ts:75-185` at v0.2.40; see the CRW-622 section above), so two runs of one session cannot interleave a withdrawal with a publication there. The port's withdrawal read the receipt, compared the bytes with its own and unlinked the path, and a second run of the same session that renamed its receipt in between the comparison and the unlink had that receipt removed, whatever its bytes were (found by the operator's pair evaluation of PR #579): a record loss that left the check without the evidence of a run that had succeeded. `RunReceiptCLI` now opens the receipt directory and holds an exclusive `flock` on that handle from just before `crwdir.PublishContext` until the call returns, which covers the cancellation check after the publication and the withdrawal's read, compare and unlink, so the other run's rename waits until the withdrawal is over. No lock file is created, so a tree a fixture compares is unchanged; a wait for the lock that ends because the context ended publishes nothing and answers the existing interrupted refusal; every uninterrupted run and every refusal that existed keeps its output and bytes. Tests: `TestReceiptWithdrawalKeepsAnotherRunsReceipt` (the race, driven through `receiptLockAfterCompareHook`), `TestReceiptDirectoryIsLockedFromPublishThroughWithdrawal` and `TestReceiptLockWaitEndsWithTheContext`. No corpus fixture drives two runs of a session, so no recorded case is retagged; port: fixed. The lock is cooperative: the run-start removal (the oracle's own, kept) and any other writer of the file do not take it, and a file system that does not support `flock` gives no exclusion.
- A run's start removes whatever receipt is at the session's fixed path, the oracle's own `rmSync` (source `receipt-cli.ts:115-117` at v0.2.40: "a stale success must not survive a failing re-run"), and the lock does not cover that removal. A run that starts after another run published, or while it holds the lock, deletes that run's receipt before it waits for the lock, and if it is then cancelled during the wait it publishes nothing, so the first run answers the path of a receipt that is gone (found by the Devin review of the pull request that added the lock). The oracle's processes end the same way when they overlap, and moving the removal under the lock or after the check would let a failed or cancelled run leave an earlier success receipt in place, which is what the removal is for; port: kept.
## Found by the CRW-633 final-gate guard port

Source: `plugins/codexclaw/components/subagent-config/src/final-gate-guard.ts` at v0.2.40 (all 189 lines), ported to `internal/role/spawn/finalgate.go` (`CheckFinalGatePrereqs`). The code is a library; the hook leg that calls it is a later issue, so the six `hook__pre-tool-use-attaching-skills__final_gate_*` fixtures stay pending (`contract/notes/cxc/CRW-633.json`). The cases are the 82 oracle runs recorded by `internal/role/spawn/testdata/finalgate/record.mjs` into `oracle.json` and replayed by `finalgate_test.go`: 32 are identical, 41 refuse with a text that differs from the oracle's only by the name substitution (R23 and R26), and 9 answer differently because of the security fixes below; all 50 are tagged intentionally changed with their reason.

- A `finalGate` that is an array passes the oracle's `typeof === "object" && !== null` test, so a plan with `"finalGate": []` is a gate with no receipt paths and the spawn is refused as "test receipt path is not recorded" instead of being allowed as a plan with no gate (source :125-128; recorded: "a finalGate that is an array passes the typeof test"); port: kept.
- A `criteria` that is not an array reads as empty and so drops the QA requirement, and an element that is not an object, or whose `surface` is not a string, has no surface and is skipped (source :132-133; recorded: three cases); port: kept.
- The oracle joins the slug of the session state under `.codexclaw/goalplans` as it is, reads a receipt path as written, absolute or climbing out of cwd with `..`, and follows links wherever they lead, so an edited session state or goalplan makes it parse any JSON file the process can read and show the first seven characters of its commitSha, and its `JSON.parse` reads a file of any size and depth (source :119-123 and :91-100; the Devin review of the port's pull request found it as three security findings); port: fixed: the slug must be a goalplan slug (`goalplan.ValidateGoalplanSlug`; any other is "no goalplan" and the spawn is allowed), every file is opened through an `os.Root` of cwd so a path, absolute or relative, or a link that leaves cwd is unreadable (a receipt is then "missing"; an absolute link is refused even when its target is below cwd, and a state directory that is a link to another directory is unreadable), and a file over 4 MiB or nested past pyjson's 10,000 levels is unreadable, because a recursive parse of a hostile document can overflow a Go stack, which no recover catches. The nine recorded cases for the three are tagged intentionally changed with that reason (an unreadable file is the answer the oracle gives for a file that is not there, so each expectation is the oracle's own answer for the case without that file).
- The oracle reads the session state and the goalplan after an existence test, stats a receipt twice before reading it, and waits forever on a named pipe at either path (source :60-70 and :91-100); the port opens each file once, without blocking, and judges the descriptor it opened, so none of these windows exists and a pipe is unreadable; port: fixed (a consequence of the containment fix above; `TestCheckFinalGatePrereqsDoesNotWaitOnANamedPipe`).
- The oracle's outer `catch` answers ok for any exception (:186-188); Go has none and every failure the port can meet is an explicit return, so the library has no outer recover and a panic of its own would propagate. The capture callback keeps the oracle's inner `catch` (a panic there is the SOURCE-ROOT refusal; recorded: "a capture that throws"), and `RunSpawnAttachHook`'s recover (`hook_route.go`) ends a panic in silence, so the hook stays fail-open once the route calls the library; port: kept (a difference by construction, no recorded case reaches it).
- A receipt is trusted for what it says: only commitSha, dirty, treeHash and sourceRoot are compared (extra keys and `capturedAt` are ignored) and nothing binds it to the run that produced it, so a hand-written receipt that matches the tree passes; an identity of kind "unavailable" on either side is never stale, so a tree that git cannot read allows every receipt, and a receipt whose `sourceRoot` is present as `null` or as a relative path is unreadable (source :45-52, :72-84 and :169; recorded: "an unavailable receipt identity is never stale", "compare: unavailable wins over a sha difference", and the sourceRoot cases); port: kept.
- The guard recaptures the current tree with the session capture's defaults, while the receipt's producer (`receipt-cli.ts:124-125`) and the enforcing check (`check-gate.ts:67-73`, whose comment says that dropping the generated paths compared an exclusive hash with an inclusive one) leave out the state directory and the paths the check declared generated; in an unbound repository that does not ignore `.crw/` (its `.gitignore` and `rules/` stay visible to git) and for a declared generated path that is dirty, a receipt that is right for the tree reads as stale, and a receipt does not carry what the guard would need to recapture as it was made (source :147-154 and :72-84; `TestCheckFinalGatePrereqsRecapturesWithoutTheReceiptsExclusions`; a Codex review comment found it twice); port: kept.
- The injected `capture` is asked about the source cwd and nothing else: the session capture's default of leaving out the state directory for a bound worktree does not apply to it, and the hook never passes one, so only the session capture reaches production (source :147-154; `TestCheckFinalGatePrereqsComparesTheBoundWorktree` runs both); port: kept.
- The session state file is found by the sanitised session id while source resolution needs the canonical one, so a session id with a character outside `[A-Za-z0-9._-]` that has a state file and a gate is refused as SOURCE-ROOT, and the same id with no state file is allowed (source :119 and `session-source.ts:76`; recorded: two cases); port: kept.

## Found by the drift and ast-grep check port (CRW-617)

- The known-issues hint tells the reader to `npm run build`, a step crw does not have (`plugins/codexclaw/components/cxc-ops/src/doctor.ts:591`); port: kept.
- The `mcpServers` reference of the manifest is joined and read wherever it lands, with no containment to the plugin root: `../x.json` and an absolute-looking reference are stat'ed and parsed outside it (`plugins/codexclaw/components/cxc-ops/src/doctor.ts:565`); port: fixed (a Devin review finding of kind security: the reference is put through the filesystem-name conversion of `hookTrustEntriesFSPath` and refused with a FAIL when it resolves outside the plugin root, `harnessDriftMCPCheck`; the recorded case `intentionally_changed_mcp_reference_escapes_root` keeps the oracle answer and the replay asserts the port's refusal).
- A probe the runner could not start and one it killed at the timeout collapse into one shape in the Go seam (`HarnessRun` carries neither `error.code` nor the signal), so every non-ENOENT spawn error (EACCES, ETIMEDOUT, a signal) reads as the missing interpreter `python3 not found` unless the runner reports a kill as the Go exit code -1 (`harnessDriftKilled`), which reproduces the oracle's install hint; the runner the command issue builds owns the pick (`plugins/codexclaw/components/cxc-ops/src/doctor.ts:620-625` and `:633-643`; port seam `internal/runtime/doctor/harness_report.go:94-97`); port: kept.
- `9009`, the Microsoft Store python alias code, stays in the interpreter test although the Windows branch is not ported, because the oracle tests it unconditionally (`plugins/codexclaw/components/cxc-ops/src/doctor.ts:622`); port: kept.

## Found by the hook observation reader and harness hook checks port (CRW-616)

Source: `plugins/codexclaw/scripts/hook-observation.mjs` (`readHookObservations` :86-129 and its helpers :11-52) and `plugins/codexclaw/components/cxc-ops/src/doctor.ts` (`runHookExecutionCheck` :448-467, `runHookTrustCheck` :468-542) at v0.2.40, through the ports in `internal/harness/observation_read.go` and `internal/runtime/doctor/harness_hooks.go`. The corpus fixtures that print these checks run the whole doctor command (`cli__doctor__hook_execution_observed`, `cli__doctor__json_report`, `cli__doctor__text_report` and the others that print a `hook-trust` line), so the cases are the oracle runs recorded in `internal/runtime/doctor/testdata/harness/hooks/oracle.json` and replayed by `harness_hooks_test.go`.

- `readHookObservations` accepts any `observedAt` that V8's `Date.parse` reads (`"Jan 1 2026"`, `"2026"`, `"2026-01-01 00:00:00 UTC"`), so a record that carries one is fresh in the oracle; both writers emit an ISO 8601 `.000Z` time and the port reads RFC 3339 only, so such a record is ignored (source `hook-observation.mjs:113-115`; a record with an unreadable time is the recorded case `record_observed_at_garbage`); port: kept as a runtime difference (the lenient reading is not ported).
- The oracle requires a record's `entrypoint` to be `components/<component>/(src|dist)/<name>.(ts|js)` inside the plugin's real path, and digests that script; the Go writer names no component script but the manifest (`harness.Entrypoint`), so the reader requires that name, the manifest's real path inside the plugin root, and the manifest's digest (of the raw bytes, where the oracle digests decoded text, so they differ only for a manifest that is not UTF-8). The sort key `entrypoint` is then one value and the records sort by event alone, in byte order, which is `localeCompare`'s order for the `[a-z][a-z0-9-]*` an event is; two records with one event keep the oracle's directory order there and name order here (source `hook-observation.mjs:44-51`, `:116-118`, `:124`); port: kept as a platform difference.
- A plugin whose real path is `/` never verifies: the entrypoint check is `path.startsWith(pluginRoot + sep)`, which for that root is `//`, so every record is refused as an entrypoint outside the payload and the execution check reports no fresh evidence (source `hook-observation.mjs:49`; the port's containment is the same prefix test, and the CRW-631 section above records the same root for the hook listing; a Devin review of this port named it); port: kept.
- `readdirSync` follows a link to the actor's directory, so a link at `<codex home>/.../<sha256 of the session>/<sha256 of the actor>` into a directory of records is read; each record in it still has to match the plugin, the session, the actor, the clock and its own slot (source `hook-observation.mjs:99-101`; the port's `os.ReadDir` follows it too, pinned by `TestReadHookObservationsSaysWhyItFoundNothing`); port: kept.
- The oracle reads a record by `lstatSync` and then `readFileSync` of the same path, so a link swapped in between the two is followed, and a pipe named like a slot is refused only because the `lstat` comes first (source `hook-observation.mjs:20-24`); the port opens the file without following a link or blocking and decides on the opened handle (regular file, at most the bound), which gives the oracle's verdict for every case that does not race and none for the swap (the recorded cases `slot_named_symlink`, `slot_named_fifo`, `slot_named_directory` and `record_over_8192_bytes`); port: fixed (the link-swap class of the CRW-601 and CRW-631 entries; no corpus fixture reaches it, so no case is tagged).
- The trust check reads the manifest name with `readFileSync` of whatever the path leads to; the port reads it through the containment the hook listing has (`hookTrustEntriesReadContained`), so a manifest link that leaves the plugin root is refused, and the differences recorded for that read in the CRW-631 section (an absolute link, a chain of more than 8, a pipe blocking the open, a root of `/`) hold here too (source `doctor.ts:471`; the test `TestHarnessHookTrustCheckRefusesAManifestLinkedOutOfThePlugin`); port: kept as in that section.
- The catch branch of `runHookTrustCheck` reports `error.message`, which is an engine's text (`ENOENT: no such file or directory, open '<path>'`, a `SyntaxError`); the port reports the error of the step in Go's words, with the same severity (FAIL), so the recorded cases `manifest_missing`, `manifest_is_not_json` and `hook_file_missing` hold only the severity; the null manifest's TypeError text is kept (source `doctor.ts:541`); port: kept as a runtime diagnostic difference.
- `options.pluginKey ?? ...` and `options.codexHome ?? ...` keep an explicit empty string where `HarnessOptions` (CRW-346) has a plain string, so the port reads an empty `PluginKey` or `CodexHome` as unset, and a Codex home that cannot be resolved (`HOME` unset and no account entry, which `homedir()` does not report) makes the execution check WARN with "invocation store unreadable" and the trust check FAIL with the error; `now` and `maxAgeMs` are numbers in the oracle and `int64` here, so a non-finite value (`NaN`, `Infinity`), which the oracle refuses as "invalid freshness filter", cannot be passed (source `doctor.ts:469`, `:479`, `hook-observation.mjs:93`); port: kept as a platform difference.

## CRW-595 — a lone surrogate in a mention link's folder name (port-introduced defect, fixed)

- `spawnInlineLowerJS` lowercased its input through `[]rune(s)`, which turns each of the three WTF-8 bytes a lone surrogate is kept in into U+FFFD, so two `skill://` link folders whose names differ only in that code point collapsed into one `MentionedFolders` key, where the oracle's `String.prototype.toLowerCase` leaves a lone surrogate as it is and keeps the two apart (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/subagent-config/src/spawn-attach-hook.ts:832-843` at v0.2.40, the link capture lowercased at `:838`); port: fixed (port-introduced, not an upstream defect: the lowercasing reads one code point at a time with `pyjson.CodePoint` and writes a surrogate's original bytes back unchanged, while the U+0130 and final-sigma rules stay as they are; `TestSpawnInlineMentionedFoldersKeepsLoneSurrogateNames` and `TestSpawnInlineLowerJSPreservesLoneSurrogates` pin it, and no recorded case changes because every recorded input is valid UTF-8).

## Found by the goalplan lifecycle port (CRW-542)

- `complete-task` marks every task that carries the requested id done once any one of them is ready, so on a plan whose duplicate task ids slipped past validation a task whose own dependency is unmet is completed too, in every copy of a duplicated phase (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/goalplan.ts:1359-1378`; recorded case `complete_ok_duplicate_task_ids` in `internal/pabcd/goalplan/testdata/lifecycle/oracle.json`); port: fixed by CRW-671.
- `decide` compares the stored answer untrimmed against the trimmed input, so a stored `" yes"` rejects the same `yes` as already having a different answer (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/goalplan.ts:1297`; recorded case `decide_reject_padded_stored_answer`); port: kept.
- `meet-criterion` judges only the first criterion of a duplicated id, so when that one is already met it answers unchanged and a later open duplicate of the same id can never be met; the plan then never reads complete (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/goalplan.ts:1389-1404`; recorded case `meet_unchanged_duplicate_met_then_open` in `internal/pabcd/goalplan/testdata/lifecycle/oracle.json`); port: fixed by CRW-671.

## CRW-639 — the deletion guard's shell reader: su's attached program, the depth limit, data operands

The managed-worktree deletion guard reads the program a shell word hands to `-c` and judges it again (`worktreeDelQuoteProgram` and `worktreeDelQuoteWalk` in `internal/pabcd/hook/worktreedel.go`). The oracle's own walk reads no shell program at all; this reader is the extension that CRW-585 and CRW-611 added, so no recorded fixture covers these lines (the guard's fixtures cover the oracle's first walk, and their claims in `contract/notes/cxc/CRW-498.json` are unchanged). Found by the operator's pair evaluation of the pull request that made the reader quote-aware.

- The reader let three things through or stopped them wrongly: it read su's attached `-c` program (`su -c'rm -rf ../repo'`, `su -lc'...'`) as one option word whose first word is `-crm`, so the deletion was allowed; at the reading depth of 8 program strings it dropped the program it had not read and allowed it, so `eval` or `sh -c` nested nine deep around `rm -rf ../repo` passed; and it read every operand that holds a blank after a shell word as a program, so `bash -c 'echo OK' 'rm -rf ../repo'`, which runs only echo, was denied (source `internal/pabcd/hook/worktreedel.go` before this change; a security finding, which the parity rule revision of 2026-10-03 fixes during the port); port: fixed by CRW-639 (a program that su takes with `-c`, attached or in the next word and wherever the options stand, is judged as the program it is; a program still unread at the depth limit is denied with the reason that names a shell program nested past the reading depth, which also denies a harmless nest that deep; only the first operand after `-c` of sh, bash, dash, ash, zsh and su is the program, and the operands after it are still read when the program can name them (a dollar sign, `BASH_ARGV` or `argv`: `bash -c 'eval "$0"' 'rm -rf ../repo'` runs rm), when a redirection word stands among the operands (`bash -c 'source /dev/stdin' <<< 'rm -rf ../repo'` runs rm, and a quoted `'>'` looks like one) or when an option-like word stands after the program (su would run a later `-c`); other shells and any option shape the reader cannot place keep the reading of every blank-holding operand; only su and fish, whose getopt gives the rest of a cluster to `-c`, read an attached `-cPROGRAM` word).
- A here-string that is written without a blank before its quoted target, `bash -c 'source /dev/stdin' <<<'rm -rf ../repo'`, reaches the reader as the one word `<<<rm -rf ../repo`, which reads as a command named `<<<rm`, so the deletion it feeds to the shell is allowed; the same command with a blank (`<<< 'rm -rf ../repo'`) is denied. The behavior is the reader's before this change (checked against the base commit); port: fixed by CRW-657 (the bash-reading tokenizer ends the word at the third `<` of a plain `<<<` whose next byte begins the target, so the operator is a word of its own and the target is read as the next word, exactly as when a blank stands between them: `worktreeDelHereStringTarget` and the flush in `worktreeDelQuoteTokenize`, `internal/pabcd/hook/worktreedel.go`; the oracle's first walk, the here-document operator and every other redirection are unchanged, so a here-string whose target names the worktree is now denied and the blank-less form answers exactly as the spaced form, and the reading only ever adds denies).

## CRW-649 — the review-round working-directory boundary and the plan key

Source: `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/review-round-cli.ts` at v0.2.40 (commit 3c1459ac), through
`internal/pabcd/cli/review_round_args.go`.

- The working directory was opened by `filepath.Clean`'s spelling of it, so an absolute `--cwd /r/alias/..` (a link followed by `..`) named `/r` where the OS reaches `/r/ws`, and an entry outside the real working directory was read and hashed: `Recomputed("/r/alias/..", [{Path: "out/000_secret.md"}])` returned the hash of `/r/out/000_secret.md` where it must read `missing`, and the collection accepted the same entry; a relative `--cwd alias/..` had the same gap (source `review-round-cli.ts:308-317`, with the cleaned spelling of the working directory at `:143` and `:147`); port: fixed (a review finding of kind security: `reviewRoundCwdBase` opens the working directory as the OS reaches it — an absolute cwd as written, a relative one joined to the kernel working directory without cleaning, so `alias/..` stays physical — for both `os.OpenRoot` and `filepath.EvalSymlinks`, keeping the `SameFile` check, and an entry is read only when its real path lies in that real base; `attest/plangate.go`'s `resolve` has the same relative-path gap, is not changed here, and is covered by a separate issue).
- The stored key was the entry's spelling relative to the working directory as spelled, which climbs out when the working directory is a link or the entry is spelled by its physical path, and the first fallback resolved the plan unit's own parent with `EvalSymlinks`, so a link above the unit was lost: with cwd `/r/ws`, `/r/alias` -> `/r/ws`, `/r/ws/link` -> `a` and `planUnit ../alias/link/u`, the key was the physical `a/u/000_plan.md`, and repointing `link` at `b` left `Recomputed` and `PlanFilesHash` hashing `a`'s document, so a changed plan read as unchanged (source `review-round-cli.ts:158`); port: fixed (a review finding: the key is the first candidate that is local and, resolved against cwd exactly as `Recomputed` resolves a stored key, reaches the entry's real path — (a) `Rel(base, abs)`, (b) `Rel(real, abs)`, then (c) the shortest-prefix spelling, the shortest leading prefix of the entry's spelled directory path whose real path lies inside the real base, joined with the rest of the entry as spelled after it, so every link below the point where the path enters the working directory stays in the key; the physical spelling is never stored, and an entry no candidate can name is refused with the existing text).
- `Recomputed` followed an entry's links with `filepath.EvalSymlinks` alone, which walks up to 255 links, so a chain of 42 links inside the working directory that ends at the same document was hashed, where the oracle's `readFileSync` answers `missing` because the kernel stops at 40 (source `review-round-cli.ts:309-316`); port: fixed (equivalence restored: `Recomputed` reads an entry only when `os.Stat(abs)` succeeds, the kernel's own link resolution).
- A case-insensitive file system where an alias spells the plan unit with a different case is refused, because the key compares path text as the oracle does: with the workspace opened as `/r/ws` and `/r/alias` pointing at `/r/WS`, the relative spelling is `../WS` and the entry is refused (CRW-649); port: kept (a platform limitation, no code change).
- One existing test is edited, and it is the only one: `TestReviewRoundArgsKeyThatIsNotTextIsRefused` (`internal/pabcd/cli/review_round_args_test.go`) is re-scenarioed so the non-UTF-8 component is the plan unit's own parent directory, reached through a link, rather than the plan unit's link target, because the key rule recovers a storable key from the old layout. The plan-unit spelling the rule builds then holds the invalid bytes and the same 'is not a readable regular file' refusal stays pinned. The plan unit's own base name cannot carry those bytes: an entry is decoded as Node decodes argv, so a base name holding them would read as U+FFFD there and the entry would fall outside the unit instead. The test keeps its name and its assertion of the refusal text, and it answers the same on dev and after this change (CRW-649).

## Found by the metric lock-wait cancellation port (CRW-637)

- The lock wait only read the context where it slept: `metricLockWait` returned `ctx.Err()` from the select's `ctx.Done()` case alone, so when the select took its timer branch — Go picks any ready case, so a timer that wins the race against a done context is ordinary — the loop called `Flock(LOCK_NB)` again, and the holder releasing the lock in that moment handed it to a waiter that had already been cancelled; `appendRow` then wrote the row without looking at the context. A cancelled `metric ingest` whose ledger lock another writer held therefore left a row that did not exist at the interrupt, where the oracle's process dies at the signal and writes none (the oracle has no lock and no signal handler, `pabcd-state/src/metrics.ts:119-137` and `pabcd-state/src/metric-cli.ts:92-151` at v0.2.40; no corpus fixture drives an interrupt); port: fixed (port-introduced, not an upstream defect — the ledger lock is this port's): `metricLockWait` reads `ctx.Err()` after the select and before its next attempt and reports whether it had to wait, and `appendRow` reads `ctx.Err()` once more after a lock taken that way, before the write, closing the file without a write and returning the context's own error as it is; the first attempt and the `context.Background` path are unchanged, so a free lock is still taken when the context has ended since the caller looked (`TestRecordMetricsFromTextContextCancelledAfterTheTimerBranchWritesNoRow` and `TestRecordMetricsFromTextContextCancelledAfterTheLockIsTakenWritesNoRow`, red on the base with a row written, plus CRW-627's five cases unchanged).

## Found by the work-phase cursor port (CRW-375)

Source: `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/goalplan.ts` (`absentSuccessorDetail` :2134-2142, `effectiveActiveWorkPhaseId` :2249-2267) and `hook.ts` (`activeWorkPhaseOpts` :373-385) at v0.2.40, through the ports in `internal/pabcd/goalplan/workphase_cursor.go` and `internal/pabcd/hook/workphaseopts.go`. No fixture in `contract/fixtures/cxc` drives these units alone — the three have no call site in the build yet — so no recorded case changes.

- No behavioural defect was found in these three units, so there is no `port: fixed` or `port: kept` divergence line. The one fidelity decision is the cursor test in `effectiveActiveWorkPhaseId`: the oracle's `if (plan.activeWorkPhaseId)` is a JavaScript truthiness check, so an empty-string cursor is unset there, while a bare Go nil check would read it as set. The port tests both nil and `""`, so a plan whose cursor is `""` falls through to the next runnable phase exactly as the oracle does (recorded case `empty-string cursor is unset and falls through`).

## Found by the SessionStart, PostCompact and PostToolUse hook port (CRW-378)

Source: `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/hook.ts` (`handleSessionStart` :604-612, `handlePostToolUse` :1951-1992 and `handlePostCompact` :2025-2033) at v0.2.40, ported to `internal/pabcd/hook/session_hooks.go` and registered as the harness legs `session-start-bootstrapping-pabcd-state`, `post-tool-use-capturing-interview-answers` and `post-compact-resetting-reinject-cursor`.

- `handlePostCompact` reads the session state, clears `lastInjectedPhase` and writes the whole state back with no lock, so an update a participating writer (a UserPromptSubmit hook, the memory gate, the idle-edit counter) lands between the read and the write is overwritten by the stale copy and lost (source `hook.ts:2025-2033`; no recorded case interleaves two writers, so none is tagged for this alone); port: fixed (a data-loss defect, fixed by decision like the idle-edit counter: the eligibility is judged again inside `state.WithSessionLock` and the file is written from that read; `TestSessionHookPostCompactKeepsAParticipatingWritersUpdate`). The case `hook__post-compact-resetting-reinject-cursor__active_cycle_resets_cursor` is tagged intentionally changed for the change even though one process observes the same bytes.
- The same write-back rebuilds the state from the reader's normalised value, so a file holding more unverified subagents than the reader keeps, a receipt cut at 256 UTF-16 units or a field of the wrong type comes back changed, a legacy D-close marker loses its distinction, and an interview tracker longer than `interview.MaxTrackerArray` (50) loses its oldest contradictions and assumptions (source `hook.ts:2025-2033` through `state.ts`'s and `interview.ts`'s readers; the unverified-list part is the same defect the CRW-556 section records for the evidence writes, and the interview part was found by the Devin review of this port's pull request, `kind: bug`); port: fixed (a data-loss defect: the write is refused when the reader would drop a stored entry, on the judgement the memory gate and the idle-edit counter already use, plus the interview arrays; `TestSessionHookPostCompactRefusesALossyOrLockedState`). The guard judges drops, not the reader's in-place normalisation: `ReconstructInterview` always rebuilds a dimension (an absent `known`/`unknown` array becomes empty and an unrecognised `level` becomes `low`), so a value comparison would refuse every write and silently stop the recovery.

## CRW-662 — the P>A plan-gate working-directory boundary

Source: `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/plan-gate.ts` at v0.2.40 (commit 3c1459ac), through `internal/pabcd/attest/plangate.go`.

- A relative `cwd` was resolved with `path.resolve`, which cleans it before the filesystem follows a link's `..`, so `--cwd alias/..` (an alias to `<wd>/ws/deep`) named the lexical parent `<wd>` where the OS reaches `<wd>/ws`, and the P>A boundary (the containment departure of CRW-425) was taken one level above the directory the kernel resolves; a plan unit beside the workspace passed the gate (source `plan-gate.ts:79` for the boundary's `resolve(cwd)` and `:46` for the unit's own resolution); port: fixed by CRW-662 (a security fix, by decision under the parity rule revision of 2026-10-03: `plangateRelCwdBase` joins a relative cwd to the kernel working directory without cleaning, so the boundary is the directory the OS reaches; an absolute cwd and every entry path keep the oracle's spelling, and every other verdict and message is unchanged; no recorded case drives a relative cwd, so none is tagged intentionally-changed).

## Found by the goalplan final-gate port (CRW-369)

- `finalGateReasons` probes the schema marker with `existsSync(schemaMarkerPath(ctx.cwd, plan.slug))`, but `goalplanDir` throws when the state root, the plans root or the slug directory is a symbolic link or the slug is invalid, so `validateGoalplan` throws instead of returning a reason and the caller's fail-open catch decides; the Go port answers `markerPresent=false` there instead (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/goalplan.ts:1773` against `:320-331`); port: kept.
- The final-gate reason that interpolates the plan's declared schema version prints a JavaScript number, which switches to exponent notation at 1e21 and above; the Go port formats with `strconv.FormatFloat(v, 'f', -1, 64)`, so a plan declaring such a `schemaVersion` (unreachable: `SupportedMaxSchemaVersion` is 3) would print differently (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/goalplan.ts:1784`, `:1803`); port: kept.
- A value thrown inside the final-gate identity checks is stringified as `err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err)`; the Go port recovers the panic and prints `Error()` for an error value and `%v` for anything else, so a thrown object would read differently (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/goalplan.ts:1869`, `:1892`); port: kept.
- `cmp.detail ?? "changed"` is a nullish test, so a comparison that reports a present but empty detail prints empty parentheses; the Go port's `Comparison.Detail` cannot tell an absent detail from an empty one and prints `changed` (source `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/goalplan.ts:1913`); port: kept.


## Found by the doctor harness assembly and command port (CRW-618)

Source: `plugins/codexclaw/components/cxc-ops/src/doctor.ts` (`runDoctor` :261-360, the inline
manifest, skills and agents checks :263-323, the metadata :362-378) and
`plugins/codexclaw/components/cxc-ops/src/cli.ts` (the doctor case :80-93, `parseHookOptions`
:46-66) at v0.2.40, through the port in `internal/runtime/doctor/harness_run.go`. The whole doctor
command is exercised by the corpus fixtures `cli__doctor__*`; the assembly and the inline checks
are pinned by the recorded oracle in `internal/runtime/doctor/testdata/harness/run`.

- The oracle derives its plugin root from its own module path (`pluginRootFrom(metaUrl)`,
  `plugins/codexclaw/components/cxc-ops/src/cli.ts:27-31`), which a Go binary installed through the
  runtime pointer (`$HOME/.local/share/crw-runtime/current/bin/`) has no relation to;
  `crw doctor harness` reads the host-provided `PLUGIN_ROOT` instead (the port's convention,
  `internal/harness/observation.go:131`, `internal/role/spawn/hook.go:249`), and an unset
  `PLUGIN_ROOT` is a usage error rather than a report over a guessed root; port: kept (a decision
  of the port, not an oracle defect).
- The oracle lists a directory's entries in the filesystem's own order (`readdirSync`), so the
  `skills` and `agents` evidence can name the entries in any order; the port reads them with
  `os.ReadDir`, which sorts, so a payload whose directory order differs from sorted order prints a
  different list (`plugins/codexclaw/components/cxc-ops/src/doctor.ts:301,315`); port: kept as a
  platform difference.
- The `manifest` check prints the engine's `SyntaxError` text for an unparseable `plugin.json`
  (`plugins/codexclaw/components/cxc-ops/src/doctor.ts:277`); the port prints `encoding/json`'s
  message with the same FAIL severity (asserted by severity and a non-empty evidence in
  `harness_run_test.go`); port: kept as a runtime diagnostic difference.
- The oracle's `spawnSync` probe has no bound on a descendant that inherited the output pipe, so
  a `codex` or `python3` probe whose grandchild holds the pipe can hold the whole report open past
  its timeout (`plugins/codexclaw/components/cxc-ops/src/doctor.ts:349,620`); the port bounds the
  wait with `WaitDelay` (the same bound `CodexVersion` uses) and answers the killed status, so a
  stuck probe degrades to the check's WARN instead of a hang; port: kept as a robustness difference
  (the oracle's hang cannot be recorded).

## Found by the managed dispatch and final-gate call port (CRW-372)

Source: `plugins/codexclaw/components/subagent-config/src/spawn-attach-hook.ts` (`dispatchSources` :420-449, the managed dispatch loop :892-907, the final-gate call :1072-1079, the fallback notice :1081-1082, the no-op bypass :1086 and the issuance :1094-1102) at v0.2.40, through the ports in `internal/role/spawn/hook_managed.go` and `internal/role/spawn/hook_route.go`.

- `dispatchSources` prefixes the message with the project layer's trust warning and strips it back off before it reads a guard block (source :426-427); the port dropped the project layer (decision 7), so the warning is always empty and the strip is the identity, and the port omits it. The route half keeps the same always-empty `trustPrefix` branch, so the omission is the only difference; port: kept.
- A managed ledger that is missing or unreadable makes the oracle throw a Node error and the port return a Go error, so the deny text `managed dispatch: <error>` carries Node's wording in the oracle and Go's in the port (source :899 and `internal/role/dispatch_ledger.go`, whose header already records this for the ledger port); port: kept as a runtime diagnostic difference. The recorder therefore records no missing-ledger case, and the Go test asserts the port's own text.
- The one-time recursion grant a subagent presents is consumed before the managed dispatch marker is checked (source :882 before the managed loop :895-907), so a child whose managed dispatch is refused has already spent its grant and cannot retry that spawn with the same one; the port keeps the oracle's order, consuming the grant in `spawnHookAssemble` before `spawnHookManaged`; port: kept (a review comment of the port's pull request found it, and the recorded managed cases keep the oracle's answers).

## Found by the steering op port (CRW-376)

Source: `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/steering.ts` at v0.2.40 (commit 3c1459ac), through
`internal/pabcd/goalplan/steering_ops.go`; the ported ranges are `:39-63,72-153,182-239`.

- The minted criterion id is the highest stored `c-N` plus one in float64, so at or above 2^53 the increment is
  lost and the new id repeats or falls below the highest stored one: on a plan holding `c-9007199254740993` the
  next criterion is minted as `c-9007199254740992`, where the comment at `:199-201` promises a dense monotonic
  sequence (source `steering.ts:199-204`; recorded case `mint-2pow53` in the port's differential, and the plan
  holding that id is refused by the definition integrity rather than corrupted, so nothing is lost);
  port: kept.
- An op that is an array passes the op object test (`:90` is a `typeof` test, and an array is an object) and
  fails the kind read instead, so the refusal names the missing kind of a value that is not an object at all
  (source `steering.ts:90-92`; the batch is still rejected whole); port: kept.

## Found by the metric lock-acquired cancellation port (CRW-667)

- The lock-acquired check read the context only when the wait had to wait: `appendRow` read `ctx.Err()` before the write only under `waited`, which `metricLockWait` set when the lock came from a retry, so a lock the first non-blocking `Flock(LOCK_NB)` took at once (a free ledger) was written with no look at the context; a `metric ingest` cancelled right after that Flock therefore left a row that did not exist at the interrupt and answered nil, where the oracle's process dies at the signal and writes none (the oracle has no lock and no signal handler, `pabcd-state/src/metrics.ts:119-137` and `pabcd-state/src/metric-cli.ts:92-151` at v0.2.40; no corpus fixture drives an interrupt); port: fixed (port-introduced, not an upstream defect — the ledger lock is this port's): `appendRow` reads `ctx.Err()` once after the lock is taken, whether or not the wait had to wait, and before it writes, closing the file without a write and returning the context's own error as it is, and `metricLockWait` drops its `waited` return; `context.Background` keeps the blocking `Flock`, so every uninterrupted run is unchanged, and the window between the check and `WriteString` stays (`TestRecordMetricsFromTextContextCancelledAfterTheFirstFlockWritesNoRow` and `TestRecordMetricsFromTextContextCancelledAfterTheFirstFlockWithTwoLinesWritesNoRow`, red on the base with a row written, plus CRW-637's two cases and CRW-627's five unchanged, and `TestRecordMetricsFromTextContextStopsBetweenLines` now expects no row).

## Found by the M2b state-copy classifier port (CRW-655)

- The classifier keeps the 255-byte single-path-component name limit and never reads the destination filesystem's real NAME_MAX, so a destination with a smaller limit (not the ext4 or APFS the design assumes) is refused by the publish step rather than the preflight (docs/port-cxc/state-migration.md:106-108 and :176-187); a limitation of this port, not an oracle defect, port: kept.

## CRW-671 — the goalplan completion and meet operations refuse an ambiguous id

Source: `plugins/codexclaw/components/pabcd-state/src/goalplan.ts` (`completeGoalplanTask` :1351-1382, `meetGoalplanCriterion` :1384-1406) at v0.2.40 (commit 3c1459ac), through `internal/pabcd/goalplan/lifecycle.go`.

- `complete-task` and `meet-criterion` judged the first entry of a duplicated id and then rewrote every entry carrying it, so the other entry's outcome or captured evidence was overwritten (the two lines above under the CRW-542 section); port: fixed by CRW-671 (a data-loss defect, fixed by decision like the parity rule revision of 2026-10-03: the operation refuses, changing nothing, when the work phase id matches more than one phase, the task id more than one task of that phase, or the criterion id more than one criterion, in the shape `DecideGoalplanDecision` already uses; the check runs after the not-in-this-plan refusal and before the already-done/met answer and the readiness check; reads that keep duplicates and every other operation are unchanged).
- Three recorded cases in `internal/pabcd/goalplan/testdata/lifecycle/oracle.json` move with it and are tagged intentionally-changed in the replay, the recording itself unchanged: `complete_ok_duplicate_task_ids` and `meet_unchanged_duplicate_met_then_open` (named by the issue) and `meet_ok_duplicate_criterion_ids`, which the same rule reaches because it holds two open criteria of one id.
